- **The car health upgrade (T-061):** at the start of every run the car's starting and maximum health are the garage's car health upgrade at its current level, `PlayerProgress.current_value("car_health")` (level 0 is run.json's `starting_health`, 100). `GameFlow.start_run()` sets `run.progress = progress` before adding the run; `run.gd` hands it to `state.progress`, and `RunState.start_run()` uses it for `max_health` and `health`, so the HUD's health bar maximum follows. A run on its own (no `progress`, null) uses run.json's value. Test: `tests/unit/run/test_run_health_upgrade.gd`.
- `run.start_run()` starts a fresh run: it replaces the map (meteor boulders gone; every challenge open again when challenges are on) and the car (back at the start, standing still), emits `world_rebuilt`, then resets the state. The scene calls it once in `_ready()`. Anything holding the old `run.map` or `run.car` picks up the new ones on `world_rebuilt`.

**The run's map (T-206)** (Level design, Decisions, "The elevation sample map becomes the run's map"; Content, "Elevation sample map", "The run on this map"):
- **Weapons at the car's height (T-225):** `neon_underglow.gd` and `windshield_wiper.gd` have `car_height()` (the car's `height`, 0 without one) and add `Vector2(0, -height * 16)` to their drawing's position, as the exhaust flames do (T-206); the drawings sit in the car's `View`, so the depth order is the car's. Tests: `tests/unit/run/test_run_elevation_map.gd` (`test_the_underglow_*`, `test_the_wiper_*`). Screenshots: `tests/fixtures/run_elevation_underglow_{street,rooftop,ramp,air}.tscn` (`run_elevation_demo.gd` has `underglow_tier` and `wiper_tier`); results in `art-archive/T-225/screenshots/`.

- **The switch:** `const USE_ELEVATION_MAP := true` in `scripts/run/run.gd`. True (the default): the run's map is built from `data/level/elevation_sample_map.json` (`ELEVATION_LAYOUT_PATH`) with its rooftops and ramps. Set it to `false` for the MVP map (`data/level/mvp_map.json`), exactly as before T-206 (no rooftops, no round shadow, the car at height 0). Tests and fixtures can set the instance's `run.use_elevation_map` (it starts at the constant) and call `run.start_run()`.
- **Another map file (T-230; Level design, Decisions, "Level creator"):** the run plays the map file in `run.layout_path` (a `res://` or `user://` path to a layout in the elevation sample map's format; it starts at `ELEVATION_LAYOUT_PATH`, and an empty one falls back to it, `run.current_layout_path()`). Either set it before the run enters the tree (`var run = preload("res://scenes/run/run.tscn").instantiate(); run.layout_path = "user://levels/my_level.json"; add_child(run)`), or start a new run on it with `run.start_run("user://levels/my_level.json")` (which sets `layout_path`, so later `start_run()` calls keep that map). `_build_world()` uses the one path for both the map (`map.layout_path`: buildings, boulders, ground, map rectangle) and the elevation layer (`elevation.setup(map, path)`: rooftops and ramps), and the enemies' navigation (`enemy_navigation.gd`, `for_map`) is built from that map, so all of them come from the same file. With nothing set, the main menu's run plays the elevation sample map exactly as before. Only read while `use_elevation_map` is true. Tests: `tests/unit/run/test_run_layout_path.gd` (a copy of the sample map under the per-run test folder with one building and the rooftop `roof-se-6` and its ramp removed: the map and the elevation layer read it, and enemy navigation finds a path from the street up `ramp-nw-0-3` to `roof-nw-3`).
- **The elevation layer** (`scripts/level/elevation_layer.gd`, taken out of the elevation sample map scene so both share it, not copied): `_build_world()` adds it as the map's child `Elevation` and calls `setup(map, layout_path)`; `run.elevation` is it (null on the MVP map). It builds one drawing per rooftop and ramp, sorts them with the buildings (`restack`), and hooks into the map (`map.elevation`), so the map's own depth queries take the areas into account at street level: `top_z_index()` is over every area too, `car_z_index()` (used by the drift smoke and others) and `_boulder_z()` go under a rooftop covering them, and `is_open_spot()` (gift packages, meteor landings) only accepts street-level spots. `enemy_depth.gd` puts enemies, coins and pickups under any area covering them, at height 0 (since T-207 enemies and coins at the ground height they stand on: "Enemies on the rooftops and ramps (T-207)"). The map's own building fade (`_process`) is off while the layer is there; the run calls the layer's `update_see_through()` with the car's height instead. The depth order and see-through rules are the sample map's: "Depth order and see-through (T-197)" below.
- **The car:** `car.ground` is the layer's `ground_height`, so the car has its real height (`car_height.gd`). `sync_view()` on this map (`_sync_view_elevation`) draws the car lifted by its height (`car_sprite.place`) with its shadowless frames in the air (`set_in_air`, T-205), its round shadow `CarShadow` (`car_shadow.gd`, a child of the run, hidden on the MVP map) on the ground under it, both in the depth order (`elevation.z_for`, on `View` and the shadow), the see-through, and the camera following the car as drawn, height included, eased (T-228). The drift smoke and boost effect are lifted with the ground under the car (a proposal).
- **Start:** unchanged, (0, 0) in the gravel square, at street level on this layout (a proposal).
- **The board's tweaks after playing it (2026-10-21):** no drift smoke and no tyre marks while the car is airborne, even with the drift keys held; both come back on landing (Drifting, Decisions, "No drift smoke or tyre marks in the air"; `drift_smoke.gd`, `bind()` emits only when the static `smokes(car)` is true, and `drift_marks.gd`, `bind()` lays only when `lays(car)` is: drifting and not `car.airborne`, so the marks end on takeoff and new ones start on landing). The exhaust flamethrower's flames are drawn at the car's exhaust at its height (Weapons, Decisions, "The flamethrower at height"; `exhaust_flamethrower.gd`, `_update_drawing()` lifts the drawing by `car_height()` units, as the car sprite is lifted); its damage area stays the flat rectangle behind the car's map position, so it burns what is at the car's spot (since T-207 an enemy there stands on the same ground as the car, except right at an edge). Screenshot fixtures: `run_elevation_air_drift_keys.tscn` (in the air with the drift keys held, no smoke or tyre marks) and `run_elevation_flamethrower_rooftop.tscn` (drifting on the height-6 rooftop with the flamethrower); `run_elevation_demo.gd` has `steer_from`, `steer` and `flamethrower_tier` for them.

Tests: `tests/unit/run/test_run_elevation_map.gd` (the switch both ways, one drawing per area, a new run with a new layer, the car's height, shadow, air frames and depth order on a rooftop and behind one, the see-through, the camera, open spots, an enemy's depth, and a run going on with enemies). Tests that check the MVP map's own blocks set `run.use_elevation_map = false` first (`test_see_through_map.gd`, `test_drift_smoke.gd`, `test_mvp_map_no_challenges.gd`, `test_run_scene.gd`'s challenges test).

**The 2-minute scripted run:** `python game/tools/run_scene_headless.py res://tests/fixtures/run_elevation_soak.tscn 150 --show` starts a run from the main menu on the elevation map and plays it for 120 s (`run_elevation_soak.gd`): the car driven by script and moved every 12 s to the street, the rooftops at 3 and 6 and a ramp's foot, two level-ups through the gift packs at 25 s, the clock moved to 1:01 at 70 s for the kaiju; one line every 10 s and a summary. `run_mvp_soak.tscn` does the same on the MVP map. At exit Godot prints `ERROR: 1 resources still in use at exit` on both maps (the engine sound's stream, `audio/sfx/engine/748027_sedan-engine-loop.ogg`, still held when the game quits): it comes from quitting, not from the run, and is older than T-206.

**Screenshot fixtures** (`tests/fixtures/run_elevation_demo.gd`: a run from the main menu with the car put at `start_units`, W held for `drive_steps` physics steps, then stopped, health kept full): `run_elevation_{street,rooftop,air,behind,kaiju}.tscn`, for example `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/run_elevation_air.tscn art-archive/T-206/screenshots/run_elevation_air.png 500` (the air shot drives 106 steps, so it needs many frames). Screenshots: `art-archive/T-206/screenshots/`.

**The state** (`scripts/run/run_state.gd`, class `RunState`). Reach it with `run.state`, or from anywhere with `get_tree().get_first_node_in_group("run_state")`.

| Function | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `start_run()` | Resets the state for a fresh run: health = max health = the starting health in `data/run.json`, 8:00 on the clock, 0 coins. Use `run.start_run()` to reset the map and car too |
| `damage_car(amount: float)` | Takes `amount` off the car's health (stops at 0). At 0 the run ends as a loss. Ignored when the run is over or `amount` is not positive |
| `add_coins(amount: int)` | Adds coins collected this run. Ignored when the run is over or `amount` is not positive |
| `kaiju_killed()` | The Enemy Behavior Agent calls it when the kaiju dies: the run ends at once as a win. Ignored when the run is over |
| `advance(seconds: float)` | Moves the clock forward by hand (tests). The clock otherwise runs on its own in `_process` |
| `is_running()` | True from `start_run()` until the run ends |
| `time_left_text()` | The time left as M:SS ("8:00" ... "0:01", "0:00"), rounded up to whole seconds |
| `shown_seconds()`, `time_elapsed()` | The time left in whole seconds as the clock shows it; seconds since the run started |

| Value | Is |
| --- | --- |
| `time_left` | Seconds left, 480 at the start, 0 at the end |
| `health`, `max_health` | The car's health now, and its full health |
| `coins` | Coins collected this run |
| `result` | "" while the run is going, then "win" or "loss" |

| Signal | Fires |
| --- | --- |
| `run_started()` | When a fresh run starts (after every value is reset) |
| `boss_time()` | Once per run, when 7 minutes have passed (1:00 left). The kaiju spawns on it |
| `run_ended(result: String, coins: int)` | Once per run: "win" (kaiju killed) or "loss" (health at 0, or the clock reaching 0:00), with this run's coins, kept either way. Saving them is a Game Data task that listens here |
| `time_left_changed(seconds_left: float)` | When the clock shows a different whole second (and on start) |
| `health_changed(health: float, max_health: float)` | When the car takes damage (and on start) |
| `coins_changed(coins: int)` | When coins are added (and on start) |

**Likes (XP) and levels** (task T-037; the rules are Game loop architecture, Decisions, the numbers are `data/run.json` through `RunData`). The run scene connects the map's `challenge_completed` and `meteor_challenge_completed` to `state.challenge_completed` (neither fires while challenges are off in the MVP, so likes come from drifting only), and after every car physics step calls `state.drift_step(car.model.is_drifting, car.model.dt)`. Drifting is the driving model's `is_drifting` (the Unity definition: W, or S without W, held with A or D). All XP gained is multiplied by the combo (below).

| Function | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `challenge_completed(challenge_id: String)` | A challenge (preset or meteor) was completed: sends `score` with the current level's `challenge_xp`, then adds it as XP |
| `drift_step(drifting: bool, delta: float)` | One physics step of the car's drift state. An unbroken drift gives `drift_xp_per_second` when it reaches 1 s (the first second included), then again at each further full second: 0.9 s gives nothing, 3 s gives 3 seconds' worth, 3.5 s gives 3. A step without drifting ends the drift |
| `add_xp(amount: float)` | Adds XP. Each time XP reaches the level's threshold, the level goes up by one and `level_up` fires; leftover XP carries over (`carry_over_xp`), so one gain can give several level-ups. At the top level (`max_level`) XP stays at 0 and the bar stops |
| `xp_needed()` | The current level's threshold (0 at the top level) |
| `is_max_level()` | True at the top level |
| `open_choice(level_reached: int)` | (T-144) Starts the weapon choice for that level: pauses the run and sends `level_up_choice_started`, so the level-up screen opens; while a choice is on show it waits its turn. The gift pack calls it when the car drives over a package (see "The gift pack drop"); with the switch on, every level-up calls it at once. Ignored when the run is not going |
| `choice_made()` | The player made the level-up's weapon choice (T-052 calls it): the next waiting choice starts, or the run resumes. Ignored when no choice is waiting |
| `is_waiting_for_choice()`, `choice_level()` | Whether the run is paused for a choice, and the level whose choice is on show (0 for none) |

| Value | Is |
| --- | --- |
| `xp` | XP toward the next level (after leftovers), 0 at the start |
| `level` | The level, 1 at the start |
| `pause_on_level_up` | The level-up pause switch, **off** (the run scene keeps it off since T-144). Off: a level-up raises the level and the run carries on; in the run scene the gift pack's package opens the choice through `open_choice()` when it is driven over. On (tests and tools only): each level-up pauses the run (`get_tree().paused`, so the clock and the car stop) at once until `choice_made()`, with no helicopter; two level-ups at once wait one after the other |
| `pending_choices` | The levels whose choice is waiting (opened by `open_choice()`), oldest first |

| Signal | Fires |
| --- | --- |
| `xp_changed(xp: float, xp_needed: float, level: int)` | When XP or the level changes (gains, level-ups, every frame the likes drain takes XP away), and on start. For the HUD's likes bar (T-049) |
| `score(amount: float, challenge_id: String)` | When a challenge is completed, with the XP (likes) it gave. For the challenge popup (T-092) |
| `level_up(new_level: int)` | Once per level reached, switch on or off |
| `level_up_choice_started(new_level: int)` | `open_choice()` (the package picked up, or the switch on): the run is now paused for that level's choice (T-052 opens its screen here) |
| `level_up_choice_made(new_level: int)` | `choice_made()` closed that level's choice |

A new run (`start_run()`) resets XP to 0 and the level to 1, forgets the drift in progress and any waiting choice (unpausing if it was waiting). Nothing is added after the run ends.

**The likes drain** (task T-115; the rule is Game loop architecture, Decisions, "Likes drain", the numbers are `likes_drain` in `data/run.json` through `RunData`, task T-114). Once the combo is back at "no combo" (`combo` at `start_multiplier`, after the combo's own wait and drain), and after `likes_drain_extra_wait_seconds` (0 for now), `xp` goes down by `RunData.likes_drain_xp_per_second(level)` per second, stopping at 0: the level never goes down. Any trick raises the combo, which stops the drain until the combo has drained again. It runs inside `advance()` (`_drain_likes()`), so it stops while the tree is paused (pause screen, level-up screen) and after the run ends; it also skips while a weapon choice is waiting. At the top level there is nothing to drain: XP stays at 0 there and the HUD shows the bar full. Each frame it takes XP away it sends `xp_changed`, so the HUD's likes bar empties (the bar rounds to whole likes, its step being 1, so it moves one like at a time).

| Function or value | Is |
| --- | --- |
| `has_no_combo()` | True when the combo is at "no combo" |
| `is_likes_draining()` | True while the drain is taking XP away now (no combo, the extra wait passed, XP above 0, not the top level, no choice waiting, the run going) |
| `likes_drain_idle` | Seconds the combo has been at "no combo"; a trick or a new run sets it to 0 |

A screenshot fixture, `tests/fixtures/likes_drain_demo.tscn`, starts a run from the main menu with level 2 and a fifth of its bar, then does nothing, printing the time, XP and level every half second: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/likes_drain_demo.tscn screenshots/likes_drain_1.png 90` (the bar emptying; at T-120's speed the fifth of level 2's bar is gone in about 2.5 s) and `... screenshots/likes_drain_2.png 3500` (empty, still level 2). The frame counts depend on the screen's refresh rate; the printed lines say how far in each shot is.

**The level-up effect** (task T-112; Visual style, Decisions, "Level-up effect"): at every level-up the car gives off one burst of pixel particles. The run scene connects the state's `level_up` signal to its burst, `run.level_up_burst`, a `CPUParticles2D` from `scenes/run/level_up_burst.tscn` (script `scripts/run/level_up_burst.gd`: `play()` starts the burst, `is_playing()`). It sits under `View`, drawn over the car, and `sync_view()` keeps it on the drawn car; `local_coords` is on, so the particles move with the car while they play. Several level-ups in the same moment (one gain giving two levels) restart the same burst, so they show as one burst. Its `process_mode` is Always, so it keeps playing while the run is paused for the level-up screen (T-052).

- **Particle art (task T-074, a proposal for the board's review):** `art/effects/level_up_particles.png`, one row of six 11 x 11 frames drawn by a script (no PixelLab, board 2026-10-13): a cyan and a pink four-point star, a pink and a cyan diamond, a violet pixel cross and a small cyan spark, each with a white-hot core and stepped darker halo pixels (glow in the pixels), 13 colours in all, no red. Its record, with the palette, is `art/effects/level_up_particles.json`. Built and checked by `python game/tools/build_level_up_particles.py build` (byte-identical every run) and `python game/tools/build_level_up_particles.py check` (every frame: transparent background, no semi-transparent pixels, about 30 colours or fewer; the whole sheet: no red; exit 1 on a fail). Python tests: `game/tools/test_build_level_up_particles.py` (also fails if the committed PNG or record is out of date).
- **How the scene uses it:** a `CanvasItemMaterial` with particles animation (`particles_anim_h_frames` 6) reads the sheet as a flipbook; `anim_offset` 0 to 1 with `anim_speed` 0 gives every particle one random shape for its whole life. `scale_amount` 0.5 draws the art at the car's density (32 art pixels per unit at 16 world pixels per unit); particles are never rotated. Tuning: `amount` 40, `lifetime` 0.8 s with `lifetime_randomness` 0.35, `emission_sphere_radius` 24, `spread` 180, `initial_velocity` 180 to 320, `damping` 180 to 240. They do not fade: `scale_amount_curve` keeps full size until 65% of the life, steps to half size, and pops out at 86%. The placeholder (a 4 x 4 pale cyan square, T-112) is gone.
- **To swap the art and retune it (T-074):** change only `scenes/run/level_up_burst.tscn` (and redraw the sheet with the script above): `texture` (the particle sprite; keep `color` white so the sprite shows its own colours), `amount`, `lifetime`, `initial_velocity_min/max`, `damping_min/max`, `spread`, `emission_sphere_radius`, `scale_amount_curve` and the flipbook material's frame count. Keep `texture_filter` Nearest, `one_shot`, `explosiveness` 1, `process_mode` Always and every colour's alpha at 1; the tests below check them. When and how it plays is in `scripts/run/run.gd` and does not change.
- **Screenshot** of the burst at a level-up in a run from the main menu: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_up_burst_demo.tscn screenshots/level_up_burst.png 40`.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/run/test_level_up_burst.gd` (part of the GUT suite): not playing before, started by the `level_up` signal and by a real level-up, on the car and following it, two level-ups at once, one burst that ends, still playing while paused, the settings in the scene, nearest-neighbour, no alpha fade, and particle art that is not the heart's red. `tests/unit/run/test_level_up_particles_art.gd` (T-074): the scene uses the sheet, the sheet matches its record, one random shape per particle, the car's art density, no rotation, the stepped shrink with no fade.

**The combo** (task T-081; the rules are Game loop architecture, Decisions, the numbers are `data/combo.json` through `ComboData`). The run scene connects the car's `trick` signal to `state.add_trick` and the car's `hit_obstacle` (walls, buildings, the map edge, boulders) to `state.collision`. `damage_car()` also calls `collision()`, so any damage to the car (enemies, meteors) cuts the combo. The multiplier multiplies all XP gained: `challenge_completed()` (and its `score` signal), the drift seconds of `drift_step()`, and `add_xp()`.

| Function | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `add_trick(trick_id: String)` | The car did a trick: the multiplier goes up by `trick_add[trick_id]`, held at `max_multiplier`, and the drain wait starts again. Sends `combo_trick`, then `combo_changed` (if it changed). An id that is not one of `TrickDetector.TRICK_IDS` is ignored |
| `collision()` | The car collided with something: the multiplier is cut by `collision_cut` (80%) of the part above `start_multiplier` (`ComboData.after_collision`) |
| `combo_spawn_multiplier()` | How much the minion spawn rate is multiplied at the current combo (`ComboData.spawn_multiplier`, 1.0 with no combo). For enemy spawning (T-085) |

| Value | Is |
| --- | --- |
| `combo` | The multiplier now, `start_multiplier` (1.0, no combo) at the start of a run |
| `combo_idle` | Seconds since the last trick (or the run's start). Once it passes `drain_wait_seconds` (2 s), the multiplier drains by `drain_per_second`, down to `start_multiplier` |

| Signal | Fires |
| --- | --- |
| `combo_trick(trick_id: String, added: float, multiplier: float)` | Every trick during the run: the trick, how much the multiplier went up (the data's amount, less at the cap, 0 once there) and the new multiplier. For the trick popups (T-083) |
| `combo_changed(multiplier: float)` | Whenever the multiplier changes (a trick, a collision, the drain, every frame while draining) and on start. For the HUD's combo counter (T-083) and enemy spawning (T-085) |

The drain runs with the clock (`advance()`), so it stops while the run is paused, and the drain wait does not count paused time. A new run resets the multiplier and the drain wait. Nothing changes after the run ends.

**Pause:** the clock runs in `_process` with the default (pausable) process mode, so setting `get_tree().paused = true` stops it. Anything that pauses the run (the pause screen, the level-up choice) does it that way. The run's length (8 minutes) and the boss's arrival (7 minutes in) are constants in `run_state.gd`, `RUN_SECONDS` and `BOSS_TIME_LEFT`, because they are Decisions, not balancing numbers.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/run/test_run_state.gd` (the clock, boss time, health, coins, win and loss, pause, a second run), `tests/unit/run/test_run_scene.gd` (the map and car loaded, the start position, the camera following the car, a second run resetting the map and car) and `tests/unit/run/test_run_likes.gd` (challenge and meteor challenge XP, drift seconds, level-ups and leftovers, the pause switch off and on, two level-ups at once, the top level, a new run resetting XP) and `tests/unit/run/test_run_combo.gd` (tricks raising the multiplier, the cap, likes multiplied, collisions and damage cutting it, the drain and a trick stopping it, a new run, pause, a run started from the main menu) and `tests/unit/run/test_run_likes_drain.gd` (T-115: no drain with a combo, the drain at T-114's speed once it is gone, a trick stopping it, never below the level's start even over a long drain, no drain while paused, while a choice waits, after the run ends or at the top level, the extra wait, the switch, `xp_changed` firing and the HUD's likes bar emptying in a run), part of the GUT suite.

## Minions

**Health drops (T-220).** `data/enemies.json` `health_drops` (`drop_chance`, `restore_fraction`, read through `EnemyData.health_drops()`). When a minion dies, `MinionSpawner._on_minion_died()` rolls `roll_health_drop()` (its own roll, apart from the coins') and calls `spawn_health_pickup_at(units)`, which adds `scripts/enemies/health_pickup.gd` (an `Area2D` on the pickups layer, mask 2, group `health_pickups`, built like the coin; `spawner.health_pickups` lists them, as `spawner.coins` lists coins) with its drawing `health_pickup_sprite.gd` (the final canister art `art/effects/health_canister.png`, 14 x 10, T-221: the board-approved PixelLab sample `art-archive/T-221/canister_raw.png` with three free script fixes by `python game/tools/build_health_canister.py`, no PixelLab: the white mark redrawn as a clean plus, the darkest red moved away from the old logo's red so no colour is within 45 of it, and scaled down to about a coin's size; 6 colours; test `game/tools/test_build_health_canister.py` and `test_canister_art_is_not_old_logo_red` in `test_health_drops.gd`; screenshots `art-archive/T-221/screenshots/`), at the ground height and in the depth order like a coin. Driving over it calls `RunState.heal_car(restore_fraction * max_health)` (capped at the maximum, no combo effect) and frees it. The kaiju drops none. Cleared by `clear()` on a new run. The chance is 0.3 (T-277: halved from 0.6, the coins' stays 0.6; the restore stays 0.3 of the maximum). Tests: `tests/unit/enemies/test_health_drops.gd`. Screenshot: `tests/fixtures/minions_demo.tscn` (two canisters beside the coins).

Task T-044 (Enemy Behavior Agent): the MVP minion as Enemies, Decisions describes it, with T-039's numbers (`data/enemies.json` through `EnemyData`, "Game data" above). What it does: `docs/enemies.md`, Content, "The minion". Placeholders for now (a light green four-legged lizard drawn in code, gold coins); the final art comes from separate art tasks.

**See it:** start the game (F5) and press Start, or open `scenes/run/run.tscn` and press F6. Minions start coming in from the screen's edges at once and walk towards the car. Screenshots, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/minions_demo.tscn screenshots/minions.png 20` (minions and two coins around the car in the gravel square) and `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/minions_draw_order_demo.tscn screenshots/minions_draw_order.png 20` (the car on the road beside a block of skyscrapers and two boulders, minions in front of and behind them). Both start the run from the main menu (fixture script `tests/fixtures/minions_demo.gd`). Copies are in `art-archive/T-044/screenshots/`.

**How it is built** (`scripts/enemies/`):

- `minion_spawner.gd`: the run's minions. The run scene adds it as `run.minions` (child `Minions`) and calls `bind(run)`. It moves everything in `step(dt)`, once per physics step from `_physics_process` (pausable, so the pause screen and the level-up pause stop the minions and their damage), and does nothing once the run is over. On `run.world_rebuilt` (a new run) it removes every minion and coin and follows the new car and map. `enabled = false` stops it (tests).
- `minion.gd`: one minion, a `CharacterBody2D` in the run's `PhysicsWorld` (flat world pixels, 16 per unit, like the car) on layer 3 (`enemies`) with mask 1 (obstacles): a circle of `RADIUS_UNITS` (0.5). It slides along walls, buildings and boulders and walks round one it meets head-on (`detour_side`, picked at random per minion); it never pushes or blocks the car. In the groups `enemies` and `minions`.
- `minion_sprite.gd`: the minion's final art (T-068): 8-frame walk sheets in `art/minion/` (`minion_s/se/e/ne/n.png`, 8 frames of 48 x 48 side by side; the other three directions mirror se, e and ne), drawn at scale 0.5 (16 screen pixels per unit), the direction chosen from the way it walks, 10 frames a second, each minion at a random phase. It stops with the game's pause. No shadow under it (board tweak, 2026-10-15). Falls back to the old placeholder shape if a sheet is missing. Sheets are built by `tools/build_minion_sheets.py` from `art-archive/T-068/`; `tools/fix_minion_frames.py` holds the free pixel fixes.
- `coin_sprite.gd`: the placeholder coin drawing, in the isometric view.
- `coin.gd`: a coin, an `Area2D` on layer 4 (`pickups`) with mask 2 (the car).
- `enemy_depth.gd`: the minions' and coins' place in the buildings' depth order.

**Spawning:** the spawner keeps `minions_wanted_now()` minions around, which is `EnemyData.minions_wanted(state.time_elapsed(), spawn_multiplier)` (12 at the start growing to 20 at 7:00, then held). While fewer are around, one spawns every `spawn_interval_seconds` (0.5 s; the first at once), at a random point on the ring `spawn_margin_units` (3) beyond the edge of the visible screen (the camera's view, so it follows the zoom), inside the map and clear of buildings and boulders (`is_free_spot()`; 12 tries, else it waits for the next interval). A minion a whole screen beyond the visible screen (the car drove away from it) is removed without coins and the spawner refills at the edge, so the minions stay around the car.

**The combo raises spawning (T-085):** every call of `minions_wanted_now()` sets `run.minions.spawn_multiplier` from `RunState.combo_spawn_multiplier()` (`ComboData.spawn_multiplier(combo)`: 1.0 with no combo, up to the data's `max_spawn_multiplier` in `data/combo.json`), so the count follows the combo live. **The switch:** `run.minions.combo_raises_spawning` (a variable of `minion_spawner.gd`, default `true`). Set it to `false` and the combo changes nothing: the multiplier stays 1.0. To flip it for a playtest, change the default on that line, or set it from a test or playtest scene. It is a setting, not a key and not player-facing text. Tests: `tests/unit/enemies/test_minions.gd`.

**Movement:** straight towards the car's centre at `move_speed` (6 units/s), plus a small push away from other minions closer than 1.2 units (`Minion.SEPARATION_UNITS`), so a crowd spreads round the car.

**Contact damage:** a minion touches the car when its circle overlaps the car's 1 x 1 unit body (`touches_car()`). While touching, it hits once every `contact_damage_interval` (0.5 s), the first hit as soon as it touches: `RunState.damage_car(contact_damage)` (5; this also cuts the combo) and `car.apply_knockback(away from the minion, knockback)` (8 units/s). Each minion keeps its own interval, so two minions touching hit twice as often.

**Damaging a minion (for the weapon tasks, T-041 and T-042):**

```
func take_damage(amount: float) -> void
```

Call it on a minion node. `amount` is in the same points as `health` (4, `data/enemies.json`). Zero or negative amounts, and calls on a dead minion, are ignored. At 0 health the minion dies: its `died(minion, position_units)` signal fires once, it leaves the groups `enemies` and `minions` at once, its drawing goes, and the node is freed at the end of the frame (so calling it from an `Area2D` callback is safe). To find targets: `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` (the minions and, once it has appeared, the kaiju: see "The kaiju") or `run.minions.minions` (the live minions). Each has `position_units()` (flat world, units; `position` is in flat world pixels), `health`, `max_health` and `is_dead()`. Bodies are on layer 3, so a projectile's `Area2D` finds them with a mask of 4. Check `is_dead()` before damaging one found earlier in the same frame.

**Pushing a minion (T-167, for the windshield wiper):**

```
func apply_knockback(direction: Vector2, strength: float) -> void
func is_knocked_back() -> bool
```

Call it on a minion node. `direction` is in the flat world (any length; it is normalized), `strength` is the distance to push in units (flat world units, 16 pixels each; the wiper's 6 in `docs/weapons.md` is one). The minion travels that distance over `Minion.KNOCKBACK_SECONDS` (0.15 s) at an even speed, inside its own `walk()` steps, so the spawner's `step()` moves it and the pause stops it; while pushed it does not walk or separate, then walks on as before. It moves with the same `move_and_collide` and mask 1 as its walking: walls, buildings, the map's edge and boulders stop it, it slides along them, and the part of the push into an obstacle is dropped, so it is never pushed into one. A second call before the push ends adds its distance to what is left and restarts the 0.15 s. Ignored when `strength` is zero or negative, `direction` is zero, or the minion is dead. `is_knocked_back()` is true while a push is moving it. Without a call, minion movement is unchanged. **Only minions can be pushed:** the kaiju (`kaiju.gd`) has no `apply_knockback`, so a weapon checks `has_method("apply_knockback")` (or the `minions` group) before pushing. (The car has its own, different `apply_knockback`, in units/s: "Knockback" above.)

**Coins:** when a minion dies, `drop_chance` (0.6) decides whether it drops coins; if so, `coins_per_drop` (2) coins of 1 each land around where it died (`roll_coin_drop()`, using the spawner's `rng`; seed it for tests). Driving over a coin (the car's body entering its circle of 0.5 units) collects it: `RunState.add_coins(1)`, and the coin and its drawing are gone. Coins stay until collected or until a new run. `run.minions.coins` lists them; `spawn_coin_at(units, value)` adds one. The spawner's `minion_killed(minion, coins)` signal fires per death with the number of coins dropped.

**Draw order:** each minion's and coin's drawing is in `run.minions.draw_root` (the node `Enemies`, just before the car's `View` in the run, Y-sorted). Every physics step each drawing is placed on its body in the isometric view and given a `z_index` by the map's rule for the car (`map.car_z_index()`, "See-through buildings" above): one under the lowest building slot or boulder sprite that covers it (it stands behind it and their outlines overlap on screen), otherwise `map.top_z_index()`, over every building. Nothing fades for a minion: the fade stays car-only, so a minion behind an opaque skyscraper is hidden by it, and one behind a building faded for the car shows through it. Where the car and a minion get the same z, the car is drawn over the minion.

**Not in the data:** the minion's and coin's sizes (0.5 unit radius each), the separation distance (1.2 units) and the rule that removes minions a whole screen behind are in the code (`minion.gd`, `coin.gd`, `minion_spawner.gd`): sizes and rules of the placeholder, not balancing numbers.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/enemies/test_minions.gd`, part of the GUT suite: more minions at the end of the 7 minutes than at the start (the run's clock moved on, the count matching `minions_wanted`), one spawn per interval off the screen and clear of obstacles, the combo hook, a minion walking straight at the car at the data's speed and round a building, contact damage at the data's rate with knockback away from the minion, no damage without touching, death at 0 health (once), coins dropping at about the data's chance over 600 deaths, driving over a coin collecting it, minions standing still while paused, a new run clearing everything, nothing moving after the run ends, minions far behind being removed, the collision layers, the draw order under a covering building and over the rest, and clear of the heart's red in the placeholders. T-167's knockback tests: a push in open ground moves the minion the call's distance in its direction (farther from the push's origin) within 0.15 s, then it walks on; pushes into a building and into a boulder stop at it and never inside; bad calls and dead minions are ignored; two pushes add up; the kaiju has no knockback call.

**Coin art and the hit tint (T-072):** `coin.png` (10 x 12, redrawn by hand in the palette of the one PixelLab sample, `art-archive/T-072/sample/`; `python game/tools/build_hit_coin_damage_effects.py build` redraws it, byte-identical, and `check` runs the rules) is what `coin_sprite.gd` draws, still bobbing. The board played the first version, which also had a hit flash, a death puff and a car damage effect, and dropped those (2026-10-15); the drawn sheets are kept in `art-archive/T-072/unused_effects/`. The one effect left: an enemy that takes damage and survives gets a slight red tint, briefly. `scripts/enemies/hit_tint.gd` (`HitTint`, `DURATION` 0.12 s, `TINT` pale red `Color(1, 0.62, 0.62)`, not the heart's red) tints the owner's `sprite` through `modulate` and restores it by itself. **Used by** `minion.gd` and `kaiju.gd` (both call `HitTint.attach(self)` once in `_ready()` and `hit_tint.flash()` in `take_damage()` when still alive); any other enemy with a `sprite` property (the CanvasItem to tint) can do the same. It works on T-068's animated minion sprite, which is drawn in `_draw`, because `modulate` tints everything the node draws. **With the flamethrower's orange burn flash (T-042):** it never fights it: the tint does not start while the sprite already has another tint, and puts the colour back only if the sprite still has its own red, so orange always wins. **The car shake (T-072; board: "a short shake or wobble of the car" when it takes damage):** `RunState.car_damaged(amount)` (emitted by `damage_car`) makes `run.gd` call `car_sprite.shake()` (`scripts/driving/car_sprite.gd`): for `SHAKE_SECONDS` 0.2 s the drawing moves about `SHAKE_PIXELS` 3 screen pixels (20 Hz, dying down) through the sprite's `offset` only, so the car's physics, position, collisions, the popups and the camera are unchanged. It runs in `_process`, so pausing stops it. Tests: `game/tools/test_build_hit_coin_damage_effects.py` and the T-072 tests at the end of `tests/unit/enemies/test_minions.gd`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-072/screenshots/` (`tests/fixtures/effects_demo.tscn`, needs a window).

## Enemies on the rooftops and ramps (T-207)

Task T-207 (Enemy Behavior Agent): on a map with rooftops and ramps (`map.elevation`, the elevation map the run plays on, T-206) the minions and the kaiju stand on the ground's heights, are stopped by rooftop edges and find their way up and down the ramps to the car (Enemies, Decisions, "Enemies follow the car up the ramps"; what they do: `docs/enemies.md`, Content, "Enemies on the rooftops and ramps"). On the MVP map (`run.use_elevation_map = false`) none of it runs and they walk as before.

**How it is built** (`scripts/enemies/`):

- `enemy_height.gd` (static functions, no state): the rules on the ground's heights (`GroundHeight`, `scripts/level/ground_height.gd`). `MAX_STEP` (0.6 units): a move whose ground height changes more than that is refused, so every rooftop edge (3 or 6 units) and the high part of a ramp's sides is a wall, and a ramp (0.25 a unit) is not. `fits(ground, units, radius)`: the ground under four points of the body's circle is within `MAX_STEP` of its centre's, so a body keeps off an edge. `limit_motion(ground, from, motion, radius)` cuts a move into `SUB_STEP` (0.25 unit) pieces, stops at the first refused one and slides the rest along the free axis (every edge runs north-south or east-west); it returns the moves to make and the edge's normal. `at_car_level(enemy_ground, car_height)`: within `LEVEL_TOLERANCE` (1 unit).
- `minion.gd` and `kaiju.gd`: `ground` (null on a flat map), `ground_height()`, and `_move(motion)`, which every walk and knockback step goes through: `limit_motion` first, then `move_and_collide` as before; an edge that stopped it becomes its wall normal, so the detour round obstacles works on edges too, and a knockback loses the part pushing over an edge. `contact_step()` returns false when the car is not at its level. `follower`: its `enemy_path_follower.gd`.
- `enemy_navigation.gd`: the pathfinding. An `AStar2D` over a grid of 2 x 2 unit cells covering the map (120 x 120, about 12 250 open points): cells in a building (grown by 0.5) or on one of the map's boulders are left out, and two neighbouring cells (sides and corners) are linked only when their ground heights differ by `MAX_STEP` or less (a corner link only when all four cells of its square do). Built once per map in about 27 ms and kept on the map (`EnemyNavigationScript.for_map(map)`, `map.get_meta("enemy_navigation")`), shared by the minions and the kaiju; a new run's map builds a new one. `find_path(from, to)` returns the cell centres, with each climb along a ramp replaced by two points on the ramp's centre line 1.5 units beyond its ends (`ramp_end()`), so every enemy goes up a ramp along its middle. `surface_at(units)` is the ground's area index (`-1` for the street). At most `PATHS_PER_STEP` (4) paths per step (`new_step()`, called by the spawner), so a crowd asks over a few steps. Why `AStar2D`: the heights are rectangles in the map file, so a grid at 2 units is exact (every edge lies on even units) and needs no baking; `AStarGrid2D` cannot leave out the link between two open cells, which is what an edge is here; a navigation mesh would need a mesh per level and links for the ramps.
- `enemy_path_follower.gd`: one per enemy. `target(nav, from, car_units, dt)` returns the car when the enemy is on the car's surface, otherwise the next point of its path (reached within 1 unit), asking for a new path when the car changes surface and every `REPATH_SECONDS` (1 s; each minion's clock starts at a random point).
- `minion_spawner.gd`: on `world_rebuilt` it takes `map.elevation.ground_height` and the navigation (`ground`, `navigation`; both null on a flat map or with `heights_enabled = false`, a switch for tests and the soak's comparison, read when a run starts). `step()` = spawn, `move_step(dt)` (walk each minion to its follower's target, contact damage; tests call it alone), remove far ones, draw. Spawn points must be on the street (`is_free_spot`, `elevation.is_street`). Minions push apart only from minions within 1 unit of their height. A minion whose follower has a path is removed only beyond `ON_PATH_KEEP_UNITS` (120) from the car, others a screen beyond the visible one as before. Sprites and coins are placed lifted by their ground height (`ISO * position + (0, -height * 16)`) and get `depth.z_index_for(..., ground_h)`. `last_step_usec` is the last step's time (the soak reads it).
- `kaiju_boss.gd`: the same `ground`, `navigation`, `heights_enabled`, follower (`target(..., budgeted = false)`), street-only arrival, lifted drawing.
- `enemy_depth.gd`: `z_index_for(centre, radius, height, ground_h = 0.0)` lifts the screen shape by `ground_h` and passes it to `elevation.z_under_areas()`, so an enemy is over the rooftop it stands on and under a higher one in front of it.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/enemies/test_enemies_on_heights.gd`: the navigation (one per map, shared, rebuilt per run, none on the MVP map; no link climbing more than a step, every drop link one-way (since T-226); no link up any rooftop edge; buildings and boulders closed; a path from the street to height 6 goes up both ramps in order), the follower (straight on the car's surface, a path otherwise, no new path before a second), the path budget, keeping minions on their way to a ramp; minions from four street spots reach a car parked on each height-6 rooftop by both ramps within 40 s (about 15 to 27 s), never changing height more than a step in one step; with the drop links left out (T-226), minions on the rooftops come down a ramp to a car on the street; minions driven at every rooftop edge (each side, every 3 units, ramps left out) neither climb it from below nor step off it from above; a knockback does not push one over an edge; spawning on the street; drawing lifted and in the depth order (over the roof it stands on, under it from behind, under a higher roof, over a ramp it is on); contact damage only at the car's level (from below an edge, from above, a car in the air); the kaiju climbing to a height-3 car by the ramp, stopped by an edge, hitting only at its level, arriving on the street.

**Frame time:** `python game/tools/run_scene_headless.py res://tests/fixtures/run_elevation_soak_screen.tscn 150 --show` (T-206's 2-minute scripted run with the game window's screen size; `..._screen_no_heights.tscn` with the enemies ignoring the heights, to compare; `run_elevation_soak.tscn` as T-206 ran it, headless screen) prints the physics time per step and the minions' own step at the most minions on screen. `python game/tools/run_scene_headless.py res://tests/fixtures/enemies_heights_stress.tscn 60 --show` puts 30 minions on the street on screen round a car parked at height 6 (and `..._no_heights.tscn`).

**Screenshots** (`tests/fixtures/enemies_heights_demo.gd`; the run from the main menu, the car parked, minions walked by the spawner for a few seconds, then still): `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/enemies_heights_ramp.tscn screenshots/ramp.png 20` (minions climbing ramp-nw-0-3 to a car on roof-nw-3), `..._rooftop.tscn` (minions on ramp-se-3-6 and on roof-se-6 by the car), `..._edge.tscn` (minions on the street under a rooftop's edge walking round to the ramp), `..._kaiju.tscn` (the kaiju half way up ramp-nw-0-3). Copies: `art-archive/T-207/screenshots/`.

## Enemies drop off ledges (T-226)

Task T-226 (Enemy Behavior Agent): going down, the minions and the kaiju drop off a rooftop's edge to chase the car instead of walking round to a ramp; going up still needs a ramp, and an edge still blocks them from below (Enemies, Decisions, "Enemies jump down off ledges"; what they do: `docs/enemies.md`, Content, "Enemies drop off ledges"). Nothing changes on the MVP map.

**Falling art (T-227):** `game/art/minion/minion_fall_s/se/e/ne/n.png` (8 frames of 48 x 48, 10 a second; the other three directions mirror them) are the minion's falling sheets, one PixelLab `/animate-with-text-v3` call each on the minion's rotation; `minion_sprite.gd` plays them through T-226's hook, so the placeholder fall is used only if a sheet is missing. Build: `python game/tools/build_minion_fall_sheets.py [dir ...]` (snaps colours to the walk sheets' palette, clears the south sheet's chin streak, reads `art-archive/T-227/fall_<dir>/`; test `game/tools/test_build_minion_fall_sheets.py`). Check: `python game/tools/style_rules.py art game/art/minion/minion_fall_e.png --size 48x48 --frames 8 --max-colors 49`. The kaiju still uses the placeholder.

**How it is built** (`scripts/enemies/`):

- `enemy_navigation.gd`: after T-207's links, `_add_drops()` adds a one-way link (`connect_points(k, e, false)`) from every open cell off the ramps to each of its four side neighbours that is open, off the ramps and more than `MAX_STEP` lower (238 on the elevation map; `drop_links`). Nothing links back up. `is_drop(a, b)`: two path points a cell apart with `b` more than a step lower (a ramp's top and foot points are much farther apart, so a ramp is never a drop). `build_for_map(map, drops = true)` builds a navigation that is not kept on the map; `drops = false` is T-207's grid (the tests' "before").
- `enemy_path_follower.gd`: `dropping(nav, from, body_h)` is true while the target is the point below an edge and the enemy is still above it, or the target is the point on an edge, `DROP_NEAR` (3 units) away or less, and the path drops from there (3 units so the kaiju, which keeps its 2-unit radius off an edge, still reaches that point).
- `enemy_height.gd`: `can_step`, `limit_motion` and `_slide` take the body's height (`body_h`, NAN: the ground's, as before) and `drop`. Ground more than `MAX_STEP` above the body is always a wall; with `drop` a step down of any size is allowed and the body need not fit. The body's height is followed piece by piece (`_next_height`), so once it is over lower ground it stays in the air at the height it left.
- `enemy_fall.gd` (new): one per enemy (`fall`). `start(h)`, `step(dt, ground_h)` (the car's `GRAVITY`, 12 units/s², from rest; true on the landing step), `body_height(ground_h)`, `landings`.
- `minion.gd` and `kaiju.gd`: `may_drop` (set each step by the spawner or the boss from `follower.dropping()`), `fall`, `body_height()`, `is_falling()`. A walk moves with `drop = may_drop or falling`; after it `_fall_step(dt, h0)` starts a fall when the body has walked out over ground more than a step under its height before the move, and steps the fall. A knockback moves with `drop` only while already falling, so it never takes an enemy over an edge. `contact_step()` compares the car's height with `body_height()`. `set_position_units()` ends any fall.
- `minion_spawner.gd`, `kaiju_boss.gd`: set `may_drop`, draw at `body_height()` (position lift and `depth.z_index_for(..., body_height())`), call `sprite.set_falling(is_falling())`. Pushing apart uses `body_height()`. `drops_total` counts the minions' drops (the soak prints it).
- `minion_sprite.gd`, `kaiju_sprite.gd`: `set_falling(on)`, `advance(delta)`, `animation()` ("walk", "fall", "land"), `anim_transform()`. The placeholder fall is made by script from the walk frames: the walk cycle at `FLAIL_SPEED` times its speed (the legs flail) and the drawing tipping from side to side (`FALL_TILT`, `FALL_WOBBLE_HZ`; the minion also stretched by `FALL_STRETCH`); after landing a squash (`LAND_SQUASH` easing back over `LAND_SECONDS`). The kaiju draws no ground shadow while it falls. **The art hook (T-227):** falling sheets in `art/minion/minion_fall_<dir>.png` and `art/kaiju/kaiju_fall_<dir>.png` (`FALL_SHEET_DIR`; `<dir>` is e, se, s, ne, n, the other three mirrored as the walk sheets), frames of the walk's `FRAME_SIZE` in one row, as many as the sheet is wide, played at `FALL_FPS` from the moment the enemy leaves the edge. When a direction's sheet exists it is drawn as it is, without the scripted tilt; when it does not, the placeholder. The landing squash applies to both.
- Fix found on the way: a mirrored walk frame (west, south-west, north-west) was drawn one frame width to the right of the enemy (Godot draws a negative-width rect from its x to x + width, flipped), so the kaiju's art stood beside its shadow and health bar. Both sprites now put the rect at `-size / 2` either way.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/enemies/test_enemies_drop_off_ledges.gd`: one-way drop links on every edge (none with `drops = false`); a path from roof-nw-6 to a street car drops once and is much shorter than the way round, a path up still goes by both ramps with no drop; four minions on roof-nw-6 reach a car on the street beside it by dropping, without a ramp, sooner than the same minions on T-207's grid (both times printed), never in a building and never climbing; a minion with `may_drop` still cannot climb an edge from below; without a drop on its path a minion does not step off; minions on the street still go up by the ramps; minions from every rooftop to cars round them never end up in a building; a falling minion's height only goes down, its drawing is lifted by it and in the depth order at it at every step, and the falling animation plays exactly while it falls ("land" right after); the sprites' animation states; the fall's time under the car's gravity; contact damage while falling only at the height in the air; a knockback never takes a minion over an edge; the kaiju drops off roof-nw-6 to a car below. T-207's tests on the grid were changed to the new rule (no link climbs; T-207's ramp-down test runs with the drops left out).

**Recordings** (`tests/fixtures/enemies_drop_demo.gd`: T-207's fixture, then the enemies keep chasing the parked car live, one step per physics frame; `stop_at_seconds` freezes them for a still, `quit_after_seconds` ends the scene): `"$GODOT_BIN" --path game --fixed-fps 50 --write-movie C:/abs/drop/f.png res://tests/fixtures/enemies_drop_gif.tscn` (six minions on roof-nw-6, the car on the street below), `enemies_drop_kaiju.tscn` (the kaiju), `enemies_drop_falling.tscn` (a still, 2.1 s in). Copies: `art-archive/T-226/screenshots/`.

## Weapons

Task T-041 (Weapon Behavior Agent): the starting gun, and how weapons plug into the run. What the gun does: `docs/weapons.md`, Content, "The starting gun". Numbers: `data/weapons.json` through `WeaponData` ("Game data" above); nothing in the weapon code changes them. The bullets are placeholders (a small cyan dot with a white core and a dark outline, drawn in code); the final bullet art comes from a separate art task and replaces `bullet.gd`'s `_draw()`.

**See it:** start the game (F5) and press Start, or open `scenes/run/run.tscn` and press F6. Once a minion is within 12 units of the car, the gun fires at it every 1.26 s. Screenshots, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/starting_gun_demo.tscn screenshots/starting_gun.png 16` (the base gun's bullet on its way to the closest minion) and `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/starting_gun_tier2_demo.tscn screenshots/starting_gun_tier2.png 34` (the gun at tier 2, two of its three bullets in the air). Both start the run from the main menu, place four standing minions round the car and set the gun's clock to fire on the first step (fixture script `tests/fixtures/starting_gun_demo.gd`). Copies are in `art-archive/T-041/screenshots/`.

**How weapons plug into the run** (`scripts/weapons/`, for T-042 and later weapons):

- `weapons.gd`, **the weapon rack**: the run scene adds it as `run.weapons` (child `Weapons`) and calls `bind(run)`; that is the only change to `run.gd`. It holds one node of each weapon in `WEAPON_SCRIPTS`, created in `_init()` and kept for the whole session (`run.weapons.weapons`, a Dictionary by id; `run.weapons.gun` is the starting gun). Once per physics step (`_physics_process`, pausable, so the pause screen and the level-up pause stop the weapons) `step(dt)` moves every projectile, then steps every owned weapon; it does nothing once the run is over (`state.is_running()` false) or with `enabled = false` (tests). On `run.world_rebuilt` (every new run) it follows the new car and map, removes every projectile and calls each weapon's `reset_for_run()`: **a new run starts with the starting gun at tier 1 and nothing else.**
- `weapon.gd`, **the base of every weapon** (extend it with `extends "res://scripts/weapons/weapon.gd"`): `weapon_id` (the key in `data/weapons.json`: `gun`, `flamethrower`), `starts_owned` (true only for the gun), `tier` (0 = not owned, 1 = just got it, up to `max_tier()` = 8), `rack` (the rack, set for you), `data` (a `WeaponData`). `set_tier(t)` clamps, calls your `apply_tier()`, then emits `tier_changed(weapon_id, tier)`; `upgrade()` goes up one tier (0 to 1 gets the weapon) and returns false at the last tier; `is_owned()`, `is_max_tier()`; `reset_for_run()` sets tier 1 or 0 (override it to clear your own state, then call `super.reset_for_run()`). Override `step(dt)`: the rack calls it once per physics step while the weapon is owned and the run is going.
- **To add a weapon (T-042):** a new script in `scripts/weapons/` extending `weapon.gd`, setting `weapon_id` and `starts_owned` in `_init()`, reading its tier's values in `apply_tier()` (for example `data.flame_at(maxi(tier, 1))`), and doing its work in `step(dt)`; then add its preloaded script to `WEAPON_SCRIPTS` in `weapons.gd` (one line). It is then reachable as `run.weapons.get_weapon("flamethrower")`, reset every run, paused with the run, and upgradable through `run.weapons.upgrade(id)`.
- **What the rack gives a weapon:** `rack.car` (the run's car; `rack.car_units()` its centre in units, flat world), `rack.state` (the `RunState`), `rack.run` (the run scene), `rack.living_enemies()`, `rack.enemies_within(centre_units, radius_units)` (living enemies whose centre is within `radius_units`, closest first), `rack.radius_of(enemy)` (how far its body reaches, in units), `rack.is_target(node)`, `rack.add_projectile(node, parent = null)` (a `Node2D` with `step(dt)` and `is_done()`, drawn in `rack.draw_root`, or in `parent` when given, and freed once done or at a new run) and `rack.depth` (the minions' depth-order rule, `enemy_depth.gd`, for the `z_index` of anything drawn in the isometric view).
- **Upgrades from code** (the level-up offers them: "The level-up offers" below): `run.weapons.upgrade("gun")` applies the gun's next tier; `run.weapons.gun.set_tier(t)` sets any tier; `run.weapons.owned_ids()` lists the owned weapons; `run.weapons.get_weapon(id).tier` and `.is_max_tier()` say where each stands.

**Targets** (the minions and the kaiju, see "The kaiju"): every node in the group `enemies` inside the run that has `take_damage(amount: float)` and is not dead (`is_dead()`, when it has one; nodes queued for deletion are skipped). Each must have `position_units()` (its centre, flat world, units). Its size comes from `hit_radius_units()` when it has one, otherwise from its first `CollisionShape2D` (a circle's radius; half the diagonal of a rectangle; the larger of a capsule's radius and half height), otherwise 0.5 units. So **the kaiju needs only to join the group `enemies` and have `take_damage()` and `position_units()`**; if its body is not a circle, giving it `hit_radius_units()` makes hits match its size. Range is measured centre to centre (the target's size does not count, as in the prototype); hits use the target's size. Nothing here reads `run.minions` or uses collision layers, so meteors (on layer 3 but without `take_damage`) are never targeted.

**The starting gun** (`starting_gun.gd`, `weapon_id` `gun`): `stats` is `WeaponData.gun_at(tier)` (plus `bullet_drawn_size` for the placeholder). `fire_clock` counts physics time; when it reaches `fire_interval()` (1.26 s at tier 1) it goes back to 0 and the gun looks for enemies within `stats.range` of the car's centre. None: nothing happens until the next check, one interval later (the gun checks only once per interval, so an enemy that walks in just after a check waits up to 1.26 s). Some: a volley of `stats.bullets` bullets, `delay_between_bullets` (0.05 s) apart. **Targeting rule** (the prototype's, Unity weapons report, section 2, "How it chooses and aims at a target"): the enemies in range sorted by distance, **closest first**; bullet 1 goes to the closest, bullet 2 to the second closest, and so on, going round them again from the closest when there are more bullets than enemies. Each bullet leaves the car's centre aimed at its target's centre at that moment, with no leading; a target that died before its bullet left is aimed at where it was last seen. `fired(bullet, target)` fires per bullet; `fire_at(aim_units, target = null)` fires one bullet by hand (tests and fixtures). `checks` and `volleys` count the checks and the volleys that fired.

**The bullet** (`bullet.gd`, a `Node2D` in `rack.draw_root`, node `WeaponShots`, just before the car's `View`): flies in a straight line in the flat world at `bullet_speed` (15 units/s), starting on the physics step after it was fired. After each move it hits every living enemy whose body comes within `bullet_hit_radius` (1 unit) of its centre, closest first, each enemy at most once: `take_damage(damage)` (2) and its `hit(enemy, damage)` signal. It is done after `1 + pierce_hits` hits (1; 2 at tier 8), or once it has flown `missed_bullet_max_travel` (the gun's range at its tier). No knockback, and it passes through walls and boulders, as in the prototype. It is drawn 0.5 units above the ground at its place in the isometric view, with the minions' depth-order rule; at the same depth the car is drawn over it, so a bullet comes out from under the car.

**Not in the data:** the bullet's drawn height (0.5 units), its placeholder colours and the 0.5-unit fallback target size are in the code (`bullet.gd`, `weapons.gd`): sizes and looks of the placeholder, not balancing numbers.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/weapons/test_starting_gun.gd`, part of the GUT suite: the rack in the run with the gun at tier 1 and its draw order; no shots with no enemy or one just out of range; shots at the data's interval (1.26 s) with an enemy in range; one check per interval; the closest enemy targeted and damaged by the data's amount (2), the others untouched; a minion killed through `take_damage`; the bullet's speed and its start at the car's centre; the 1-unit hit circle plus the minion's body; a missed bullet removed past the range; a volley going round the enemies closest first, 0.05 s apart; every tier's values equal to `WeaponData.gun_at()`; `upgrade()` through the tiers and not past the last; tiers 2 and 6 firing 3 and 5 bullets; tiers 3, 4 and 7 shortening the interval to the data's; tier 5 reaching 15 units; tier 8 piercing one enemy (tier 7 not); a new run (also one started from the main menu) back to the base gun with no projectiles; a non-minion enemy in the group (a stand-in for the kaiju) targeted and damaged; nothing firing while paused or after the run ends; and clear of the heart's red in the placeholder bullet.

### The exhaust flamethrower

Task T-042 (Weapon Behavior Agent): the exhaust flamethrower as Weapons, Decisions describes it, copying the Unity prototype's Flame Exhaust (Unity weapons report, section 3) with the board's fixes, and T-040's numbers (`data/weapons.json` through `WeaponData.flame_at(tier)`). What it does: `docs/weapons.md`, Content, "The exhaust flamethrower (built in task T-042)". The flames are placeholders (orange and yellow squares); the final flame art comes from a separate art task.

**See it:** with the playtest switch on (below), start the game (F5), press Start and drift (W or S held with A or D). Screenshot, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/weapons/flamethrower_demo.tscn screenshots/flamethrower.png 90` (a run from the main menu, the car drifting in a circle with the flames behind it). Copies in `art-archive/T-042/screenshots/`.

**See the hit area (T-267):** `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/weapons/flame_hit_area_shot.tscn out.png 160` (the car standing, the drift held, a minion over the flame and one clear of it; the hit outline in green and the minions' circles in yellow are a debug draw in the fixture only) and `bash game/tools/record_flame_hit_area.sh out.gif 6` (a tier 1 drift through still minions; needs a window and Pillow). Fixture `tests/fixtures/weapons/flame_hit_area_demo.gd` (`mode` shot or gif). Results in `art-archive/T-267/screenshots/`.

**How it plugs in:** `scripts/weapons/flamethrower/exhaust_flamethrower.gd` extends `weapon.gd` (`weapon_id` `flamethrower`, `starts_owned` false) and is listed in `weapons.gd`'s `WEAPON_SCRIPTS`. The rack owns it (`run.weapons.flamethrower`, or `get_weapon("flamethrower")`), steps it once per physics step while it is owned (after the car has moved: the run's `PhysicsWorld` comes before `Weapons`), and resets it at every new run to tier 0, not owned (Weapons, Decisions). `run.weapons.upgrade("flamethrower")` gives it (0 to 1) and then applies its tiers, the call the level-up (T-043) will use; `set_tier(t)` sets any tier. `run.gd` has no flamethrower lines.

**Playtest switch:** `game/data/playtest.json`, key `"flamethrower_at_start"` (the file is a dictionary of independent switches). `true` makes the rack call `upgrade("flamethrower")` at the start of every run (`weapons.gd`, `reset_for_run()`), so the board can play it before the level-up offer exists; `false` is the game's rule (every run starts without it). Not player-facing. Tests override it with the script's static `playtest_override` (0 off, 1 on, -1 read the file).

**Each step while owned:** on while the car's drift state (`CarModel.is_drifting`) is on at `min_speed` or faster, then for `linger` seconds, or always from tier 8. The flame (drawn and hit area, below) starts each drift at `ramp_start_fraction` of full size and grows over `ramp_seconds` (`size_fraction()`, `drawn_scale()`). An entering enemy takes one hit at once and flashes (its `sprite`'s `modulate` = the data's `burn_flash_tint` for `burn_flash_seconds`); every `tick_interval` every enemy inside takes one hit: `take_damage(stats.damage)`, signal `enemy_hit(enemy, amount)`. Tier 7 multiplies the car's `tuning.turn_speed` (a new run's car starts at its own speed). Values: `tier`, `stats`, `active`, `hits`, `is_burning(enemy)`.

**Targets:** `rack.living_enemies()`. **Hit area = the drawn flame (T-267; Weapons, Decisions, "A smaller exhaust flame that burns what it touches"):** `hit_polygon()` is the outline of the flame as drawn now: `ExhaustFlameDrawing.cell_hull(cell)` (the convex hull of the opaque pixels of the sheet cell being drawn, made once per cell from the sheet and cached) at `drawn_scale()` (`size_multiplier` times the ramp, the same number the drawing's `scale` gets), from `exhaust_units()`, taken back through the isometric projection into the flat world. `current_cell()` is the cell the drawing shows (the direction from the car's heading, the flicker frame from the drawing). An enemy is hit (`is_inside()`) when its body circle (`rack.radius_of(e)`: a minion's 0.5 units, the kaiju's `hit_radius_units()`) touches the outline (`overlaps_circle()`; `contains_point()` for a point), so a minion's edge counts, not only its centre. Height is ignored as before: the area is on the flat world at the car's map position (the drawing is lifted). **Why the area and the art differed before:** the area was a rectangle from four numbers in the data (`car_half_size`, `inner_edge_multiplier`, `depth_multiplier`, `half_width_multiplier`, now removed from `weapons.json` and from `flame_at()`), 3.25 units deep and 2.6 wide at full size, in the flat world and starting 0.175 behind the car's centre; the drawing was the 95-pixel plume at the same size factor but unrelated to it: about twice the rectangle's depth on the screen, starting 0.42 behind the centre, and a teardrop in a rectangle; and the minions counted by their centre only. **Size:** `base_size_multiplier` 0.625 (a quarter of 2.5), tiers 2, 4 and 6 times 1.2, 1.2, 1.3. **Start point:** `EXHAUST_OFFSET_UNITS` 1.2 (was 0.42, under the car's body; a quarter-size flame there would be hidden by the car), a proposal for the playtest.

**The drawing (T-071, final art):** `scripts/weapons/flamethrower/exhaust_flame_drawing.gd` (a `Node2D` on `scenes/weapons/flamethrower/exhaust_flame.tscn`, node `ExhaustFlame`; it replaced the particle placeholder), made the first time the flamethrower is owned and kept in the run's `View`, with `z_index` -1 (`DRAW_Z_BEHIND`, T-267: the car and the enemies not covered by a building sit at the same depth, so a flame at the car's depth drew over the enemies that came before the `View` in the tree; one step lower it is behind the car and them, still over the ground, rooftops and buildings, whose depths are at least 2 steps lower), at 1.2 units behind the car's centre (`EXHAUST_OFFSET_UNITS`) and, since T-206, lifted by the car's height. The flamethrower still sets its `position`, `rotation` (the screen angle pointing back), `scale` (size multiplier times the ramp) and `emitting` (on while the flame is on) each step. The node draws the nearest of 32 pre-turned flames from `art/effects/exhaust_flame_sheet.png` (2 flicker frames, 224 x 224 cells, base at the cell centre, drawn at 0.5) and counters its own rotation, so the engine never rotates the pixels; the flicker changes every 0.08 s. The sheet is built by `tools/build_weapon_effects.py` from the one PixelLab generation `art-archive/T-071/exhaust_flame_raw.png` (tailpipe erased by script). Clear of the heart's red. The drift smoke stays on with the flamethrower (board, 2026-10-14).

**Weapon effects art (T-071):** the starting gun's bullet is `art/effects/bullet.png` (a cyan neon ball with a white-hot core, 2 pulse frames of 12 x 12, drawn by script with no PixelLab call; `bullet.gd` draws it at 0.5 and changes frame every 0.4 units flown; the hit circle is unchanged). Burning enemies get `scripts/enemies/burn_flames.gd` (`BurnFlames`): a weapon calls `BurnFlames.add_source(enemy, id)` when it burns the enemy and `remove_source` when it stops (the flamethrower's burn flash, id `"flamethrower"`; each molotov fire the enemy stands in, id the fire's instance id). The flames are a child of the enemy's `sprite`, drawn from `art/effects/burning_flames.png` (one PixelLab generation, `art-archive/T-071/burning_raw.png`, logs cut off by script, 2 mirrored frames of 48 x 54 flickering every 0.12 s), one set per enemy whatever the sources, bigger in whole steps for a bigger body (the kaiju). Drawing only: damage, tint and movement are unchanged. All three sheets and their records are made by `python game/tools/build_weapon_effects.py build` (`check` only runs the rules: alpha 0 or 255, at most 30 colours, clear of the heart's red; tests `tools/test_build_weapon_effects.py` and `tests/unit/enemies/test_burn_flames.gd`). Look at it: `T071_FRAMES=<folder> bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/weapons/weapon_effects_demo.tscn out.png 560` (it also saves every 4th frame to the folder); pictures and a GIF are in `art-archive/T-071/screenshots/`.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/weapons/test_exhaust_flamethrower.gd` (part of the GUT suite; test enemies `tests/fixtures/weapons/fake_enemy.gd` and `fake_big_enemy.gd`): on only while drifting and off after the linger, the minimum speed, the data's damage on entering and per tick inside and none outside, a big enemy burnt when its body overlaps the flames, the ramp, the same size on the screen in every direction, killing a real minion, the hit outline equal to the drawn flame through the ramp, 8 directions and 5 tiers (T-267), every drawn pixel inside it, a minion touching with its edge burnt, tier 1 a quarter of the old size, the depth behind the car and a minion, the burn flash, nothing while paused, every tier's values and effects (size, damage, flash, steering, always on), a new run without it, the playtest switch, its place in the rack, the drawing's place and colours. `test_starting_gun.gd` turns the playtest switch off, so it checks the game's rule.

### The neon underglow

Task T-168 (Weapon Behavior Agent): the neon underglow as Weapons, Decisions describes it, with T-166's numbers (`data/weapons.json` "underglow" through `WeaponData.underglow_at(tier)`). What it does: `docs/weapons.md`, Content, "The neon underglow (built in task T-168)". The zone is drawn in code (T-274, "The drawing"); the T-175 sprite is kept with a switch back to it.

**See it:** open `tests/fixtures/weapons/underglow_demo.tscn` and press F6: a run from the main menu with the underglow given and four minions just inside the ring; after 2 seconds the keyboard drives the car. Or set `underglow_at_start` to `true` in `data/playtest.json` and start the game (F5). Screenshot, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/weapons/underglow_demo.tscn screenshots/underglow.png 40`.

**How it plugs in:** `scripts/weapons/neon_underglow.gd` extends `weapon.gd` (`weapon_id` `underglow`, `starts_owned` false) and is listed in `weapons.gd`'s `WEAPON_SCRIPTS`; `run.weapons.underglow` (or `get_weapon("underglow")`). A new run resets it to tier 0; `run.weapons.upgrade("underglow")` gives it and applies its tiers; `set_tier(t)` sets any tier. `stats` is `underglow_at(tier)` with `damage` times `rack.damage_multiplier()` (re-applied by `set_damage_bonus()`). Offered at a level-up as `get_underglow` / `upgrade_underglow` (T-172, "The level-up offers").

**Playtest switch:** `data/playtest.json`, `"underglow_at_start"` (false, the game's rule). `true` makes the rack call `upgrade("underglow")` at the start of every run (`weapons.gd`, `reset_for_run()`). Tests override it with the script's static `playtest_override` (0 off, 1 on, -1 read the file).

**Each step while owned:** the targets are `rack.living_enemies()`; `is_inside(enemy)` is centre within `radius()` of the car's centre (`contains_point()`), or, for an enemy with `hit_radius_units()` (the kaiju), its body circle overlapping the ring's circle. Each enemy has its own clock (`since_hit`, by instance id): an enemy inside is hit (`take_damage(stats.damage)`, signal `enemy_hit(enemy, amount)`) when it has not been hit in the last `tick_interval` seconds, so it is hit on the step it enters and every interval after; leaving and coming back within its interval gives no extra hit. A clock is dropped once the enemy is outside and its interval is over. `hits` counts the hits this run.

**The drawing (T-274):** the zone is drawn in code, not from the sprite. `scripts/weapons/underglow_glow.gd` (node `NeonUnderglow`, made the first time the underglow is owned) is the first child of the run's `View` (under the car sprite, the drift smoke and the flames), placed on the car each step at the car's height (T-225) and hidden while not owned. It marks the damage circle through the isometric transform (an ellipse about twice as wide as tall, flat radius `radius x 16` px) as an image built pixel by pixel, opaque pixels or nothing: a bright pink ring (light core line, hot pink line inside it) just inside the edge, a stepped purple halo outside it (a solid band, then a checker-dithered one) and a purple floor glow inside it (half-dithered, then sparse). The halo and floor glow breathe out and in in steps (the pulse, 2 pulses in the 2 s cycle) while a lighter arc travels once round the ring in the cycle (the shimmer); the animation runs in `_process`, so it stops while paused. The images are built once per radius and frame (24 frames at 12 per second) and reused. **Every colour, width and speed is in `scenes/weapons/neon_underglow_look.tres`** (script `scripts/weapons/underglow_look.gd`): edit it in the Godot inspector to retune; clear of the heart's red. **Switch back to the T-175 sprite:** set `use_sprite` on that resource (the sprite `art/weapons/underglow.png` is kept). Screenshot fixtures: `tests/fixtures/run_underglow_look_{street,roof}_{t1,t8}.tscn`.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/weapons/test_neon_underglow.gd`: not owned in a new run and given by `upgrade()`; the entering hit on that step and one per tick interval with the data's damage times the garage multiplier; nothing outside; leaving and coming back within the interval; separate clocks per enemy; a big enemy hit by body overlap and a minion by centre; a real minion damaged; nothing while paused or after the run ends; a new run removes it; the playtest switch; every tier's values; one test per tier 2 to 8 checking the change from weapons.json's tier list and playing it; the damage bonus on every tier; the ring under the car sprite, following the car, the isometric shape, its size with the tiers, and its colours (opaque, not the heart's red).

### The molotovs

Task T-169 (Weapon Behavior Agent): the molotovs as Weapons, Decisions describes them, with T-166's numbers (`data/weapons.json` "molotov" through `WeaponData.molotov_at(tier)`). What they do: `docs/weapons.md`, Content, "The molotovs (built in task T-169)". The bottle and the fire are placeholders drawn in code; the final art comes from a separate art task.

**See it:** open `tests/fixtures/weapons/molotov_demo.tscn` and press F6: a run from the main menu with the molotovs given, three bottles thrown at once, two minions dropped into each of the first three fire areas and a ring of minions walking in; after 2 seconds the keyboard drives the car. Or set `molotov_at_start` to `true` in `data/playtest.json` and start the game (F5). Screenshot, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/weapons/molotov_demo.tscn screenshots/molotov.png 125`. A copy is in `art-archive/T-169/screenshots/`.

**How it plugs in:** `scripts/weapons/molotovs.gd` extends `weapon.gd` (`weapon_id` `molotov`, `starts_owned` false) and is listed in `weapons.gd`'s `WEAPON_SCRIPTS`; `run.weapons.molotov` (or `get_weapon("molotov")`). A new run resets it to tier 0; `run.weapons.upgrade("molotov")` gives it and applies its tiers; `set_tier(t)` sets any tier. `stats` is `molotov_at(tier)` with `damage` times `rack.damage_multiplier()`. Offered at a level-up as `get_molotov` / `upgrade_molotov` (T-172, "The level-up offers").

**Playtest switch:** `data/playtest.json`, `"molotov_at_start"` (false, the game's rule). `true` makes the rack call `upgrade("molotov")` at the start of every run. Tests override it with the script's static `playtest_override` (0 off, 1 on, -1 read the file).

**Throwing:** `throw_clock` counts physics time while owned; at `throw_interval()` it goes back to 0 and `throw_volley()` throws `molotovs_per_throw` bottles, each to `random_landing()`: a uniform angle and a distance uniform between `throw_distance_min` and `throw_distance_max` from the car's centre, from the weapon's own `rng` (a `RandomNumberGenerator`; tests set `rng.seed`, and a new run does not re-seed it). The run has no shared random generator, so the weapon keeps its own, as the minion spawner and the kaiju do. `throw_to(landing_units)` throws one bottle by hand. Signals `thrown(bottle, landing)` and `fire_started(fire)`; counters `throws`, `bottles`, `fires`.

**The bottle** (`MolotovBottle`, inner class of `molotovs.gd`, a rack projectile in `rack.draw_root`): flies from the car's centre to its landing spot in `FLIGHT_SECONDS` (0.5 s, in the code: a look and feel value), drawn at its place in the isometric view raised by an arc (`height_units()`: from 0.5 units down to the ground plus a 1.5-unit peak), spinning, with the minions' depth rule. When it lands it calls the weapon's `land(centre)`, which makes the fire with the tier's values at that moment.

**The fire area** (`scripts/weapons/molotov_fire.gd`, a rack projectile): `centre`, `radius`, `duration`, `damage`, `tick_interval`, `age`. Each step it ages and, while `age` is within `duration`, hits the living enemies inside, each with its own clock as the underglow does (`since_hit`): hit on the step it enters, then every `tick_interval` while inside; nothing once it has left. `is_inside(enemy)`: centre within `radius` (`contains_point()`), or, with `hit_radius_units()` (the kaiju), its body circle overlapping. Signal `enemy_hit(enemy, amount)`, counter `hits`. Done just after `duration`. It is drawn on the ground: `rack.add_projectile(fire, parent)` (the rack's new optional `parent`) puts it in a node `MolotovFires` in the map's `view_root`, right after `DriftMarks`, so the map's isometric transform makes its flat circle the ground ellipse and the buildings, enemies and car are drawn over it. It is still stepped, freed when done and removed at a new run like any projectile.

**Look (placeholders):** bottle: green glass, pale rag, yellow flame, dark outline (`BOTTLE_*`). Fire: dark rim, orange disc, yellow middle, pale core, six yellow flickers that move every 0.1 s (`FIRE_*`). All opaque, hard-edged, clear of the heart's red.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/weapons/test_molotovs.gd`: not owned in a new run and given by `upgrade()`; a seeded throw at the interval, landing within the data's distances and leaving a fire of the data's radius and duration where it landed, and the next throw one interval later; 200 random spots within the distances and all round the car; the same seed giving the same spots; an enemy inside hit on entering and per tick with the data's damage times the garage multiplier, one outside never; one that leaves taking no more; a big enemy by body overlap; a real minion damaged; a fire going out after its duration; fires, bottles and throws stopping while paused and after the run ends; a new run removing fires, bottles and the molotovs; the playtest switch; every tier's values; one test per tier 2 to 8 checking the change from weapons.json's tier list and playing it; the damage bonus on every tier; the bottle in the draw root and arcing, the fire in `MolotovFires` after the drift marks; and the placeholder colours (opaque, not the heart's red).

### The windshield wiper

Task T-170 (Weapon Behavior Agent): the windshield wiper as Weapons, Decisions describes it, with T-166's numbers (`data/weapons.json` "wiper" through `WeaponData.wiper_at(tier)`).
