What it does: `docs/weapons.md`, Content, "The windshield wiper (built in task T-170)". The blade is a placeholder drawn in code; the final art comes from a separate art task. **It is the only weapon that pushes enemies.**

**See it:** open `tests/fixtures/weapons/wiper_demo.tscn` and press F6: a run from the main menu with the wiper given and four minions across the semicircle in front of the car, pushed away as the blade reaches them; after 2 seconds the keyboard drives the car. Or set `wiper_at_start` to `true` in `data/playtest.json` and start the game (F5). Screenshot, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/weapons/wiper_demo.tscn screenshots/wiper.png 70`.

**How it plugs in:** `scripts/weapons/windshield_wiper.gd` extends `weapon.gd` (`weapon_id` `wiper`, `starts_owned` false) and is listed in `weapons.gd`'s `WEAPON_SCRIPTS`; `run.weapons.wiper` (or `get_weapon("wiper")`). A new run resets it to tier 0 (and the arm to its start); `run.weapons.upgrade("wiper")` gives it and applies its tiers; `set_tier(t)` sets any tier. `stats` is `wiper_at(tier)` with `damage` times `rack.damage_multiplier()`. Offered at a level-up as `get_wiper` / `upgrade_wiper` (T-172, "The level-up offers").

**Playtest switch:** `data/playtest.json`, `"wiper_at_start"` (false, the game's rule). `true` makes the rack call `upgrade("wiper")` at the start of every run. Tests override it with the script's static `playtest_override` (0 off, 1 on, -1 read the file).

**The arm:** it turns round `pivot_units()`, `PIVOT_OFFSET_UNITS` (0.5, the nose of the car's 1-unit body) ahead of the car's centre, and reaches `length()` units from it. `sweep_angle` is its angle in degrees from the car's facing (`forward()`, from `CarModel.rotation`), between `-sweep_arc_degrees / 2` and `+sweep_arc_degrees / 2`; it moves `sweep_degrees_per_second` each second in `sweep_dir` (+1 or -1) and turns back at each end, which starts a new pass (`pass_index`). A run gets it at the -90 end. `arm_direction()` and `tip_units()` give the arm in the world, so the semicircle turns with the car.

**Hits:** each step, the arm's swept wedge (from its old angle to its new one, out to `length()`) is tested against `rack.living_enemies()`; an enemy is hit when its body (`rack.radius_of()`) touches the wedge or the arm at either end of the step. One enemy is hit at most once per pass and never twice within `hit_cooldown` seconds (`last_hit`, by instance id: [pass, seconds since]). A hit is `take_damage(stats.damage)`, then, when `pushes(enemy)` is true, `enemy.apply_knockback(away, knockback_distance)` with `away` from the car's centre to the enemy's (the minions' call, T-167); signal `enemy_hit(enemy, amount, pushed)`, counter `hits`. `pushes()` is true only for an enemy with `apply_knockback()` that is not in the group `kaiju` (unless the data's `knockback_kaiju` is true): the real kaiju has no knockback call at all.

**The drawing:** `WiperDrawing` (inner class, node `WindshieldWiper`), made the first time the wiper is owned, in the run's `View`, placed on the pivot each step with `arm_px` the arm through the isometric transform; hidden while not owned. It sits just over the car sprite while the arm's middle is lower on screen than the car's centre (`drawn_over_car()`) and just under it otherwise. It draws a dark outline, a dark steel arm and a pale grey rubber blade on its outer 80%, and a small pivot square (`ARM_COLOUR`, `BLADE_COLOUR`, `OUTLINE_COLOUR`; opaque, hard-edged, clear of the heart's red).

**Tests:** `tests/unit/weapons/test_windshield_wiper.gd` (test minion `tests/fixtures/weapons/fake_minion.gd`, which records every knockback call): not owned in a new run and given by `upgrade()`; the arm sweeping back and forth between -90 and +90 degrees at the data's speed, always in front of the car; the semicircle turning with the car; a minion hit for the data's damage times the garage multiplier and pushed the data's distance straight away from the car; a real minion damaged and knocked back; a big enemy, a kaiju stand-in with a knockback call and the real kaiju damaged but not moved; once per pass and never within the cooldown; nothing out of reach or behind the car; nothing while paused or after the run ends; a new run removes it; the playtest switch; every tier's values; one test per tier 2 to 8 checking the change from weapons.json's tier list and playing it; the damage bonus on every tier; the blade on the pivot out to the tip, following the car, growing with the tiers, over or under the car by its direction; and its colours. `test_starting_gun.gd` and `test_exhaust_flamethrower.gd` each check that their weapon never moves an enemy (a real minion and a recording one).

### Size upgrades grow

Task T-286 (Weapon Behavior Agent). What it does and why these numbers: `docs/weapons.md`, Content, "Size upgrades grow on screen (built in task T-286)". `scripts/weapons/size_grow.gd` (`SizeGrow`, a RefCounted) holds the whole curve: `DURATION` 0.4 s and `OVERSHOOT` 3.0 (an ease-out-back like `LikesSpring`, T-273), `value_at(from, to, t)`, and one instance per size weapon (`grow`) with `value` (the size now), `target` (the data's), `active`, `grow_to(size)`, `snap(size)` and `advance(dt)`. Each weapon calls `grow.advance(dt)` first in its physics `step`, so the grow stands still while paused, and reads its size from `grow.value`: the wiper's `length()` (and `base_length()`, the art's 2.5, so the sheet is stretched by `length() / base_length()` through `WiperDrawing.set_arm(v, k)`), the underglow's `radius()` (the drawn ring and `contains_point` both use it) and the flame's `drawn_scale()` (`grow.value * size_fraction()`, used by both the drawing's scale and `hit_polygon()`). In `apply_tier()` a weapon calls `grow.grow_to(new size)` when `is_upgrade_step()` and `grow.snap(...)` otherwise. `weapon.gd` sets `_via_upgrade` only inside `upgrade()` (the call the level-up and gift pack make), so only a picked upgrade of an owned weapon grows; `set_tier()` called directly (a new run, tests), getting a weapon (tier 0 to 1) and a tier that keeps the size snap. `stats["length"]`, `["radius"]` and `["size_multiplier"]` stay the data's values. The molotovs have no grow (each fire area is made at the size of its bottle). Tests: `tests/unit/weapons/test_size_grow.gd`. Recording fixture `tests/fixtures/weapons/size_grow_demo.tscn` (the environment variable `SIZE_GROW_WEAPON` picks wiper, underglow or flamethrower): `SIZE_GROW_WEAPON=wiper "$GODOT_BIN" --path game --write-movie C:/abs/m/f.png --fixed-fps 30 --quit-after 150 res://tests/fixtures/weapons/size_grow_demo.tscn`, then `python game/tools/frames_to_gif.py C:/abs/m out.gif --fps 15 --skip 2 --crop 0.7 --shared --colors 64`. GIFs: `art-archive/T-286/screenshots/`.

### The level-up offers

Task T-043 (Weapon Behavior Agent): which weapon choices a level-up gives and applying the one taken. What they do: `docs/weapons.md`, Content, "The level-up offers". Code: `scripts/weapons/level_up_offers.gd`, made by the rack in `bind()` as `run.weapons.offers` (node `LevelUpOffers`). It listens to `RunState.level_up_choice_started`, which fires when the car drives over the gift pack's package (T-144, `RunState.open_choice()`; see "The gift pack drop"), or at once on a level-up with `state.pause_on_level_up` on (tests).

**For the UI Agent (T-052):**

- **Choice ids** (constants on the script, all in `ALL_IDS`): `"upgrade_gun"`, `"get_flamethrower"`, `"upgrade_flamethrower"`, and since T-172 `"get_underglow"`, `"upgrade_underglow"`, `"get_molotov"`, `"upgrade_molotov"`, `"get_wiper"`, `"upgrade_wiper"`: `get_<weapon>` while the car does not have the weapon, `upgrade_<weapon>` once it does, below tier 8. `LevelUpOffers.weapon_of(id)` gives the weapon id (`"upgrade_wiper"` → `"wiper"`). Show text by id (the three new weapons' card texts are a separate text and UI task; until then `card_text()` gives empty strings for them).
- **Signal** `run.weapons.offers.offers_ready(level: int, offers: Array)`: the choice has started (the run is already paused). `offers` has 1 or 2 different ids, in `ALL_IDS` order (the gun's first). Also readable at any time: `offers.current_offers` (empty when none waiting) and `offers.has_offers()`.
- **Function** `run.weapons.offers.choose(id: String) -> bool`: call it with the id taken. It applies the choice, emits `offer_taken(id)`, then calls `state.choice_made()` (which resumes the run, or starts the next waiting level-up's choice and a new `offers_ready`). Returns false and changes nothing for an id not on offer.
- **Nothing to offer** (every weapon at the last tier): the choice is skipped on its own, `state.choice_made()` is called at once and `offers_ready` is not emitted, so the screen never opens. The screen must not call `choice_made()` itself.

**The draw (T-172):** `available_offers()` lists every available id (in `ALL_IDS` order, changes nothing). `_draw()` gives the choice: `FIRST_OFFERS` (`upgrade_gun`, `get_flamethrower`) while `is_first_offer()` (no choice taken this run, `choices_taken == 0`, reset on `run_started`; the car owns only the gun; both available); otherwise the whole list when it has `OFFER_COUNT` (2) ids or fewer, else 2 different ids picked uniformly with the script's own `rng` (a `RandomNumberGenerator`, randomized once; tests set `offers.rng.seed`), returned in `ALL_IDS` order. `offers.compute_offers()` returns what the next choice will show without changing anything (it puts `rng.state` back). `choose(id)` calls `rack.upgrade(weapon_of(id))`.

**Playtest switches:** a run that starts with the flamethrower (`flamethrower_at_start`) is not a normal run: its first level-up is drawn, never offers `get_flamethrower`, and offers `upgrade_flamethrower` among the choices. `data/playtest.json` `"all_weapons_at_start"` (T-172; false, the game's rule; never visible to players) makes `weapons.gd`'s `reset_for_run()` give every weapon the car does not have yet at tier 1 (after the per-weapon switches, so a weapon is never given twice): all five weapons, and the level-ups offer only upgrades. Read by `weapons.gd`'s static `all_weapons_switch_on()`; tests override it with the static `all_weapons_override` (0 off, 1 on, -1 read the file).

**Tests:** `tests/unit/weapons/test_level_up_offers.gd`: the first offer over 40 seeds; later offers drawn; 40 seeded runs taken from level 1 until nothing is left (39 choices each), every offer two different available ids (one when one is left) and every one of the 9 ids offered; the same seed giving the same offers; `compute_offers()` matching the next draw and leaving the generator alone; the shown order; tier-8 weapons never offered; one choice left; nothing left skipping the choice; every id applied and the run resumed (the six new ids each); refused ids; `weapon_of()`; two level-ups at once; a new run bringing back the first offer; `flamethrower_at_start` leading to its upgrades; `all_weapons_at_start` on (all five at tier 1, with the flamethrower switch too), off, and false in the committed file. `test_level_up_screen.gd`'s two tests that needed a fixed later offer now max the other weapons or press whichever card is first.

### Level-up screen

T-310: the ribbon's `TitleLabel` is lettered in cream (`TITLE_CREAM`, RGB 246, 250, 232) with a dark outline (`TITLE_OUTLINE`, RGB 14, 22, 18, `TITLE_OUTLINE_SIZE` 4) so it reads on a dark green ribbon; card titles, descriptions and buttons keep black ink (`INK`).

Task T-052 (UI Agent). What the player sees and does: `docs/hud-and-menus.md`, "Level-up screen". `scripts/ui/level_up_screen.gd` (`LevelUpScreen`, a CanvasLayer at layer 15: over the HUD, under the pause screen) is a child of the run (`run.level_up_screen`, node `LevelUpScreen`), set up with the weapon rack. Since T-144 a level-up no longer opens it by itself: the car drives over the gift pack's package (see "The gift pack drop"), which calls `state.open_choice(level)` and pauses the tree (`run.gd` keeps `state.pause_on_level_up` off); the screen listens to `run.weapons.offers.offers_ready`, shows a card per id and calls `offers.choose(id)` for the one taken; it closes on `offer_taken` and on a new run. It keeps processing while the tree is paused.

- `cards`: one `{id, panel, body, highlight_frame, image, title, desc, button}` per offered id; `highlight` is the highlighted index. `card_text(id)` gives `{title, desc}`: every `upgrade_<weapon>` id uses that weapon's tier now + 1 (`upgrade_gun.tier4.*` for a gun at tier 3, `upgrade_wiper.tier5.*` for a wiper at tier 4; T-173 made this work for all five weapons through `rack.get_weapon(offers.weapon_of(id))`).
- Weapon image (T-173): each card's `WeaponImage` (`TextureRect`, 64 x 64, `STRETCH_KEEP_ASPECT_CENTERED`, nearest filtering) sits above the title in the card's `VBoxContainer`. `LevelUpScreen.weapon_image(weapon_id)` loads `res://art/level_up/weapon_<weapon id>.png` (`WEAPON_ART`; ids `gun`, `flamethrower`, `underglow`, `molotov`, `wiper`) when it exists, otherwise `placeholder_image(weapon_id)`: a 16 x 16 `ImageTexture` built (and cached) from `PLACEHOLDER_GRIDS` (one character per pixel, `.` transparent) and `PLACEHOLDER_COLORS` (opaque, clear of the heart's red). The art task (T-174) added the five PNGs under those names in `art/level_up/` (`weapon_gun.png`, `weapon_flamethrower.png`, `weapon_underglow.png`, `weapon_molotov.png`, `weapon_wiper.png`, 64 x 64, PixelLab, each with its record JSON; requests and the raw molotov in `art-archive/T-174/`), so the placeholders now only show for an id without a file; `test_final_weapon_art_replaces_the_placeholders` checks size, hard edges and transparent background. `CARD_SIZE` is 360 x 374 (74 px taller than T-141's for the image and its gap); the `HighlightFrame` is now a `NinePatchRect` (margin 10) of `highlight_frame.png` so it keeps its 6-art-pixel border on the taller card. `take(id)`, `take_highlighted()`, `move_highlight(step)`, `show_offers(ids)`, `close()`, `is_open()`.
- Text: `scripts/ui/level_up_texts.gd` (`LevelUpTexts.TEXTS`), T-051's table keyed as there (`levelup.title`, `levelup.take`, `get_flamethrower.title/.desc`, `<id>.tier<N>.title/.desc`; 32 rows, the two `weapon.*.name` rows are not used here). Generated from the table, not retyped. T-173 added T-171's 24 cards for `get_underglow`, `get_molotov`, `get_wiper` and their `upgrade_<weapon>.tier2` to `tier8` (80 entries in all); `python game/tools/check_level_up_texts.py [task file]` checks them word for word against T-171's result-notes table (tests: `tools/test_check_level_up_texts.py`).
- Input: `_input` takes only fresh key presses (`not echo`); keys down when the screen opens are remembered (`key_down` callable, `Input.is_physical_key_pressed` by default) and ignored until released. A/D or Left/Right, Enter/KP Enter/Space.
- Look: Bangers via `Hud.comic_font()` (smoothing off), `StyleBoxFlat` with `anti_aliasing = false`, white cards with black borders, the highlighted card a thick `RING` (the combo's yellow) border with a black outline (shadow).
- Escape: `pause_screen.gd` ignores Escape (`toggle()`) while `target.state.is_waiting_for_choice()`, so the level-up pause is never resumed or covered by the pause screen.
- Playtest switch: `data/playtest.json` `flamethrower_at_start` is `false` since this task, so a run starts with the gun only and the first level-up (100 likes, about 10 seconds of drifting at combo x1) offers "get the flamethrower" and the gun's tier 2.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_level_up_screen.gd` (open with 2 cards and T-051's text, next-tier text for gun and flamethrower, button and real mouse click, A/D and arrow keys, Enter and Space, key held at open and key-repeat, the highlight's ring, Escape, two waiting level-ups, a normal run's first offers, a new run, the comic look; T-173: every choice id and tier the offers can produce has a title and description, T-171's text word for word, a new weapon's next-tier text, each card's image node showing its weapon's image with a different image per weapon, placeholders opaque or transparent only and never the heart's red, every card's image, title, description and button laid out inside the card in that order without overlapping, and the highlight frame on the taller card). Screenshot: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_up_screen_demo.tscn screenshots/level_up_screen.png 120` (120 frames, so the build-up has ended); the fixture's `offer_ids` export shows chosen cards instead of the first offer: `level_up_screen_new_weapons_demo.tscn` (underglow and molotovs) and `level_up_screen_wiper_demo.tscn` (gun and wiper). Saved ones: `art-archive/T-052/screenshots/level_up_screen.png`, and with the weapon images `art-archive/T-173/screenshots/`.

The wait runs in real time, so take the shot late: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_up_from_menu_demo.tscn art-archive/<ticket>/screenshots/level_up_screen.png 400` (60 frames is too early and shows one card). **Check** `python game/tools/check_level_up_shot.py <png>` prints JSON: every pixel `is_old_brand_red` flags (count and the first 20 positions), the colour counts of the screenshot's pixels that are exactly an opaque colour of `header_ribbon.png`, `card_frame.png`, `highlight_frame.png` or `gift_pack_emblem.png` (without the cream fills and the black text ink), the most common of them and whether it is Greenbull's dark green (12, 76, 44). ### Level-up screen animation (T-141)

Task T-141 (UI Agent). `scripts/ui/level_up_screen.gd` now builds the screen from T-140's art (2 screen pixels per art pixel): the cards are a `NinePatchRect` (`card_frame.png`, margin 10) inside a `Body` control that slides, the highlighted card shows its `HighlightFrame` (`highlight_frame.png`, 12 px past the card), "Take it" is a `Button` with `StyleBoxTexture` plates, the ribbon (`header_ribbon.png`, 760 x 88) holds the title label between x 152 and 608 and the emblem hangs at its left end, and `Sunburst` (`sunburst.png`, 4x, alpha 0.75, spun 1.5 degrees every 0.1 s) is behind the cards. T-051's text is unchanged. Card text wraps with `AUTOWRAP_WORD_SMART` inside a text width of 300 px, and a test checks every word of every text fits a line, so words never break mid-word.

- **Animation:** one 0.04 s frame per step, every curve one value per frame (constants `DIM_STEPS`, `SUN_STEPS`, `RIBBON_DROP`, `CARD_SLIDE`, delays `RIBBON_DELAY` 0.05, `EMBLEM_DELAY` 0.12, `CARD_DELAY` 0.2, `CARD_STAGGER` 0.1). Length `animation_length(n)`: 0.54 s for two cards, 0.64 s for three. The screen's own `_process` runs it (the screen is `PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS`, so it plays while the run is paused); tests call `advance(delta)` and `skip_animation()`.
- **Input guard:** `is_animating()` is true until it ends. `take()` and `move_highlight()` do nothing then, `_input` uses up (and ignores) the take and move keys, and each card button ignores the mouse until the end. A key pressed during the build-up and still held at the end takes nothing until it is let go.
- **Likes bar overlap:** the screen uses only its own nodes and clock, so T-139's likes bar animation (another branch) cannot interfere; the screen sits at layer 15, above the HUD.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/test_level_up_screen.gd` (T-052's tests now play the build-up to its end first; new ones for the art, the title's place, word-boundary wrapping, the length and finished layout, stepped and ordered frames, the spin, the input guard for keys, mouse and a held key, input after it ends, a second screen animating again, closing mid-way). Screenshots: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_up_anim_demo.tscn out.png 12` shows the finished screen; for a moment in the build-up call Godot as `screenshot.gd` does and add `--at=<seconds>` after `--` (key frames saved in `art-archive/T-141/screenshots/`, `t_<seconds>.png`).

### The gift pack drop

Task T-144 (Game Loop Agent; Game loop architecture, Decisions, "Gift pack drop", with the board's five answers of 2026-10-16). A level-up no longer opens the level-up screen by itself. On every level-up (`RunState.level_up`, unchanged, so other level-up effects such as the burst and the likes bar keep firing at that moment) a helicopter flies across the screen and drops a package, which falls and lands a short way ahead of the car on open road. Driving over the landed package removes it and opens the level-up screen above for that package's level, with T-043's offers, pausing the run as before.

- **Code:** `scripts/run/gift_drops.gd` (`GiftDrops`, a pausable `Node2D`, `run.gift_drops`, node `GiftDrops`, drawn just before `View`). `bind(run)` is called by `run.gd`. It owns `helicopters` (in the air) and `packages` (carried, falling or landed, not yet picked up; `landed_packages()`). Signals: `helicopter_sent(helicopter)`, `package_dropped(package)`, `package_landed(package)`, `package_picked_up(package)`. Every frame `advance(delta)` moves the helicopters and falling packages, then `check_pickups()`. `pick_up(package)` removes it and calls `state.open_choice(package.level)`. A new run (`run_started`) clears everything (`clear()`); nothing moves or is picked up after the run ends, or while the tree is paused (pause screen, level-up screen). With `state.pause_on_level_up` on (tests only) no helicopter comes and the old direct flow runs. `land_all()` and `pick_up_all()` are shortcuts for tests and screenshot fixtures.
- **The flight:** `send_helicopter(level)` picks the landing spot (`landing_spot()`, below), then a path in the view's drawn pixels from `offscreen_margin_px` beyond the screen's left edge to the same beyond its right edge (`visible_rect()`, the camera's view at that moment). While it carries the package the helicopter flies `drop_height_px` above the landing spot (its centre), but never so high that its frame (`helicopter_top()`, read from its sprite) comes closer than `screen_margin_px` to the screen's top edge (`fly_y()`, worked out every frame, since the camera follows the car), so it is always fully on screen. The package hangs `hang_below_px` under the helicopter's centre (`carry_offset()`, its bottom; clear of the belly) until the helicopter is over the landing spot, then floats straight down under its parachute (T-146's falling look) at a steady speed for `fall_seconds` and lands. The fall starts where it hangs and is at least `min_fall_px` (starting a little higher, behind the helicopter, when it hangs too low), but never so high that the parachute's top (`art_height()`) is above the screen's top edge less `screen_margin_px`: zoomed in close, with the landing spot high on the screen, the fall is shorter, down to none, and the parachute still shows for `fall_seconds`. The helicopter flies on and is removed beyond the right edge. In the air (helicopter, carried and falling package) they are drawn over the whole world (`SKY_Z`); a landed package takes the depth order a car would have on that spot (`map.car_z_index`), under the car's drawing.
- **Where it lands (answer 2):** `land_ahead_units` ahead of the car, along the way it is moving (its nose when standing still), if that spot is open road: `map.is_open_spot(spot, clearance_units)` (new in `scripts/level/mvp_map.gd`: inside the map, no building or boulder within the clearance; `can_place_meteor_boulder()` now uses it, unchanged). Otherwise it tries other ways and distances, nearest to straight ahead first (`SEARCH_ANGLES`, `SEARCH_SHARES`, up to 1.5 times the distance), and falls back to the car's own spot.
- **Pickup:** a landed package is picked up when the car's centre comes within `pickup_radius_units` of the package's centre. Several packages (answer 3) each open their own screen; two picked up together show one screen after the other (`RunState.pending_choices`). Packages stay until picked up (answer 4).
- **Off-screen arrow (answer 5):** `scripts/run/gift_arrows.gd` (`GiftArrows`, a CanvasLayer at layer 11, over the HUD and under the level-up screen; `run.gift_drops.arrows`, node `GiftArrows`). For each landed package whose centre is off screen it draws one arrow (a pale cyan triangle with a dark outline, placeholder look, no red) on a track 12 px inside the screen's edges, where the line from the screen's centre to the package crosses it, pointing at the package, slid along the track until it is 8 px clear of the HUD's parts (the timer, the health column, the coins row, and the likes bar with its row, fire and combo counter). `shown` lists the arrows drawn; `GiftArrows.place(screen, target, avoid)` is the placement on its own.
- **Numbers** (`data/gift_pack.json` through `scripts/data/gift_pack_data.gd`, `GiftPackData`; proposals for the board's playtest, retuned for the final art in the playtest build): `helicopter.speed_px_per_second` 360, `helicopter.drop_height_px` 200, `helicopter.screen_margin_px` 12, `helicopter.offscreen_margin_px` 80, `package.hang_below_px` 80, `package.fall_seconds` 1.4, `package.min_fall_px` 60, `package.land_ahead_units` 8, `package.clearance_units` 1.0, `package.pickup_radius_units` 2.0. Drawn pixels are the isometric view's; units are the car's width.
- **Placeholder art and the art swap:** `scenes/run/gift_helicopter.tscn` (script `scripts/run/gift_helicopter.gd`, `GiftHelicopter`) and `scenes/run/gift_package.tscn` (script `scripts/run/gift_package.gd`, `GiftPackage`) each have one `Sprite2D` child named **`Sprite`** (`sprite()`), with nearest filtering on the root. **T-145 (helicopter)** replaced the `texture` of `GiftHelicopter/Sprite` with the final art, `res://art/gift_pack/helicopter_sheet.png` (4 rotor frames of 192 x 160 side by side, `hframes` 4, drawn at `scale` 0.5, facing right, the way it flies, then in the old sponsor's red with its old logo on the fuselage and the tail fin; the old 42 x 20 placeholder file stays for its test). The rotor spin is `GiftHelicopter._process` stepping `frame` at `ROTOR_FPS` (24, art only). Built by script from the board-approved PixelLab sample (1 generation, `art-archive/T-145/`): `python game/tools/build_helicopter.py build` (and `check`); Python tests `game/tools/test_build_helicopter.py`. **T-305 (goal G-006)** rebuilt the sheet in Greenbull's colours. The name, size and frames are unchanged, so `gift_helicopter.tscn` is untouched. The body is recoloured to a dark green ramp led by Greenbull's dark green (12, 76, 44), with light green trim lines. Greenbull's emblem (`art/logo/greenbull_emblem_32.png`, fitted to 18 px on the fuselage and 12 px on the tail fin, snapped to the emblem's own colours) is stamped on cream panels in place of the gull. Any pixel `style_rules.reads_as_red` flags is moved onto the green ramp. Commands: `build` writes the sheet and `helicopter_sheet.json`, then runs `check`; `check` tests 4 frames of 192 x 160, alpha 0/255 only, transparent corners, 30 colours or fewer per frame and on the whole sheet, and clear of the heart's red; `piece <png>` writes the sheet elsewhere (the style loop's step); `archive` copies the current sheet to its folder in `art-archive/T-305/`, where the old red sheet is kept. 0 PixelLab generations. **T-146 (package, done):** `GiftPackage/Sprite` now shows `res://art/gift_pack/package.png` (64 x 64, the box about 40 px wide, `offset` (0, -32): its bottom row stands on the landing spot) and, only while the package is falling, `res://art/gift_pack/package_falling.png` (64 x 96, the same box under a parachute, `offset` (0, -48)); `GiftPackage.refresh_look()` (called every frame) picks the texture from the phase, looks only. The box is T-140's gift box (board's choice, 2026-10-16, so the drop matches the level-up screen). **T-304 (goal G-006)** rebuilt both files in Greenbull's colours, with the same names, sizes and offsets: the box is recoloured by script with the level-up screen's mapping (`build_level_up_art.recolour_box`, T-303: a dark green box, light green ribbon), scaled down, with Greenbull's emblem (`art/logo/greenbull_emblem_32.png`, scaled to 16 px and snapped to the emblem's own colours) on a pale tag on its right face in place of the gull, and the parachute drawn by script in dark green and cream gores: `python game/tools/build_gift_package.py build` then `check` (alpha 0/255 only, transparent corner, 30 colours or fewer, clear of the heart's red; tests `game/tools/test_build_gift_package.py`); `... piece package|package_falling <png>` writes one piece (the style loop's step), `... archive` copies the current files to their folder in `art-archive/T-304/` (the old red versions are kept there). 0 PixelLab generations in either build; the crate sample (1 generation) is kept in `art-archive/T-146/`. The old `package_placeholder.png` stays in `game/art/gift_pack/`, unused. (Placeholder note, T-144: it was 16 x 16, offset (0, -8).) Change only the texture (and the sprite's `scale`/`offset` to fit the new size); how they move is in `gift_drops.gd`. The placeholders are drawn by a script (no PixelLab, 0 generations): `python game/tools/build_gift_placeholders.py build` (byte-identical every run) and `... check` (sizes, no semi-transparent pixels, no red); Python tests `game/tools/test_build_gift_placeholders.py`.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/run/test_gift_drops.gd`: a level-up sends the helicopter instead of opening the screen; it crosses the screen and drops a package that lands; the carried package and the straight fall; picking up removes it and opens the screen with T-043's offers, pausing the run; a real drive onto it; no pickup in the air; the five answers (the run keeps going, clock and likes drain, until the screen opens; ahead of the car on open road: its motion, a building, a boulder, 150 spots over the map; one package and one screen per level-up; packages that stay; the arrow only while off screen, one per package, clear of the HUD at four window sizes); the helicopter never above the screen's top; placeholders not red; one named sprite each; the numbers from the data file (also from a test file). `tests/unit/test_level_up_screen.gd` now opens the screen by picking up the package. Screenshots from the main menu: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/gift_drop_demo.tscn screenshots/gift_drop.png 250` (the helicopter crossing; frame counts depend on the screen's refresh rate) and `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/gift_drop_arrow_demo.tscn screenshots/gift_drop_arrow.png 40` (a landed package moved off screen, with its arrow). Variants of the first (same script, `gift_drop_demo.gd`): `gift_drop_down_demo.tscn` (the car heading down the screen), `gift_drop_default_zoom_demo.tscn` and `gift_drop_default_zoom_down_demo.tscn` (the run's default zoom instead of the saved camera); the playtest build's sequences are in `art-archive/T-144/screenshots/` (`sheet_*.png`).

### The pickup magnet (T-222)

`scripts/run/pickup_magnet.gd` (child "PickupMagnet" of the run, `run.pickup_magnet`): every physics step, coins (group `coins`), landed gift packs and health drops (group `health_pickups`) within `RunState.pickup_magnet_radius_units` of the car's centre fly straight at the car at `RunState.pickup_pull_speed` units per second. Both start from `data/run.json`'s `pickup_magnet` (`radius_units` 3.0, 1 car length: the car is drawn 3 units long; `pull_speed_units_per_second` 30, faster than the car's top speed so a pickup always catches up) through `RunData`, and are set again at every `RunState.start_run()`; the garage upgrade (T-224) sets the radius there: `RunState.start_run()` takes it from the `pickup_magnet` upgrade's bought level (`garage.json`, base 3.0 at level 0; +0.6 units per level to 9.0 at level 10; key `pickup_magnet`, text "Brand Magnet" in `garage_screen.gd`). Test: `tests/unit/run/test_magnet_upgrade.gd`. Picking up is unchanged (the coin's Area2D, the package's pickup radius); a pulled coin or drop is also collected once within 0.5 units of the car's centre. The reach is measured on the flat map (proposal: it ignores heights), and falling gift packs are not pulled, only landed ones. Test: `tests/unit/run/test_pickup_magnet.gd`; GIF fixture `tests/fixtures/pickup_magnet_gif_demo.tscn` (GIF in `art-archive/T-222/screenshots/`).

## The kaiju

Task T-045 (Enemy Behavior Agent): Crash City's final boss as Enemies, Decisions describes it, with T-039's numbers (`kaiju` and, T-119, `kaiju_coins` in `data/enemies.json` through `EnemyData`). What it does: `docs/enemies.md`, Content, "The kaiju". A placeholder for now (a large dark slate green Godzilla-style shape drawn in code); the final art comes from a separate art task. Its meteor attack (T-046): "The meteor attack" below.

**See it:** start the game (F5) and press Start, or open `scenes/run/run.tscn` and press F6. With the playtest switch on (below) the kaiju walks in from a screen edge 30 seconds into the run; the starting gun (and the flamethrower, with T-042's `flamethrower_at_start` on) can kill it, which wins the run. Screenshot, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/kaiju_demo.tscn screenshots/kaiju.png 20` (the kaiju beside the car in the gravel square, with a few minions, in a run started from the main menu; fixture script `tests/fixtures/kaiju_demo.gd`). A copy is in `art-archive/T-045/screenshots/`.

**Playtest switch: early arrival.** The boss time is 7 minutes in, so for playtests `data/playtest.json` (its other keys are other tasks' switches) can bring the kaiju's arrival forward:

```
"kaiju_early_arrival": { "enabled": true, "after_seconds": 30 }
```

With `enabled` true, the kaiju arrives `after_seconds` into the run instead of at the boss time. **To switch it back, set `"enabled": false`**: the kaiju then arrives at the boss time (7 minutes in, 1:00 left) as Enemies and Game loop architecture decide. A missing file or key is off too. Only the kaiju's arrival moves: the clock, the minions' growth and the `boss_time` signal stay as they are, and a later `boss_time` brings no second kaiju. It is on (30 s) for the board's playtest of T-045 and is set back to `false` right before T-045 merges (board, 2026-10-14). `KaijuBoss.read_early_arrival_seconds()` (in `kaiju_boss.gd`) reads it into `run.kaiju_boss.early_arrival_seconds` (-1 = off) when the run is made. Tests set the static `playtest_override` in `kaiju_boss.gd` (-1 = off, seconds = on, -2 = read the file), as T-042's tests do for the flamethrower.

**How it is built** (`scripts/enemies/`):

- `kaiju_boss.gd`: the run's kaiju. The run scene adds it as `run.kaiju_boss` (child `Kaiju`) and calls `bind(run)`. On the state's `boss_time` it makes the kaiju appear (`try_appear()`; once per run). It moves everything in `step(dt)`, once per physics step from `_physics_process` (pausable, so the pause screen and the level-up pause stop the kaiju and its damage), and does nothing once the run is over. On `run.world_rebuilt` (a new run) it removes the kaiju; it arrives again at that run's boss time. `enabled = false` stops it (tests).
- `kaiju.gd`: the kaiju, a `CharacterBody2D` in the run's `PhysicsWorld` (flat world pixels, 16 per unit) on layer 3 (`enemies`) with mask 1 (obstacles): a circle of `RADIUS_UNITS` (2.0, so 4 units across). It slides along walls, buildings and boulders and walks round one it meets head-on, as a minion does; it never pushes or blocks the car. In the groups `enemies` and `kaiju` (not `minions`).
- `kaiju_sprite.gd`: the kaiju's drawing in the isometric view, `HEIGHT_UNITS` (7) tall. **Final art (T-069):** 5 walk sheets in `art/kaiju/` (`kaiju_s/se/e/ne/n.png`, 6 frames of 224 x 224 side by side, 30 colours at most per sheet; south-west, west and north-west mirror south-east, east and north-east), drawn at scale 0.5 (so the 224-pixel canvas is the 7 units), the direction chosen from the way it walks (`compass_index`, as the minion's), the frame from the walk cycle (`frame_for`), feet on the ground point (`ART_FEET_Y`), the ground shadow kept. `drawn_height_px()` is the art's visible height (head top `ART_TOP_Y` to feet) so the health bar sits on its head; `drawn_width_px()` is unchanged. Without the sheets it draws the T-045 placeholder (left or right facing). The sheets: `python game/tools/build_kaiju_sheets.py` (free; from the 6 first frames of each `art-archive/T-069/walk_<dir>/`, one shared palette per sheet by `reduce_palette.py`), tests `tools/test_build_kaiju_sheets.py` and `tests/unit/enemies/test_kaiju_sprite.gd`. PixelLab record, review pictures and the one-round notes: `art-archive/T-069/`; the raw 8 rotations are there too. Screenshots and a GIF of it walking in a run (`tests/fixtures/kaiju_walk_demo.tscn`, `KAIJU_REC_DIR=<folder>` saves a picture every 4 frames): `art-archive/T-069/screenshots/`.

**Arrival:** at a random point on `arrival_ring()`, the visible screen (the camera's view) grown so that the whole drawing, its ground circle and its height, is `OFFSCREEN_MARGIN_UNITS` (1) beyond the screen's edge, inside the map and clear of buildings and boulders (`is_free_spot()`; 24 tries a step, else it tries again on the next step). `appear_at(units)` puts it at a given place (tests, fixtures).

**Movement:** straight towards the car's centre at `move_speed` (3 units/s).

**Contact damage:** as a minion's: it touches the car when its circle overlaps the car's 1 x 1 unit body (`touches_car()`); while touching, it hits once every `contact_damage_interval` (1 s), the first hit at once: `RunState.damage_car(contact_damage)` (20; this also cuts the combo) and `car.apply_knockback(away from the kaiju, knockback)` (16 units/s).

**Damaging the kaiju (for the weapon tasks, T-041 and T-042):** exactly as a minion, so a weapon that finds minions finds the kaiju too: `take_damage(amount: float)` on the kaiju node, the same points as `health` (500). It can be damaged at any time, as soon as it has appeared (no shield, no vulnerability windows). Zero or negative amounts, and calls on a dead kaiju, are ignored. It is in the `enemies` group and its body on layer 3, so `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group("enemies")` and a projectile's `Area2D` with a mask of 4 find it; it has `position_units()`, `health`, `max_health` and `is_dead()` like a minion. It is much bigger than a minion, so it also has `hit_radius_units()`, its body on the ground as a radius in units (`RADIUS_UNITS`, 2.0, its collision circle; the minion's body is 0.5). The weapons read it ("Weapons", Targets): the gun's bullets hit when they come within their hit radius of its body, and the flamethrower counts any overlap of the flames with its body as a hit (board, 2026-10-14; minions keep the centre rule). Check `is_dead()` before damaging one found earlier in the same frame.

**Health, for the HUD:** `run.kaiju_boss.kaiju` is the live kaiju (null before it appears and after it dies). The boss relays everything a HUD needs, so it can connect once:

| Signal or function | Is |
| --- | --- |
| `kaiju_appeared(kaiju: Node)` | The kaiju appeared (just off-screen) |
| `kaiju_health_changed(health: float, max_health: float)` | Once when it appears (full health), then on every hit |
| `kaiju_defeated(coins: int)` | It died, with the coins it added |
| `kaiju_health()`, `kaiju_max_health()`, `has_appeared()` | Its health now (0 when there is none), its full health, and whether it has appeared this run |

The kaiju node itself sends `health_changed(health, max_health)` on every hit and `died(kaiju, position_units)` once.

**Death:** at 0 health the kaiju dies once: `died` fires, it leaves the groups `enemies` and `kaiju` at once, its drawing goes, and the node is freed at the end of the frame. The boss then adds its coins, `EnemyData.kaiju_coins_on_defeat()` (10), through `RunState.add_coins()`, **before** calling `RunState.kaiju_killed()`, which ends the run as a win; so `run_ended("win", coins)` carries the kaiju's coins and they are saved with the run.

**Draw order:** the kaiju's drawing is in the minions' draw node (`run.minions.draw_root`, the Y-sorted `Enemies` node just before the car's `View`), placed every physics step on its body in the isometric view with a `z_index` from `enemy_depth.gd` (the map's rule for the car, as for the minions, with the kaiju's size).

**Not in the data:** the kaiju's size (4 units across, 7 tall) and the off-screen margin are in the code: sizes and rules of the placeholder, not balancing numbers.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/enemies/test_kaiju.gd`, part of the GUT suite: no kaiju before boss time; boss time bringing it just off-screen, clear of obstacles, walking towards the car at the data's speed, once per run; walking round a building; weapon damage through `take_damage()` lowering its health as soon as it appears, with both health signals; a projectile-style physics query on the enemies mask finding it; damage to 0 adding its coins before the run ends as a win, `kaiju_killed()` called once; contact damage at the data's rate and knockback away from it; no damage without touching; standing still while paused and after the run ends; a new run removing it and boss time bringing it back; the collision layers; the draw order; the placeholder's size and clear of the heart's red; `hit_radius_units()` matching its body; the starting gun hitting it, the flamethrower burning it when only its body overlaps the flames, and the gun killing it winning the run with its coins; the playtest switch (arrival at its time, once, again in a new run), reading its key beside other keys, and the committed file keeping both switches.

**The kaiju's health bar (task T-050):** `scripts/hud/kaiju_health_bar.gd` (`KaijuHealthBar`), `run.kaiju_health_bar`, a child of the world view like the trick popups. `bind(boss)` follows `kaiju_appeared` (shows), `kaiju_health_changed` (`fraction`, 0 to 1) and `kaiju_defeated` (hides); each physics step (priority after the boss's) it sits `GAP` above the kaiju sprite's head and takes its width from the sprite's `drawn_width_px()` and `drawn_height_px()`, so the final kaiju art only has to provide those two functions (the placeholder's width is its ground circle, 90 px). It hides when there is no kaiju (a new run). Neon purple `FILL_COLOR` on white, black outline (`OUTLINE` 2 px, stepped corners), a black 1 px divider at each of `DIVISIONS` (10) tenths, `BAR_HEIGHT` 8 px, whole pixels, no text. Absolute depth `CANVAS_ITEM_Z_MAX`, so buildings never cover it. Change to the kaiju's files: only the static `drawn_width_px()` in `kaiju_sprite.gd`. Test: `tests/unit/hud/test_kaiju_health_bar.gd`. Screenshot (copy in `art-archive/T-050/screenshots/`): `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/kaiju_bar_demo.tscn screenshots/kaiju_bar.png 20`.

### The meteor attack (T-046)

Task T-046 (Enemy Behavior Agent): the kaiju's one special move, with T-039's numbers (`meteor_attack` in `data/enemies.json`, through `EnemyData.meteor_attack()`; the schedule matches `EnemyData.meteor_attack_times()`). What it does: `docs/enemies.md`, Content, "The kaiju's meteor attack". The warning circle, the meteor, the dust and the windup are placeholders drawn in code; the final look comes from separate art tasks.

**See it:** turn the playtest switch `kaiju_early_arrival` on in `data/playtest.json` (it is off, the game's rule), start the game and wait: the kaiju walks in 30 seconds into the run and its first attack comes 5 seconds later. Screenshot, from the repo root: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/kaiju_meteors_demo.tscn screenshots/T-046_meteors.png 20` (the kaiju beside the car, three warning circles, one meteor falling, and two boulders just left by meteors with their dust, all held still; fixture script `tests/fixtures/kaiju_meteors_demo.gd`). A copy is in `art-archive/T-046/screenshots/`.

**How it is built:** `scripts/enemies/kaiju_meteors.gd`, a child `Meteors` of the boss (`run.kaiju_boss.meteors`), made and bound in `kaiju_boss.gd`'s `bind()` (the only change to the boss; `kaiju.gd` is unchanged). It moves in `step(dt)`, once per physics step from `_physics_process`, and its drawings animate in `_process`, so pausing the tree stops it; it does nothing once the run is over, and `run.world_rebuilt` (a new run) clears it. `enabled = false` stops new attacks (tests, fixtures); `rng` picks the landing spots (seed it for tests).

- **Clock:** `fight_time` counts the seconds the live kaiju has been on the map. The first attack starts at `first_attack_after_seconds`, then every `seconds_between_attacks`. `start_attack()` starts one now: `windup_seconds` of windup (`is_winding_up()`), then `drop_meteors()`.
- **Warnings:** `drop_meteors()` adds `meteors_per_attack` warnings (`add_warning(units)`), each at `pick_spot()`: uniform within `land_within_units_of_car` of the car, and `is_good_spot()`: the map's `can_place_meteor_boulder()` (T-097), no overlap with a warning still showing or a boulder still waiting, and off the kaiju's body. T-147: `is_good_spot()` also keeps `boulder_clearance_units` (`meteor_attack`, 1.5) of free ground between the circle and every boulder in `map.meteor_boulders` (standing or fading ones are there until `remove_meteor_boulder`), and between it and warnings and `pending_boulders`; the map's own boulders are covered by the placement check. Before, touching was allowed and the tall boulder sprite made a circle behind it look as if it fell on it. Test: `test_kaiju_meteors.gd` (many seeded attacks around a standing, a fading and a waiting boulder, a freed spot used again); screenshot fixture `tests/fixtures/meteors_avoid_boulders_demo.tscn`. `SPOT_TRIES` (40) tries per meteor; a meteor with no spot is left out. `warnings` lists `{units, time_left}`; each lands when `time_left` (from `warning_seconds`) reaches 0.
- **Landing:** `land(units)`: if the car's centre is within `impact_radius_units`, `RunState.damage_car(impact_damage)` and `car.apply_knockback(away from the spot, knockback)`. Then the dust, and the boulder through the map's `add_meteor_boulder(units)` if `can_place_meteor_boulder(units)` still accepts it. While the car's 1 x 1 body or the kaiju's body overlaps the boulder's circle, the boulder waits in `pending_boulders` and is placed on the first step the spot is clear.
- **Signals:** `attack_started()`, `warnings_shown(spots)`, `meteor_landed(units, hit_car)`, `boulder_placed(units, body)`.
- **Drawing:** the warning circles are the node `MeteorWarnings` in the map's `View`, right after `DriftMarks` (on the ground: under buildings, boulders, enemies and the car), drawn in screen pixels like the ground tiles. The windup rings, the falling meteor and the dust are the node `MeteorSky`, a child of the run with a z_index over everything. Colours: `WARNING_COLOR` hazard amber `Color(1, 0.62, 0.12)` (not the heart's red), a dark purple meteor with a magenta glow, warm grey dust, pale yellow windup.

**Not in the data:** the spot tries, the falling time (0.5 s) and height, the dust time (0.5 s), the rule that a spot avoids the kaiju and other meteors, and the rule that a boulder waits for the car or the kaiju to move off: rules of the code and the placeholder, not balancing numbers.

**Boulders fade (T-142).** `kaiju_meteors.gd` keeps each boulder it placed in `standing_boulders` and ages it in `step()` (so it stops while paused). Up to `boulder_lifetime_seconds` (10) it is untouched; then `map.set_meteor_boulder_alpha(body, a)` lowers its sprite's transparency in a straight line over `boulder_fade_seconds`, and at the end `map.remove_meteor_boulder(body)` takes it off the map: the body leaves the physics world (the car no longer bounces off it), the sprite is freed, and its entries in `meteor_boulders`, `meteor_boulder_bodies` and `all_boulders()` go, so the spot is free for a new boulder. Both functions only accept bodies from `add_meteor_boulder`, never the map's own boulders. Both numbers are in `data/enemies.json`, `meteor_attack`. The flat view's placeholder circles are not faded, they vanish at the end.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/enemies/test_kaiju_meteors.gd`, part of the GUT suite: the attack hooked into the boss and the map's drawing; no attack without the kaiju; attacks on the data's schedule; the windup, then the data's number of warning circles near the car where the placement check accepts, shown for 2 seconds, and the meteors landing where they showed; a meteor on the car damaging it and knocking it away, one elsewhere doing nothing; one boulder per meteor through `add_meteor_boulder()`, none on a spot the check refuses; the boulder waiting until the car is off the spot; the car bouncing off a meteor boulder; everything stopping while paused; nothing landing after the run ends; a new run clearing it; the placeholders not the heart's red; (T-142) a boulder fully visible and solid just before 10 seconds, fading and then removed from the scene and the physics world, the map's own boulders staying, and both numbers read from the data. The tests set the playtest switch off and do not depend on it.

**The old logo (T-075), history:** the old sponsor was renamed Greenbull (Visual style, Decisions, "The rebrand"), whose emblem and wordmark are `game/art/logo/greenbull_*` (T-294, T-295, T-300); this paragraph, the three old logo files and the tools `draw_gull_silhouette.py`, `flatten_logo.py`, `make_logo_sizes.py` and `check_logo_one_colour.py` (each marked HISTORY at its top) are kept as the old logo's record. The board's pick (2026-10-16) is PixelLab's `/create-image-pixflux` result at init strength 80 (the raw init-128, strength-80 file in `art-archive/T-075/`, made from the script silhouette `game/tools/draw_gull_silhouette.py`, variant `v1`'s init image `silhouette_init_128.png`). `python game/tools/make_logo_sizes.py <raw.png> [<out_dir>]` (the folder defaults to `art-archive/T-075/`) flattens it to its own main red (the old logo red, `style_rules.OLD_LOGO_RED` since T-306) as the 128 px old logo file and builds the 64 px and 32 px files from it (area-averaged alpha, hard threshold, eye cut-out kept at least 2x1 px). Since T-312 these three files (with their `.import` files) are in `art-archive/T-075/` with every other candidate, and `game/art/logo/` holds only Greenbull's; T-306's one-off rename script is in `art-archive/T-306/`. Their check (one visible dark-red colour, a transparent background, no semi-transparent pixels) is `style_rules.check_old_logo`, run by `check_logo_one_colour.py`; `--logo` now runs Greenbull's logo check. Tests: `game/tools/test_style_rules.py` and `game/tools/test_check_logo_one_colour.py`. No scene uses them. 6 generations in total: the first sample plus 5 of the board's allowance of 10.

## Elevation test scene (T-182)

The elevation test scene (Level design, Decisions, "Raised areas and ramps"; Drifting, Decisions, "Elevation"). T-182 made the ground side (data and a query), T-183 and T-184 the car's height, T-185 the scene and its drawing ("The scene and its drawing (T-185)" below, with how to run it), T-186 the platform and ramp art, T-192 the round shadow (T-187's bigger-when-high option was tried and removed). Every number is a proposal for the board's playtest.

### Ground-height data

`game/data/level/elevation_test.json`, in flat-world units (x east, y south, so north is -y; the car's units). Two lists, so more can be added later (the level editor):

- `platforms`: each has `id`, an axis-aligned rectangle (`x`, `y` = its north-west corner, `w`, `h`) and `height` (units). Proposal: one platform, 16 by 16 units at (-8, -8), height 3.
- `ramps`: each has `id`, `platform` (the id it climbs), `side` (`north`, `east`, `south` or `west`: the platform side it climbs), a footprint rectangle (`x`, `y`, `w`, `h`) next to that side, `bottom_height` (0) and `top_height` (the platform's). The height rises in a straight line from the far end of the footprint (bottom) to the platform side (top). Proposal: one ramp on the east side, 12 by 8 units at (8, -4).
- Every platform side without a ramp (and the platform's sides beside a ramp) is a drop-off.

### The query

`scripts/level/ground_height.gd` (`GroundHeight`, a RefCounted with no nodes or physics). `GroundHeight.from_file(path = DEFAULT_PATH)` (or `new()` then `load_file(path)`, which returns false on a bad file and leaves the ground flat):

- `height_at(point: Vector2) -> float`: 0 off the platform, the platform's height on top, the straight rise along a ramp.
- `is_ramp(point) -> bool` and `ramp_at(point) -> Dictionary` (empty off a ramp).
- `edge_between(a, b) -> Dictionary`: `{"drop": bool, "height": float, "up": bool}`. `drop` is true when the move from a to b crosses a platform edge that is not a ramp; `height` is the step's size and `up` says it is a climb (from below). Moving along a ramp is never a drop.

Tests: `tests/unit/test_ground_height.gd`.

### The car's height (T-183)

`scripts/driving/car_height.gd`, a node-free model (Drifting, Decisions, "Elevation"). The car (`car.gd`) owns one in `height_model` and steps it once per physics step, after its flat step, with the flat position before and after and the flat velocity. It reads the ground from `car.ground` (a `GroundHeight`); with `ground` null (the drift prototype, the run) the height stays 0. The flat driving (50 steps per second, the Unity values) is not changed, in the air too: the car steers in the air exactly as on the ground (a proposal; the docs don't say). The drawing of the height: "The scene and its drawing (T-185)" below.

- `car.height` (units, up), `car.vertical_speed` (units/s, up positive, 0 on the ground), `car.airborne`. Signals on `height_model`: `took_off(from_ramp, vertical_speed)` and `landed(impact_vertical_speed)`.
- **On the ground** (the ramp included) the height is the ground's height under the car.
- **Takeoff at the ramp's top:** on the step the car goes from the ramp onto ground that is not ramp, climbing, at a flat speed of at least `RAMP_TAKEOFF_MIN_SPEED`, it leaves the ground with vertical speed `vz = (velocity . climb direction) x (top_height - bottom_height) / ramp length x RAMP_TAKEOFF_FACTOR` (the climb direction points from the ramp's foot to its top; ramp length is the footprint's size along it). Its height that step carries on along the slope's line: `height + vz x dt`. With the test scene's ramp (rise 3 over 12) at cruise speed, 11 units/s, that is 2.75 units/s. Slower, it just follows the ground onto the platform.
- **Takeoff at a drop-off edge:** on the step the car crosses a step down (a drop-off edge or, since T-184, a ramp's side; see "The platform's sides" below), it is in the air with vertical speed 0 (flat platform ground), at any speed, and falls.
- **In the air:** each step `vertical_speed -= GRAVITY x dt`, then `height += vertical_speed x dt`.
- **Landing:** when the height reaches the ground under the car, the height snaps to the ground and the vertical speed becomes 0.
- The platform's and the ramp's sides against the car: "The platform's sides (T-184)" below.

Every number, in one place at the top of `car_height.gd`, is a proposal for the board's playtest:

| Constant | Value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `GRAVITY` | 12 units/s² | Pulls the vertical speed down in the air |
| `RAMP_TAKEOFF_MIN_SPEED` | 4 units/s | The slowest flat speed that leaves the ground at the ramp's top |
| `RAMP_TAKEOFF_FACTOR` | 1.0 | Share of the ramp's vertical speed kept at takeoff |
| `STEP_CLIMB_MAX` | 0.1 units | The biggest step of the ground driven over like a curb (T-184) |

Tests: `tests/unit/test_car_height.gd`.

### The platform's sides (T-184)

The proposals (Drifting, Content, "Elevation: the platform's sides"), all in `car_height.gd`'s `step`, which now returns what the sides did to the move:

- **The walk.** Each step, the move from the flat position before to the one after is walked in 0.05-unit pieces (`SAMPLE_STEP`) with the car's height along it: in the air, the straight line from the step's start height to its end height under gravity; on the ground, the ground's height. A piece that steps the ground **up** by more than `STEP_CLIMB_MAX` (a side, not a slope: the test ramp rises 0.0125 per piece) to more than `STEP_CLIMB_MAX` above the car is a **wall**: the walk stops at the last piece outside it. On the ground, the first piece that steps the ground **down** by more than `STEP_CLIMB_MAX` is a **drop**: the car takes off there (`took_off(false, 0)`) at its height before the step and falls. This replaces `edge_between` for the car (still in `GroundHeight`, unchanged), so a ramp's side is a drop too; driving along, up or down a ramp is neither.
- **A wall** (a drop-off edge from below, a ramp's side from beside it, or a platform's side met in the air below its top): `step` returns `{"wall": true, "point", "normal", "area", "wall_height", ...}`. `car.gd` moves the car to `point`, reflects its velocity off `normal` exactly as off a physics wall (`tuning.wall_bounce`, "Bounce") and emits `hit_obstacle(ground, area, normal, impact_speed)`: the collider is the `GroundHeight`, the shape index the area's index (`GroundHeight.area_index_at`: a platform's index in `platforms`, a ramp's `platforms.size()` + its index in `ramps`). Pressed against the side it counts once; it re-arms once the car is more than `REARM_GAP_UNITS` (0.25) from where it stopped, as for walls. The height is stepped as if the car had moved only to `point`, so a car in the air keeps falling beside the side.
- **Landing** is unchanged: when the height reaches the ground under the car's end position, on the top or on the street. Together with the walls, a step never ends with the car lower than the ground under it.
- `GroundHeight` adds `area_index_at(point) -> int`, `area_rect(index) -> Rect2` and `side_normal(index, a, b) -> Vector2` (the outward normal of the side a move from a, outside, to b, inside, enters through; at a corner the side crossed last).

Tests: `tests/unit/test_elevation_edges.gd`.

### The scene and its drawing (T-185)

The scene itself (Drifting, Content, "Elevation test scene"): `scenes/elevation_test/elevation_test.tscn`, script `scripts/driving/elevation_test.gd`. Not the main scene, not reachable from the menus, no on-screen text.

**Run it:** open the scene in the editor and press F6, or from the repo root `"$GODOT_BIN" --path game res://scenes/elevation_test/elevation_test.tscn`. W/S/A/D drive. The car starts at (26, 0) facing west (`START_UNITS`, `START_ROTATION`), in front of the ramp's foot.

How it is built (like the drift prototype, isometric only):

- `PhysicsWorld` (never drawn) holds the `Car` (`car.gd`), whose `ground` is the scene's `GroundHeight` (`ground_height`, from `data/level/elevation_test.json`). `place_car(units, rotation)` puts the car at rest on the ground there (the fixtures use it).
- `View` draws the street (`ground_grid.gd`, z_index -100), one `elevation_platform_drawing.gd` node per platform and ramp (`areas`, back to front), the `CarShadow` (`car_shadow.gd`), the `CarSprite` (`car_sprite.gd`, the final isometric sheet) and the `Camera`.
- `sync_view()` runs after every physics step (`car.stepped`): `car_sprite.place(drawn ground position, car.height)`, `shadow.place(map position, ground height under the car)`, `update_depth()`, then the camera (`camera_follow.gd`, as in the drift prototype) on the car's drawn position at height 0.

The drawing's main functions:

- `car_sprite.gd`: `lift_for_height(height_units, px_per_unit = 16) -> Vector2`, the lift straight up the screen (`(0, -height x 16)`); `place(drawn_ground_px, height_units)` sets the sprite's position to the drawn map position plus the lift. The lift is in the position, so physics interpolation smooths it.
- `car_shadow.gd` (round since T-192): `place(map_units, ground_height)` puts the shadow at the drawn map position lifted by the ground's height under the car; `outline()` is a 32-point circle on the ground, `DIAMETER_UNITS` (1.0 unit across, a proposal), run through the view's transform so it is an ellipse twice as wide as tall on screen, centred on the shadow's position; it does not turn with the car. Drawn in one opaque `COLOR`, no antialiasing. The car's drawing is always at the sheet's own scale, at every height.
- `elevation_platform_drawing.gd`: `setup(kind, block, view_basis)` with a `GroundHeight` platform or ramp; `corner_heights()` (NW, NE, SE, SW), `top_height_at(point)`, `top_face()`, `east_face()`, `south_face()` (drawn polygons; a side of height 0 has none); `colors_used()` lists every colour (clear of the heart's red); `is_behind(centre, radius, height)` is the depth rule: the centre west or north of the area's rectangle (the footprint, `radius` around it, not wholly east or south of it) and lower than its top there. The centre, not the footprint, since T-191: the sides stop the car's centre, so at a back-side wall hit its footprint reaches into the rectangle.
- `elevation_test.gd`: `z_for(centre, height, offset)` puts something just under the first area it is behind, or over every area (areas at z_index 3, 6, ...; the shadow at offset 0 under the car at offset 1); `update_depth()` places the shadow with the ground's height under the car and the car with its own height; `sync_view()` then calls `update_see_through(car.model.dt)` (see-through, T-191 below).

Every colour and size in the drawing is a proposal for the board's playtest. Tests: `tests/unit/test_elevation_drawing.gd`. Screenshot fixtures: `tests/fixtures/elevation_test_{ground,ramp,platform,air,behind}.tscn` and, for the round shadow (T-192; `lift_units` puts the car in the air), `elevation_test_shadow_{street,platform,air,platform_air}.tscn` (`elevation_test_demo.gd`: a start, W held for `drive_steps` physics steps, then the car stops), for example `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/elevation_test_air.tscn docs/drifting/elevation-air.png 90`.

### See-through raised areas (T-191)

The platform and the ramp turn see-through like the map's buildings (Drifting, Content, "Elevation test scene"; Level design, Content, "When a building fades") while they cover the car. The code is shared with the buildings, not copied:

- The numbers: each area's `see_through_alpha` and `fade_time` default to `mvp_map.gd`'s `SEE_THROUGH_ALPHA` (0.15) and `SEE_THROUGH_FADE_TIME` (0.15 s), the buildings' constants.
- The fade step: `map_building.gd`'s static `faded_alpha(a, covering, delta, see, time)`, which the buildings' `step_fade` now calls too.
- The car's shape on screen: `mvp_map.gd`'s static `car_screen_shape_at(centre, rotation, lift_px)` (the 1 by 3 unit car, 1 unit tall; `car_screen_shape` calls it with no lift). The scene lifts it by the car's height (`elevation_test.gd`, `car_shape()`).
- `elevation_platform_drawing.gd`: `silhouette()` (the convex hull of the top face and the ground corners, drawn pixels), `covers(centre, radius, height, car_shape)` (`is_behind` and the shapes overlap), `update_fade(centre, radius, height, car_shape, delta)` (sets `covering` and moves `modulate.a`; the art sprite is a child, so it fades with it), `alpha()`.
- `elevation_test.gd`: `update_see_through(delta)` updates every area with the car's position, height and shape, after every physics step (from `sync_view`); `covering_areas()` lists the areas covering the car.

Tests: `tests/unit/test_elevation_see_through.gd` (the fade and its numbers; driving into the west and north sides straight, angled and steering, the car under the platform on every step). Screenshot fixtures: `tests/fixtures/elevation_test_see_through.tscn` (behind the platform), `elevation_test_wall_west.tscn` and `elevation_test_wall_north.tscn` (the step the car meets each back side), and `elevation_test_wall_{west,north}_solid.tscn` (the same with the fade off, `elevation_test_demo.gd`'s `see_through = false`, to show the depth order alone).

### The round shadow, and no bigger-when-high (T-192)

The board tried drawing the car bigger when high (T-187) and removed it: `draw_bigger_when_high`, `HEIGHT_SCALE_PER_UNIT`, `height_scale_per_unit` and `scale_factor_for_height` are gone, and the car is drawn at the sheet's own scale at every height. The shadow is a round shadow instead (`car_shadow.gd`, above): a 1-unit circle on the ground under the car's map position, an ellipse twice as wide as tall on screen, not turning with the car. Tests: `tests/unit/test_elevation_drawing.gd` (`test_the_cars_drawing_scale_is_the_same_at_every_height`, `test_the_shadow_*`). Screenshots: `art-archive/T-192/screenshots/`.

**The shadow on raised ground (T-258).** In the run and the level creator's runs (`run.gd` `_sync_view_elevation`, `elevation_sample_map.gd` `update_depth`) the shadow's depth is `elevation_layer.gd` `shadow_z_for(units, rot, height, ground_h)`: under the car, under the buildings and boulders that cover it, but under a rooftop or ramp only while the car itself is under it (`z_for(..., include_areas = false)` for the ground part). Before, the shadow took the depth of the ground height alone, so a car in the air drawn over a rooftop or ramp (flying in from behind it or off its edge) had its shadow drawn under that area and lost. Test: `tests/unit/run/test_run_shadow_on_raised_ground.gd`; screenshots and a GIF of a 6 to 3 jump: `art-archive/T-258/screenshots/` (fixtures `tests/fixtures/run_shadow_air_*.tscn`, `run_shadow_jump_6_to_3.tscn`). The elevation test scene (`elevation_test.gd` `update_depth`) uses the same rule (under the car, and under an area only while the car is); `test_elevation_drawing.gd` `test_the_car_above_the_platform_behind_it_is_over_it_and_so_is_its_shadow` was changed to expect it.

### The platform and ramp art (T-186)

PixelLab art replaces the script drawing: `art/elevation/platform.png` (1024 x 608) and `ramp.png` (640 x 416), each with its record (`platform.json`, `ramp.json`: sources, `top_left_art_px` from the drawn origin, 32 art px per unit). `elevation_platform_drawing.gd` adds a `Sprite2D` (`art_sprite`, scale 0.5, nearest filter, at `top_left_art_px` x 0.5) when the PNG and record exist, and then draws nothing itself; the script drawing stays as the fallback, and its geometry functions and the depth order (`is_behind`) are unchanged.

Made from 3 PixelLab textures (art-archive/T-186/, 3 generations) by `python game/tools/build_elevation_art.py build` (projects them onto the faces of `data/level/elevation_test.json`; `check` runs the rules on the committed files; run `build` again if the JSON's sizes change). Tests: `game/tools/test_build_elevation_art.py` (Python) and `tests/unit/test_elevation_art.gd`. Screenshots: `docs/drifting/elevation-final-art.png` (car at the platform's edge, in the air) and `elevation-final-art-platform.png` (on the platform).

## Elevation sample map (T-193)

The drive-only sample map with three layers (Level design, Decisions, "Elevation sample map"; Content, "Elevation sample map"): the street (height 0), a first floor (height 3) and a second floor (height 6). T-193 made its height data and the validator, T-194 the layout file ("The layout file (T-194)" below), T-196 the drive-only scene ("The scene (T-196)" below). Every number is a proposal for the board's playtest.

### Height data

The same format as the elevation test scene's (`data/level/elevation_test.json`, unchanged), read by the same `GroundHeight` (`scripts/level/ground_height.gd`). A JSON object with two lists, in flat-world units (x east, y south, so north is -y), so a level editor can later add, move and remove areas. Every field:

- `platforms` (a list of raised areas, rooftops):
  - `id` (text, unique): the name ramps and other platforms use for it.
  - `x`, `y` (numbers): the rectangle's north-west corner; `w`, `h` (numbers, above 0): its width (east) and depth (south). Axis-aligned.
  - `height` (number): the ground's height on top, in units (3 for a first floor, 6 for a second floor).
  - `on` (text, optional, new in T-193): the `id` of the platform it stands on (a second-floor rooftop on a first-floor one). Leave it out for an area standing on the street. Its whole rectangle must be inside that platform, and it must be higher. Two platforms may overlap only when one stands on the other (directly or through others); any other overlap is a mistake. `GroundHeight` keeps it as `on` ("" when missing) but doesn't need it: at any point the highest area wins.
- `ramps` (a list of slopes):
  - `id` (text, unique).
  - `platform` (text): the `id` of the area the ramp climbs to.
  - `side` (`north`, `east`, `south` or `west`): that area's side the ramp climbs; the ramp lies outside the area on that side (an `east` ramp is east of its area and rises westward).
  - `x`, `y`, `w`, `h` (numbers): the ramp's footprint. Its top end lies on the area's side, within its length.
  - `bottom_height` (number, default 0): the height at the foot (the end away from the area). It must match the ground the ramp stands on: 0 on the street, or the height of the platform holding its whole footprint (3 for a ramp on a first floor up to a second floor).
  - `top_height` (number): the height at the top; it must match the height of the area it climbs to.
  - The height rises in a straight line from foot to top. The ramp's other two sides are drop-offs.
- Anything else (for example `_comment`) is ignored.

The query is unchanged ("Elevation test scene", "The query" above): `height_at`, `ramp_at` and `area_index_at` give the highest area at a point (a ramp over the platform it stands on), and `side_normal` and `edge_between` work between rooftops too (the step from a height-6 area onto the height-3 area under it is 3).

**Validator:** from the repo root, `python game/tools/check_height_data.py <file.json>`. It prints `OK` and exits 0 when the file is valid; otherwise one line per problem and exit code 1. It rejects: overlapping platforms neither of which stands on the other; an `on` naming an unknown area, or an area that doesn't contain the whole footprint, or one that isn't lower; a ramp climbing to an unknown area; an unknown `side`; a ramp whose top doesn't touch the side it climbs; a `top_height` that doesn't match the area it climbs to; a `bottom_height` that doesn't match the ground at its foot; a ramp overlapping an area it doesn't stand on wholly; missing numbers, non-positive sizes, missing or repeated ids. Run it on every height-data file before handing a map over.

Tests: `tests/fixtures/height/three_layers.json` (a height-3 area at (-20, -20), 40 by 40; a height-6 area on it at (-10, -10), 16 by 16; a 0-to-3 ramp east of the first and a 3-to-6 ramp on it east of the second, each 12 by 8) with `tests/unit/test_ground_height_layers.gd` (GUT), and `tools/test_check_height_data.py` (Python tool tests).

### The layout file (T-194)

`data/level/elevation_sample_map.json`: the MVP map's layout (`data/level/mvp_map.json`) with the same keys `mvp_map.gd` reads (`map`, `gravel`, `buildings`, `pattern_copies`, `challenges`, `challenge_designs`, `ground_rows` and the rest), so the map script can load it, plus `platforms` and `ramps` in the height-data format above. The layout (two parking garages, rooftops at 3 and 6) and its picture: Level design, Content, "Elevation sample map". Every rooftop and ramp is a proposal for the board's playtest.

From the repo root:

- `python game/tools/build_elevation_sample_map.py` rebuilds the file from `mvp_map.json` (never changes it), runs the validator on the result, and draws `docs/level-design/elevation-sample-map.png` (and its copy in `art-archive/T-194/screenshots/`).
- `python game/tools/build_elevation_sample_map.py --check` rebuilds it in memory and prints `OK` (exit 0) when the committed file matches and the height data is valid; otherwise it says what differs (exit 1).
- To change the layout, edit `ROOFTOPS` and `RAMPS` (and `RAMP_LENGTH`, `RAMP_WIDTH`, `FOOT_APPROACH`) at the top of the script and run it again.

What the script does to the MVP layout: it leaves out every building overlapping a rooftop; every boulder overlapping a rooftop, a ramp or the strip `FOOT_APPROACH` (6) units deep in front of a ramp's foot; every pattern copy left with no boulder; and every challenge whose copy lost a boulder (challenges are off in the MVP). It rebuilds `ground_rows` from the remaining buildings (cells under a rooftop are `R`), updates `challenge_rule`'s counts, and adds `name`, `task`, `based_on` and `elevation_note` (what was left out).

Tests: `tools/test_build_elevation_sample_map.py` (Python tool tests): heights exactly {0, 3, 6}; an area at 3 and one at 6; a 0-to-3 and a 3-to-6 ramp; every area reachable from the street up ramps (a graph walk); no ramp or ramp foot strip on a building, a boulder or off the map; no boulder on a rooftop or ramp; no building under a rooftop; the validator accepts the file; the check mode matches.

### The scene (T-196)

`scenes/elevation_sample/elevation_sample_map.tscn`, script `scripts/level/elevation_sample_map.gd`: the elevation test scene made city-sized. Drive-only: no enemies, minions, kaiju, weapons, run timer, likes, levels or HUD; the car just drives around freely. Not the main scene, not reachable from the menus (no button: one would need its text from the Narrative Theme Agent), no on-screen text.

**Open it (a proposal, as the elevation test scene):** open the scene in the editor and press F6, or from the repo root `"$GODOT_BIN" --path game res://scenes/elevation_sample/elevation_sample_map.tscn`. W/S/A/D drive. The car starts on the road at (-81, -50) facing north (`START_UNITS`, `START_ROTATION`), 4 units from the foot of the north-west garage's 0-to-3 ramp, so W drives straight up it.

**Run it headless** (no window, to check for errors): `python game/tools/run_scene_headless.py res://scenes/elevation_sample/elevation_sample_map.tscn 10` imports the project, runs the scene for 10 seconds and exits 0 when Godot printed no error lines (1 with the lines, 2 for bad arguments or no `GODOT_BIN`). `--show` also prints what the scene printed. Tests: `tools/test_run_scene_headless.py`.

What it holds:

- `Map`: the MVP map's script (`mvp_map.gd`, from `scenes/level/mvp_map.tscn`) with `layout_path` set to `data/level/elevation_sample_map.json` before it enters the tree, in the isometric view: the ground art, the buildings with their art (see-through: the scene fades them, see "Depth order and see-through (T-197)" below; `map.car` stays unset), the boulders and the edge walls. `layout_path` (new in T-196) defaults to `LAYOUT_PATH` (`mvp_map.json`); the run sets it to this file since T-206 ("The run's map (T-206)").
- `PhysicsWorld` (never drawn) holds the `Car` (`car.gd`), whose `ground` is the scene's `ground_height` (a `GroundHeight` read from the same file), so the car has its real height (`car_height.gd`) on the rooftops and ramps. It collides with the map's buildings, boulders and edge walls. `place_car(units, rotation)` puts it at rest on the ground there.
- `Map/Elevation` (since T-206, `elevation_layer.gd`; before, under `View`): one `elevation_platform_drawing.gd` node per platform and ramp in the file (`areas`, named `platform_<id>` and `ramp_<id>`), drawn with its own art from `art/elevation_sample/<id>.png` ("Rooftop and ramp art (T-198)" below) or, when that art is missing, with its script drawing. `View`: the `CarShadow` (`car_shadow.gd`, on the ground under the car: the rooftop's top, the ramp or the street); the `CarSprite` (the final isometric sheet, lifted by the car's height); and the `Camera` (`camera_follow.gd` with the saved zoom and camera options, following the car as drawn, height included and eased, T-228).

Tests: `tests/unit/test_elevation_sample_map.gd`. Screenshot fixtures (`tests/fixtures/elevation_sample_demo.gd`: a start, W held for `drive_steps` physics steps, then the car stops; `hold_w = false` keeps it standing, `zoom` > 0 fixes the zoom with the dynamic camera off): `elevation_sample_{street,ramp_0_3,ramp_3_6,height_3,height_6,air}.tscn`. For example `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/elevation_sample_air.tscn art-archive/T-196/screenshots/air.png 700` (the air shot drives 106 steps, so it needs many frames). Screenshots: `art-archive/T-196/screenshots/`.

### Rooftop and ramp art (T-198)

PixelLab art for every rooftop and ramp of `data/level/elevation_sample_map.json`: `art/elevation_sample/<area id>.png` (for example `roof-nw-3.png`, `ramp-se-0-3.png`) with its record `<id>.json` (kind, `top_left_art_px` from the drawn origin, 32 art px per unit, drawn at scale 0.5, the PixelLab records it came from). `elevation_platform_drawing.gd`'s `setup(kind, block, basis, art_dir, art_name)` adds a `Sprite2D` (`art_sprite`) when the PNG and record exist, and then draws nothing itself; with them missing the script drawing is the fallback. The elevation sample map scene passes `SAMPLE_ART_DIR` and the area's id; the elevation test scene keeps its own art (`art/elevation/`, T-186). The sprite is a child of the drawing, so it fades with its area when the area turns see-through.

Made from 8 PixelLab textures and details (`art-archive/T-198/`, 8 generations, `requests/` holds the request files) by `python game/tools/build_elevation_sample_art.py build` (needs Pillow and numpy; it projects the textures onto each area's top and its east and south walls, adds the parapet, details and cracks on the rooftops, outlines, and reduces each sprite to 30 colours; a few minutes for the two 84 x 30 rooftops). **To rebuild after a layout change** (the JSON's platforms or ramps): run `build` again; the details' places are fixed by each area's id. `python game/tools/build_elevation_sample_art.py --check` runs the art rules and the record checks on the committed files and exits 1 on a failure. It never calls PixelLab. Tests: `game/tools/test_build_elevation_sample_art.py` (Python: `python -m unittest discover -s game/tools -p test_build_elevation_sample_art.py`) and `tests/unit/test_elevation_sample_art.gd`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-198/screenshots/` (`street.png`, `rooftop_height_3.png`, `rooftop_height_6.png`, `ramp.png`, `see_through.png`).

### Depth order and see-through (T-197)

Every raised area, ramp and building of the sample map is in one depth order with the car and its shadow (Level design, Decisions, "Elevation sample map: depth order and see-through"). Since T-206 the code is in `scripts/level/elevation_layer.gd` (the map's child `Elevation`), shared by this scene and the run; the scene's `restack()`, `z_for()`, `update_see_through()`, `covering_areas()` and the rest below call its layer (`elevation`).

- **One back-to-front order:** `restack()` sorts the map's building slots and the areas together (`drawn_before`: an item standing on another, a stacked rooftop or a ramp whose foot is on a rooftop, is drawn after it; otherwise one wholly west or north of the other first; ties by the centre's x + y) and gives each its own z_index, `BUILDING_Z_STEP` (3) apart from `DEPTH_Z_BASE` (5, one step over the map's base, so a car under a boulder under the first item, and its shadow, stay over the ground at z 0); `depth_items` is the order. Then the boulders (`boulder_z`: under the buildings and areas that cover them, otherwise over every item, areas included) and the pavement signs are put back in it.
- **The car and its shadow:** `z_for(height)` puts the car just under the lowest building, boulder or area that covers it, otherwise over everything (`top_z_index()`). An area covers the car when the car is behind it (its centre west or north of the area, judged by the centre so a wall hit from behind doesn't pop it over, T-191), lower than the area's top there, and its shape (lifted by its height) overlaps the area's outline on screen. So the car is drawn under an area it is behind and lower than, and over the ones it is on, above or in front of; a car on a height-3 rooftop behind a height-6 one is between the two.
