The shadow takes `z_for` at the ground's height under the car, one lower.
- **See-through:** `update_see_through(delta)` (every physics step, from `sync_view`) fades each area and ramp covering the car (`elevation_platform_drawing.gd`, `update_fade`) and each building covering it (`map_building.gd`, `update_fade`), both with the car's shape at its height, the shape the depth order uses, to `mvp_map.gd`'s `SEE_THROUGH_ALPHA` (15%) over `SEE_THROUGH_FADE_TIME` (0.15 s), and the others back to solid. The fade step is `map_building.gd`'s `faded_alpha` for both: no second formula. The map's own fade (`map.car`) is left off here so the buildings are not faded twice with the car's shape on the ground. `covering_areas()` and `covering_buildings()` list what covered the car at the last update.

Tests: `tests/unit/test_elevation_sample_depth.gd` (the car on height 3 behind a height-6 area, in the air above 6 over a height-3 area, behind a building next to the garage, on top of a height-6 area; every `drawn_before` pair kept; grids over both garages checking the car is under every area covering it and over every area it overlaps without being behind; the shadow over the ground everywhere; uncovered boulders over every item) and `tests/unit/test_elevation_sample_see_through.gd` (the fade's numbers and step, no second fade formula, which areas fade where, the buildings fading, and drives into the west and north sides of height-3 and height-6 areas, from the street and from a height-3 rooftop, with the car and shadow under the area on every step of the hit). Screenshot fixtures: `elevation_sample_behind_{height_3,height_6,building}.tscn` (the car standing for 15 steps, so the fade is done), for example `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/elevation_sample_behind_height_6.tscn art-archive/T-197/screenshots/behind_height_6.png 60`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-197/screenshots/`.

### The car's height on the three layers (T-195)

The car's height (`car_height.gd`, "The car's height (T-183)" and "The platform's sides (T-184)" above) works unchanged on the sample map's three layers: no fix was needed for ramps whose foot is on a rooftop (a 3-to-6 ramp), because the walk, the takeoff and the landing read the ground's height under the car, wherever its zero is. Its constants are unchanged.

**Tests** (`tests/unit/test_car_height_layers.gd`, the real `car.gd` and `car_height.gd` on `data/level/elevation_sample_map.json`'s ground). The ramps and areas are picked from the file by their heights, never by coordinates, so a layout change doesn't break them:

- up a 0-to-3 ramp and up a 3-to-6 ramp, the height is the ground's under the car on every step;
- off a 3-to-6 ramp's top at about cruise speed, the car takes off with `vertical_speed = ramp_vertical_speed x RAMP_TAKEOFF_FACTOR`, along the slope's line;
- off a height-6 side, it falls and lands on the height-3 area past it, and, off another side, on the street (each side found by searching the height-6 areas' sides for flat ground at 6 inside and at 3, or 0, outside, no ramp);
- on a height-3 rooftop, the side of a height-6 area is a wall: one hit (`hit_obstacle` with the `GroundHeight` and the area's index), the bounce, the car stays at 3;
- in the sample map scene, every tall building and every boulder with a clear 6-unit approach (88 of them) blocks the car on the street, on height 3, on height 6 and in the air (at 8 units, rising). The layout keeps rooftops away from buildings and boulders, so for 3 and 6 the test raises a temporary rooftop under the approach and the obstacle; the buildings and boulders are the map's own bodies, which block in the flat world at every height.

**The scripted drive** (`tools/drive_elevation_sample.gd`): the real car in the sample map scene, driven by scripted inputs from the street up a 0-to-3 ramp, up the 3-to-6 ramp on the rooftop it reaches, across the height-6 rooftop and off its far edge. The route comes from the file by heights (`plan()`: the first 0-to-3 ramp with a 3-to-6 ramp on its rooftop); the inputs hold W and steer toward waypoints along it (A/D in proportion to the heading error, `STEER_GAIN`), then release W once the car leaves the edge. It prints each layer reached with its time, the waypoints, every jump (where it took off, its takeoff vertical speed, its maximum height, its airtime and where it landed), the maximum height, the airtime of the jump off the height-6 edge, any wall or obstacle hit, and PASS or FAIL. From the repo root:

```
bash game/tools/drive_elevation_sample.sh [--csv=<file.csv>] [--plot=<file.png>]
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File game/tools/drive_elevation_sample.ps1 [--csv=<file.csv>] [--plot=<file.png>]
```

It runs headless. Exit codes: 0 = the street, height 3 and height 6 reached in that order and the car landed off the height-6 edge; 1 = not; 2 = bad arguments or no such route in the file. `--csv` writes one line per physics step (time, position, height, the ground's height under the car, airborne, W, steer); `--plot` draws the height over time as a PNG (and `<name>_light.png`): the car's height, the ground under it, the time in the air shaded, lines at 0, 3 and 6, a tick every second. `tests/unit/test_drive_elevation_sample.gd` runs the same drive and checks it passes (no wall on the way up, about 1 s in the air off the edge, landing on the street).

Screenshot fixtures (`tests/fixtures/elevation_sample_drive_demo.gd`: the same drive, stopped after `steps` physics steps, or `plot = true` to show the plot instead of the map): `elevation_sample_drive_{height_3,ramp_jump,off_edge,plot,plot_light}.tscn`, for example `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/elevation_sample_drive_plot.tscn art-archive/T-195/screenshots/height-over-time.png 30`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-195/screenshots/`.

**The car without its sprite shadow in the air (T-205).** `scripts/driving/car_sprite.gd` loads `art/car/car_iso_noshadow.png` (T-204; same layout as `car_iso.png`) next to the normal sheet. `set_in_air(bool)` swaps only the texture, so the frame, offset and scale do not change; `is_in_air(height, ground_under)` is true when the car's height is above the ground under it. `sync_view()` of the elevation sample map and of the elevation test scene calls it after every physics step, so the sheet switches on the takeoff step and the landing step. The run calls it too on the elevation map (T-206, "The run's map (T-206)"); on the MVP map and in the drift prototype the car always draws `car_iso.png` (and the flat view has no air sheet). Tests: `tests/unit/test_car_noshadow_in_air.gd`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-205/screenshots/` (`air.png` from `elevation_sample_air.tscn`, `street.png` from `elevation_sample_street.tscn`).

## Level creator (T-231)

The level creator (Level design, Decisions, "Level creator") builds levels in the map-file format of `data/level/elevation_sample_map.json` (`map`, `legend`, `ground_rows`, `buildings`, `pattern_copies` with the boulders, `platforms`, `ramps`), so the run, `GroundHeight` and the enemies' pathfinding read them unchanged. Later creator tickets fill in the rest of this section.

- **Where levels are saved (a proposal for the board):** `game/data/levels/`, read as `res://data/levels/` (`LevelStore.LEVELS_DIR`). The creator runs from the repo and is never exported.
- **Storage:** `scripts/level/level_store.gd` (`LevelStore`): `list_levels(dir = LEVELS_DIR)` gives `[{name, path}]` sorted by name (the file's `name`, or its file name), `load_level(path)` the level as a Dictionary (empty when missing or not a JSON object), `save_level(path, data)` writes indented JSON, creating the folder, and returns `OK` or an error. Tests: `tests/unit/test_level_store.gd` (only under `RunPaths`, never the real folder).
- **Validator:** from the repo root, `python game/tools/check_level.py <file.json>` prints `OK` (exit 0) or one line per problem (exit 1). It runs `check_height_data.py` on the file and also checks that every building, rooftop, ramp and boulder lies inside `map.rect`, that the edges of buildings, rooftops and ramps lie on even units (`enemy_navigation.gd`'s `CELL` = 2), and that `ground_rows` has as many rows and columns as the map's size in tiles (1 tile = 1 unit); since T-272 also that every pair of obstacles (`pairs`) names two existing obstacles ("Thread the needle (T-272)" below). Tests: `tools/test_check_level.py`.
- **Opening the creator (T-234):** a separate app with its own main scene, `scenes/level_creator/level_creator.tscn`; the game's main scene in `project.godot` stays the game, and no game menu or script refers to the creator. From the repo root: `bash game/tools/level_creator.sh`, or `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File game/tools/level_creator.ps1`. It opens on the level list (`scripts/level_creator/level_list_screen.gd`): each level from `LevelStore.list_levels()` by name with Play and Edit, and the New level button (the only thing there when there are no levels). New level and Edit open the editor scene (`scenes/level_creator/editor.tscn`, an empty map or the level loaded; "The editor (T-235)" below); Play starts a run on the level ("Playing a level (T-239)" below). Every label comes from `data/level/creator_texts.json` through `CreatorTexts.get_text(key)`; `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_texts_only.gd` fails on any hard-coded label in the creator's scripts and scenes. Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-234/screenshots/`.
- **Playing a level (T-239):** on the level list, press **Play** on a level's row. The creator (`scripts/level_creator/level_creator.gd`, `play_level(path)`) swaps the list for a full run on that level's file: the game's own run (8:00 on the clock, the minions, the kaiju at boss time, weapons, level-ups, the pause screen on Escape), built by `GameFlow.add_run(parent, progress, layout_path)`, the same function the main menu's Start uses, so the garage upgrades from the saved progress, the saved zoom and camera options apply, and the run's coins are banked into the save as in the game. The run's `layout_path` is the level's file (T-230), so the map, its rooftops and ramps, and the enemies' navigation are all built from the level. **Where the run returns (a proposal for the board):** when the run ends, win or loss, the creator shows its level list again (no message), and Play can start the level again. The game's own flow is unchanged: Start on the main menu still plays `data/level/elevation_sample_map.json`. Signals: `play_requested(path)` just before the run starts, `run_shown` after it, `list_shown` whenever the list is on screen. Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_play_level.gd` (a level saved into a temporary folder, `RunPaths`). Screenshots (`bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_play_demo.tscn art-archive/T-239/screenshots/run_on_custom_level.png 150`, and `level_creator_play_demo_ended.tscn` for the list the run returns to; `level_creator_play_demo_list.tscn` stops on the list before Play): `art-archive/T-239/screenshots/`.
- **Export (must be excluded):** `game/` has no `export_presets.cfg` today. When one is made, its export filter must exclude `scenes/level_creator/*`, `scripts/level_creator/*` and `data/levels/*` (the levels folder), so the creator and its levels are never part of what players get. If an `export_presets.cfg` exists, it must carry those exclusions.
- **Asset catalogue (T-232):** `data/level/creator_assets.json` lists every placeable asset already in the game (ground and road tiles, skyscrapers and short buildings, boulders with their mirrors, rooftops at heights 3 and 6, ramps 0 to 3, 3 to 6 and, since T-251, 0 to 6), each with `id`, `kind`, `footprint_units`, `views`, `mirrors` and `textures`; the rooftops and ramps are of any size since T-251 (`resizable`, `style`, and `min_size_units`, or `length_units`, `width_units`, `min_width_units`: "Rooftops and ramps of any size (T-251)" below). No art is made. To rebuild it after the art data changes, run from the repo root `python game/tools/build_creator_assets.py` (same input, same file). Tests: `tools/test_build_creator_assets.py`, `tests/unit/test_creator_assets.gd`.
- **Small and long buildings (T-254):** two more pieces under Buildings, labelled `building_small` and `building_long` in `creator_texts.json`: a small square building (6 x 6 units) and a long one (12 x 6, and 6 x 12 with Rotate, views `ew` and `ns`), each with a mirror (a second window pattern, written as the building's `look`). Both are short buildings of **4.3 units** (a proposal, the same as today's short ones), solid (a wall of their footprint, `mvp_map.gd`), see-through when the car is behind them (a `MapBuilding` fade node) and closed cells for the enemies' pathfinding. **Where the art is:** one data file, `data/level/creator_buildings.json`: per piece, per view and mirror, the `texture`, the `footprint_units` that view covers and `anchor_art_px` (the image pixel on the footprint's front corner). The catalogue builder copies it into `creator_assets.json` (a texture's own `footprint_units` wins over the entry's; `LevelDocument.footprint_of`), and `building_towers.gd` (`piece_of`, `_setup_piece`) draws a map entry whose `asset` is one of them. The art is a code-drawn placeholder in the city's palette (`python game/tools/build_creator_buildings.py` draws it, `--check` checks anchors, sizes and colours); T-257 swaps in the final art by changing the paths and anchors in the data file and running `python game/tools/build_creator_assets.py`. **Final art (T-257):** the data file now points to `art/buildings/creator_final/` (6 PixelLab facades, 6 generations; `python game/tools/build_creator_buildings_art.py --raw art-archive/T-257/raw --out-dir game/art/buildings/creator_final` assembles it for free: sheared facades, script roof, T-129 props, palette B, pavement over the footprint with a half-unit margin; same sizes and anchors as the placeholders). `build_creator_buildings.py` only redraws textures still under `art/buildings/creator/`. Request files: `game/tools/creator_building_requests.py`. Tests: `tools/test_build_creator_buildings_art.py`. Screenshot: `art-archive/T-257/screenshots/new_buildings.png`. Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_small_long_buildings.gd`, `tools/test_build_creator_buildings.py`. Screenshot (`tests/fixtures/level_creator_buildings_demo.tscn`): `art-archive/T-254/screenshots/new_buildings.png`.
- **The editor (T-235):** `scripts/level_creator/editor.gd` on `scenes/level_creator/editor.tscn`. The level is data (`scripts/level_creator/level_document.gd`, `LevelDocument`) drawn by the run's own scripts: `mvp_map.gd` built from the level's data (its `layout` and `ground_layout`) and `elevation_layer.gd` (`setup_data`, the same as `setup` from data already loaded) for the rooftops and ramps, rebuilt when a piece changes (only the ground tiles are redrawn for a ground or road tile). A new level is an empty map of today's size (240 x 240 units, centred), every ground tile `ground-gravel0` (a proposal). The side panel (`scripts/level_creator/asset_panel.gd`) shows every catalogue asset as a button with its art, under the category names of `creator_texts.json` (Ground, Roads, Buildings, Boulders, Rooftops, Ramps); rooftops and ramps carry their height labels. **Since T-262 the panel is one button per kind of piece** ("The side panel: one button per kind of piece (T-262)" below).
  - **The side panel: one button per kind of piece (T-262):** (Level design, Decisions, "Level creator rework"). Six groups from the ground up, each a heading (`group_*` in `creator_texts.json`) over a grid of buttons, two to a row, each showing its first look's art and its name (`piece_*`): **Roads** Road, Centre line, Zebra crossing, Cracked road; **Ground** Gravel; **Levels** Platform, Ramp; **Buildings** Small building, Long building, Low-rise, Skyscraper; **Obstacles** Boulder, Barrier, Cones, Crates; **Decoration** Street lamp, AC unit, Water tank, Roof vent, Rooftop sign (20 buttons). **Pavement is left out:** the only pavement art is the 30-unit block pavements under buildings (`cityB_pavement_*.png`, 960 x 486 px), not a 2 x 2 ground tile. Click a button to pick its first look; click it again (or Esc) to drop it.
    - **Keys:** **V** next look, **R** turn (a prop's or ramp's direction, the long building's two views, a road tile along y or x, a road piece's four turns), **M** mirror (the art's mirrored look: boulders, low-rise, small and long buildings, and a road tile's `a0m` asphalt), **H** height (Platform 3 / 6, Ramp 0 to 3, 3 to 6, 0 to 6), each round to the first after the last; **T** style as before. V and H keep the turn and mirror where the new look or height has them. The Rotate and Mirror buttons do the same as R and M and are greyed out when there is nothing to step to. The top bar names the picked height, a ramp's direction and a road piece (Corner, T-junction, Crossroads, Dead end). The bottom line is `controls_hint_rework` (its font shrinks, to 10 at the least, when the line is wider than the map view; A and B are T-264 and T-265's; road dragging is T-263's road brush, where R does nothing).
    - **The looks strip:** under the picked piece's group: `looks_heading`, a small picture per look at the picked height (in the picked turn and mirror), the chosen one pressed, clickable to pick it, and `look_counter` ("Look 2 of 12").
    - **Which looks a piece has (proposals for the board's playtest):** Road: the asphalts `a0` (mirror `a0m`), `g6`, `g9`; Cracked road: the visibly cracked `g4`, `g5`, `g7` (plain tiles only); Centre line: the line's right and left half tiles on each asphalt (12 looks), then the T-252 pieces (corner, T-junction, crossroads, dead end) and the first approved road tile (17 looks); Zebra crossing: the three zebra tiles on each asphalt (18 looks); Gravel: 3; Low-rise: 4; Skyscraper: 6 towers x 2 bodies (12 looks: each tower's second body, never offered before, is placed as `look: 1`, which the run already draws, `building_towers.gd` `single_of`); Boulder: 2. **Since T-263** the road kinds are brushes and their looks are asphalts ("The road brush (T-263)" below); the old road tiles and pieces are no longer looks (they stay in the catalogue).
    - **The family data (one place):** `game/tools/build_creator_assets.py` (`family_entries`, with `GROUPS`, `FAMILY_ORDER`, `SIMPLE_FAMILIES`, `HEIGHT_FAMILIES` and the road lists) writes `groups` and `families` into `data/level/creator_assets.json`: per family `id`, `group`, `label` (a text key), counts of `looks` and `turns`, `mirrors`, `heights` and `members`. A member is what is placed, `{asset, view, mirror, variant}` (a catalogue entry in one of its views and mirrors: exactly what an old button placed), and where it sits, `{look, turn, flip, height}`. New art adds members (looks) there, not buttons. **Stepping (one place):** `scripts/level_creator/creator_families.gd` (`CreatorFamilies`: `step`, `can_step`, `with_value`, `looks`, `member_for`). **Keys (one place):** `editor.gd` `KEY_ACTIONS` (V look, R turn, M flip, H height) through `step_pick`; the strip and the keys both end in `apply_member`. `asset_panel.gd`: `picked` (the member's asset), `picked_family`, `member`, `show_member`, `pick(family or asset id[, view, mirror])`.
    - **Saved levels are unchanged:** a member places through `LevelDocument.place` as before (`variant`: only for a skyscraper's second body). A test walks every old pose (each panel entry in each view and mirror its art has) and places it both ways into two levels, which come out the same. A level the board saved before (`tests/fixtures/t262_level_saved_before.json`, its `level_01.json`) opens, takes new pieces, keeps its own, gives the same `check_level.py` report and plays; a new level with every kind passes `check_level.py`.
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_families.gd`, the panel tests in `test_editor_place_delete.gd`, `test_creator_props.gd`, `test_road_pieces.gd`, `test_editor_rotate_heights.gd` updated, `tools/test_build_creator_assets.py` (`FamilyTest`). Screenshots (`bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_panel_demo.tscn art-archive/T-262/screenshots/panel.png 30`; also `level_creator_panel_skyscraper.tscn`, `_boulder.tscn`, `_end.tscn`): `art-archive/T-262/screenshots/`.
  - **The road brush (T-263):** (Level design, Content, "Level creator: the road brush"; the points marked there are proposals). The Roads group's families are brushes (`"brush"` in a family of `creator_assets.json`: `"paint"` for Road, Centre line and Cracked road, `"zebra"` for Zebra crossing; `build_creator_assets.py` `ROAD_BRUSHES`). A brush family's look is an **asphalt** (the member's `"asphalt"`; its asset, the asphalt's straight tile along y, is only the button's and the strip's picture): Road and Centre line `a0` (M: `a0m`), `g6`, `g9`; Cracked road `g4`, `g5`, `g7`. R does nothing (the brush turns the road itself).
    - **Controls:** with a paint brush picked, press the **left button** on the map and drag: each 2 x 2 tile **block** (4 x 4 units, today's two-tile-wide road; blocks lie on the even cells) the mouse passes over becomes road of the picked kind and asphalt, with the blocks in between filled in so a diagonal drag leaves no gap (`RoadBrush.blocks_between`: steps along x and y). A **right-drag** erases road along it, and **Del** erases the block under the mouse. With Zebra crossing picked, a **left click** on a straight road lays a crossing across it on the row of tiles clicked. A blue outline shows the block (or the crossing's tiles) under the mouse, red while erasing. The top bar shows `road_brush_hint` ("Drag to paint a road", `creator_texts.json`). Esc ends a stroke and drops the pick. Editor functions (also for tests and fixtures): `brush_mode`, `brush_asphalt`, `begin_road_stroke(units, erase)`, `continue_road_stroke`, `end_road_stroke`, `paint_road(points)`, `erase_road(points)`, `place_zebra(units)`.
    - **How it picks the tiles (`scripts/level_creator/road_brush.gd`, `RoadBrush`):** a block is a road when its four cells are road tiles; its **arms** are the sides with a road block next to it (`arms_of`), which give its piece (`piece_of`): `straight_y` (n and s), `straight_x` (e and w), a T-252 corner, T-junction or crossroads, a dead end (one arm), or `single` (none: plain asphalt). `tiles_for(kind, asphalt, arms, zebra)` gives the four tiles: Centre line: `y_line_r` / `y_line_l` across a road along y (`x_line_r` above `x_line_l` along x), T-252's `piece_<piece>_<column><row>` on `a0` and T-263's `brush_line_<piece>_<asphalt>_<column><row>` on `a0m`, `g6`, `g9` (another asphalt falls back to `a0`); Road and Cracked road: `y_plain` / `x_plain`, a junction all `y_plain`, a dead end `brush_plain_dead_<side>_<asphalt>_<column><row>` (the kerb, no line). A crossing is the full `y_zebra` / `x_zebra` tile on both tiles across the road, on one row (along y) or column (along x) of the block. `paint`, `erase` and `zebra_at` change the blocks and then write the tiles of every block they touched and the blocks next to them in one go (`LevelDocument.set_cells`). What a block is (`state_of`: `kind`, `asphalt`, `zebra`) is read back from its tiles (`tile_info`), so painting next to a road saved earlier joins it when that road's tiles fill whole blocks. Painting over a road changes its kind and asphalt and keeps its crossing; a crossing goes when its block is no longer a straight along it. `blocked`: a block overlapping a building, platform or ramp is never painted.
    - **The new tiles** (no PixelLab; `python game/tools/build_ground_tiles.py`, then `python game/tools/build_creator_assets.py`): `build_ground_tiles.py` `brush_tiles` appends 268 tiles to the ground atlas after T-252's (indexes 139 to 406): the 13 T-252 pieces on `a0m`, `g6` and `g9` (`BRUSH_LINE_ASPHALTS`, `brush_line_*`, the same straight tiles' line pixels on each open side, so the line joins without a gap) and the four dead ends without a line on all seven asphalts (`brush_plain_dead_*`, the same kerb and gravel as T-252's). They are `"piece": true` in `ground_atlas.json` (catalogue `"part": true`, never panel looks). Every earlier tile keeps its index, so the game's own ground is unchanged.
    - **Saved levels** are the same ground data as before (a catalogue road tile per cell, `ground_rows` R under it), so a painted level passes `check_level.py` and plays, and earlier levels open unchanged. `LevelDocument.place` still places any single road tile or piece, as before.
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_road_brush.gd` (a drag paints a full-width road along y and x for each kind; a diagonal drag has no gap; the mouse left-drag paints, right-drag and Del erase; a plus, an L and a T give a crossroads, dead ends, a corner and a T-junction for each kind with the right tiles; the street grid has every junction kind; the centre line joins across every piece; centre-line pieces on the other asphalts; erasing re-joins; painting over changes the kind; zebra crossings; never over a building or platform; a painted level saves, passes `check_level.py` and plays; the board's earlier level opens unchanged), `tools/test_build_ground_tiles.py` (`BrushTileTests`: the line pixels on each open side are the straight tiles' own, pixel for pixel; the kerb and no line on the plain dead ends; the sprite rules), `tools/test_build_creator_assets.py` (`test_road_brushes`), and the T-262 tests updated for the brush. **The street grid:** `tests/fixtures/level_creator_road_brush_grid.gd` (6 drags and 4 crossings through the editor). Screenshots: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_road_brush_demo.tscn art-archive/T-263/screenshots/street_grid.png 30` (also `level_creator_road_brush_close.tscn`, `street_grid_close.png`); the GIF: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_road_brush_paint.tscn art-archive/T-263/screenshots/paint_end.png 640` (frames in `user://t263_paint_frames`), then `python game/tools/frames_to_gif.py "<that folder>" art-archive/T-263/screenshots/paint.gif --fps 12 --skip 1 --scale 0.6 --crop 1.0 --shared --colors 64`. All in `art-archive/T-263/screenshots/`.
  - **Closed ramps, arrows up or down, platform sizes (T-264):** (Level design, Content, "Level creator: closed ramps, arrows and platform sizes"; the numbers are proposals).
    - **Closed ramps:** `elevation_platform_drawing.gd` `textured_faces()` draws a wall at every ramp's tall end (its `join_edge()`) from the top down to what the ramp stands on, in the ramp side's texture, in every direction and height step. The east and south ends face the view; the west and north ends are drawn first, behind the top. The outline follows the wall. The game's own map and pieces that name an art piece are unchanged.
    - **Arrows up or down:** a ramp of any size has an optional `arrows` in the map file (`"up"` the slope, the default and what a ramp without the key means, or `"down"`). `GroundHeight` reads it, `arrow_direction()` gives the flat direction the arrows point, and the top face's texture runs the other way for "down" (the sign of v in `textured_faces()`). **A** turns the arrows of the next ramp (top bar buttons "Arrows up" and "Arrows down" show with a ramp picked); with a ramp selected and no ramp picked, A turns that ramp's. `LevelDocument.place(..., arrows)` and `set_ramp_arrows` write it. The preview shows the choice.
    - **Platform sizes:** with a platform (height 3 or 6) picked, the top bar shows S, M, L and "Any size (drag)" (`size_buttons`, `pick_platform_size`). A click without a drag places `PLATFORM_SIZES` in `editor.gd`: S 8 x 8, M 16 x 16, L 32 x 32 units; the default is M (it was the elevation sample's 84 x 30). "Any size" is dragging the size out, as before, and a click then gives M. A drag always sizes freely. Snapping and the overlap refusal are unchanged.
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_ramps.gd`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-264/screenshots/` (fixtures `tests/fixtures/level_creator_ramps_demo.gd` and the `level_creator_ramps_*.tscn` and `level_creator_sizes.tscn` scenes).
  - **Skyscraper banners on or off (T-265):** (Level design, Content, "Level creator: skyscraper banners on or off"; the choices are proposals).
    - **Where the banner comes from:** it is the vertical neon sign painted into every skyscraper body (`build_skyscrapers.py` `place_sign`, T-064; `build_city.py`), baked into `art/buildings/skyscraper_N_K.png`, not a separate sprite and not `art/banners/` (those are the game map's kaiju alert signs, `kaiju_signs.gd`, which the creator's map never builds). `python game/tools/build_skyscrapers_nobanner.py` (`--check` rebuilds the originals from `art-archive/T-064/round2/raw` and compares them with the art in the game, pixel for pixel) writes the same 12 bodies with the sign left out to `art/buildings/nobanner/skyscraper_N_K.png`. No PixelLab; the originals are untouched. `BuildingTowers.nobanner_path(path)` maps one to the other.
    - **Data:** a skyscraper in `buildings` has `"banner": true` or `false` (written by `LevelDocument.place(..., banner)`, `LevelDocument.is_skyscraper`, `banner_of`, `set_building_banner`; other buildings never get the key). `BuildingTowers.single_of` passes it on and `setup` draws `art/buildings/nobanner/` when it is not true. **A skyscraper without the key (a level saved before T-265) opens with the banner off** (a proposal). The game's own map (whole blocks of four, no `asset`) has no such entries and keeps every banner and sign as before.
    - **Controls:** **B** (`editor.gd` `KEY_ACTIONS`, `banner_pick`) turns the banner of the next skyscraper placed on or off, **off by default** (`pick_banner`); with no skyscraper picked and a skyscraper selected, B changes that building and redraws it. The top bar's "Banner on" and "Banner off" (`banner_on`, `banner_off`; `banner_buttons`, `set_banner(on)`) show with a skyscraper picked or selected and do the same. The preview under the mouse shows the choice (`preview_of`).
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_banners.gd` (the 12 plain bodies differ from the signed ones only in the sign; off by default; B on and off; selected building; preview; saved, reopened and drawn in a run; earlier level without the key; the game's own map keeps its 4 banners, 2 signs and signed bodies), `tools/test_build_skyscrapers_nobanner.py`. Screenshot (`bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_banners_demo.tscn art-archive/T-265/screenshots/banners.png 30`): `art-archive/T-265/screenshots/`.
  - **Thread the needle (T-272):** (Level design, Content, "Level creator: thread the needle"; the choices and numbers are proposals). Pair two obstacles in the creator; in a run, driving between them is a trick.
    - **Obstacles and refs (one place, `scripts/level/obstacle_pairs.gd`, `ObstaclePairs`):** an obstacle is a boulder or a prop of the catalogue's `obstacles` group (`obstacle_props()`: `prop-barrier`, `prop-cones`, `prop-crates`). A level file's `pairs` is `[{id: "pair-N", a, b}]`, each ref a boulder's pattern copy id (`"boulder-3"`, a copy of one boulder: the creator's) or `"<copy id>/<index>"` (a copy of several, the game's own map), or a prop's id (`"prop-2"`). `resolve(layout, ref)` gives the obstacle and its collision (circle or rect, units), `line_of(layout, pair)` the line between the two centres (`a`, `b`) and its gap part from edge to edge (`gap_a`, `gap_b`), `crosses(from, to, a, b)` the crossing test. The run and the creator both use it; `check_level.py` (`check_pairs`) checks the same rules: two different existing obstacles per pair, no obstacle in two pairs, a copy of several boulders named with its index.
    - **Data (`LevelDocument`):** `is_obstacle(piece)`, `obstacle_ref(piece)`, `piece_of_ref(ref)`, `pairs()`, `pair_of(piece)`, `make_pair(a, b)` (a pair either was in goes first: the new pair wins), `unpair(piece)`. `delete` removes the pairs of a deleted obstacle and writes the other pairs' refs again (a copy's boulders shift when one goes). Saved with the level (`file_data`) and reopened.
    - **Controls:** **P** (`editor.gd` `KEY_ACTIONS`, `pair_pick`) or the top bar's **Pair obstacles** (`pair_tool`; `pair_button`, `set_pair_tool(on)`) turns the pair tool on (`pair_mode`; it drops the pick and the selection; the top bar shows `pair_hint`). A left click (`pair_click(units)`) holds the first obstacle (`pair_first`), the second makes the pair and shows `pair_made`; a click on anything else shows `pair_not_obstacle` and keeps the first; the same obstacle again lets it go. The tool stays on until P, Esc, a right-click or picking a piece. **Unpair** (`unpair_label`; `unpair_button`, `unpair_selected()`) shows with a paired obstacle selected. The bottom line is `controls_hint_pairs`. The overlay (`_Overlay._draw_pairs`) draws each pair as a dashed line across the gap and a ring round each obstacle in `PAIR_COLOR` (mint green), lifted to the height they stand on, and the held first obstacle ringed thicker.
    - **In a run (`scripts/level/thread_the_needle.gd`, `ThreadTheNeedle`):** `run.gd` makes one per map (`run.needle`, from `map.layout` and `ComboData.needle_rules()`), calls `observe(car centre, airborne, dt)` after every physics step and `touched(collider, shape_index)` on every `hit_obstacle` (a prop body's `prop_id`, a boulder body's `copy_id` and shape index name the obstacle). A pass is the car's centre crossing a pair's centre line (only its gap part can be reached); it fires `threaded(pair_id)` `clear_seconds` later unless either obstacle was touched from `touch_window_seconds` before the crossing until then; a pair pays again only `repeat_seconds` after it last paid; with `counts_in_air` false a crossing in the air does not count. The run then calls `RunState.add_needle_trick()`: the combo goes up by `trick_add.thread_the_needle` (held at the cap), the drain waits start again, and `combo_trick` is sent with the id `thread_the_needle` (`RunState.TRICK_THREAD_THE_NEEDLE`), so the popup shows **THREAD THE NEEDLE!** and "+{amount}" (`trick_popups.gd` `TEXTS`, normal size). A crash cuts the combo as before and pays nothing. **The marker (a proposal):** `build_markers(map, height_at, depth_for)` draws a faint dashed chalk line (`MARKER_COLOR`, alpha 0.35, 0.25 units wide) across each gap in `map.view_root` right over the ground tiles (`NeedleMarkers`), lifted and depth-sorted on raised ground as the drift marks are. The game's own map has no `pairs`: no gates, no marker.
    - **Numbers (`data/combo.json`, proposals with their reasons there):** `trick_add.thread_the_needle` 0.5; `thread_the_needle`: `repeat_seconds` 3.0, `counts_in_air` true, `touch_window_seconds` 0.5, `clear_seconds` 0.25 (`ComboData.needle_rules()`).
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_pairs.gd` (labels, key P, the button, pairing boulders and the three props, refusals, the same one twice, one pair per obstacle, Unpair, deleting either obstacle, a copy of several boulders, drawing, saved, reopened and checked, a missing obstacle refused, the game's map has none), `tests/unit/run/test_thread_the_needle.gd` (the rules on their own: one pay after `clear_seconds`, beside the pair nothing, the cool-down, a touch before or just after, another obstacle's touch, in the air, props and copies of several; in a run: the trick with its amount and popup, a crash into either boulder pays nothing and cuts the combo, the marker, the game's own map), `tools/test_check_level.py` (`TestCheckPairs`). Screenshot: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_pairs_demo.tscn art-archive/T-272/screenshots/pair_in_editor.png 30`; the GIF: `T272_POPUP_PNG="<abs path>/popup.png" bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_pairs_drive.tscn art-archive/T-272/screenshots/drive_end.png 230` (frames in `user://t272_drive_frames`), then `python game/tools/frames_to_gif.py "<that folder>" art-archive/T-272/screenshots/thread_the_needle.gif --fps 12 --skip 1 --scale 0.5 --crop 0.8 --shared --colors 64`. All in `art-archive/T-272/screenshots/`.
  - **Controls:** **pick**: click an asset in the side panel (click it again, or Esc, to drop the pick). **Place**: with an asset picked, left-click the map; the piece's top-left corner goes on the corner of the 2 x 2 unit tile clicked (a blue outline under the mouse shows where). **Select**: with nothing picked, left-click a piece (or right-click, which also drops the pick); a yellow outline marks it. **Delete**: the Delete button or the Del key removes the selected piece (a ground or road tile goes back to the plain ground). **Pan**: drag with the middle mouse button, or the arrow keys. **Zoom**: the mouse wheel, around the mouse. Where a placed piece really goes: "Snapping (T-237)" below.
  - **The spawn point (T-280):** (Level design, Decisions, "The car's spawn point"; labels T-279's; the numbers and looks are proposals). Every level has exactly one, where its run starts the car.
    - **Data (one place, `scripts/level/level_spawn.gd`, `LevelSpawn`):** the level file's `spawn` is `{x, y, heading}`: the middle of a 2 x 2 unit cell (units) and a heading in degrees, the car model's rotation (0 faces +x, 270 faces -y, the run's own start), one of eight (`HEADING_STEP` 45). No height is stored: the car stands on the ground there (`GroundHeight`: a rooftop's or ramp's height, else 0). `LevelSpawn.of(data)` gives it (a level without one: the map's centre cell and heading 270); `problem(data, units)` gives `""`, `OFF_MAP` or `BLOCKED`: the car can stand inside the map clear of every building, prop and boulder by `CLEARANCE` 1 unit (the cell beside one is fine); rooftops and ramps are fine. `check_level.py` (`check_spawn`) checks the same rules.
    - **In the editor:** `LevelDocument.ensure_spawn()` runs when a level opens (`_load_document`) and when it is saved (`file_data`), so a new level and a level saved before T-280 open with the point at the centre cell, and a saved level always has one. The overlay (`_Overlay._draw_spawn`) draws it on every frame above the pieces (z `OVERLAY_Z`): a green arrow on its cell turned to the heading (`SPAWN_COLOR`, a dark outline, never Redgull red), the label `spawn_label` above it, lifted to the surface it stands on. **Move:** press on it and drag (`begin_spawn_drag`, `update_spawn_drag` shows a ghost at the cell under the mouse, grey when refused, `end_spawn_drag` drops it): it snaps to that cell's middle (`LevelDocument.move_spawn`). A cell the car can't stand in (a building, a prop or boulder, off the map) is refused with `spawn_blocked` and the point stays; a piece placed over it is refused the same way (the placement is undone). A plain click on it selects it (`select_spawn`; the top bar shows `spawn_hint`). **Turn:** R with it selected steps the heading round the eight (`turn_spawn`). **Delete:** Del (or the Delete button) shows `spawn_no_delete` and keeps it. The bottom line is `controls_hint_spawn`. The old plan of "select it and click where it should go" is not built: a click selects, only a drag moves.
    - **In a run:** `run.gd` starts the car at the layout's `spawn` (place, heading; its height comes from `car.ground`) when the map has one; the game's own map has none and starts as before (`START_POSITION_UNITS`, `START_ROTATION`). `check_level.py` refuses a level without a valid `spawn` (except the files in `game/data/level/`, the game's own maps).
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_spawn.gd` (a new and an old level open with it at the centre, drag and mouse events, onto a rooftop and a ramp, the refusals, R through eight headings, Del, a piece over it, saved/reopened/checked, a run starts the car there with height and heading, the game's own map unchanged) and `TestSpawn` in `tools/test_check_level.py`. Screenshot fixtures: `tests/fixtures/level_creator_spawn_demo.tscn` (`T280_SHOT` = new, roof, refused, drag) and `level_creator_spawn_drive.tscn`.
  - **Rotate, mirror and heights (T-236):** **Rotate**: the Rotate button on the top bar, or the R key, steps the picked asset to the next view its catalogue entry lists (`views`; rooftops and ramps have `nw` and `se`, the rest one view), back to the first after the last. **Mirror**: the Mirror button, or the M key, steps it to its next mirror (`mirrors`; boulders and short buildings have one). The art exists only in the angles that were generated, so only those are offered; the button is greyed out when the asset has nothing to switch to. A new pick starts in its first view and mirror. The preview under the mouse shows the art of the current view and mirror (see-through, `PREVIEW_ALPHA`). **Heights**: pick a rooftop under Rooftops, Height 3 or Height 6, or a ramp under Ramps, Ramp 0 to 3 or Ramp 3 to 6; the top bar shows the picked piece's height label (all labels from `creator_texts.json`: `rotate_label`, `mirror_label`, `rooftop_height_3`, `rooftop_height_6`, `ramp_height_0_3`, `ramp_height_3_6`). A 6 placed wholly on a 3 stands on it (`on`, as in the elevation sample map); a ramp climbs the side its view's art faces (`nw`: a 0 to 3 up the south side, a 3 to 6 up the west; `se`: north and east) and is linked (`platform`) to the rooftop at its top height whose side its top end touches. The links are filled in again after every place and delete. A rooftop or ramp also writes its `view` and `art` (the art piece of `art/elevation_sample/` it is drawn with, moved from where it stands on the sample map; `elevation_layer.gd` `art_offset`). The data passes `python game/tools/check_height_data.py <level file>` when every ramp's top touches its rooftop. **Since T-251** the catalogue's rooftops and ramps are of any size and drawn in the engine ("Rooftops and ramps of any size (T-251)" below): a rooftop has one view (`iso`), a ramp's views are the four directions it climbs, and neither writes `view` or `art`; pieces saved before still carry their `art` and draw as here. The T-236 screenshot fixtures (`level_creator_heights_*.tscn`) are a record of the earlier pieces and no longer place their south-east set.
  - **What a piece writes:** ground and road tiles into `ground` (`tiles`: the catalogue ids used, `cells`: an index into `tiles` per 2 x 2 unit cell, rows of `cols`), with `ground_rows` and `legend` kept in step (G ground, R road, B building, one letter per unit); a building `{x, y, w, h, asset, look}` into `buildings` (the catalogue's 12 x 12 footprint, drawn as that one tower: `building_towers.gd` `single_of`; entries without `asset` stay whole blocks of four as on today's map); a boulder as its own pattern copy `{id: "boulder-N", boulders: [{x, y, diameter, variant}]}` (`mvp_map.gd` draws the named variant); a rooftop `{id: "roof-N", x, y, w, h, height}` into `platforms`; a ramp `{id: "ramp-N", platform: "", side, x, y, w, h, bottom_height, top_height}` into `ramps`. A file opened without `ground` (the game's own map) shows the game's ground tiles and is not changed until a piece is placed. The editor's map has no kaiju alert signs (`mvp_map.gd` `alert_signs_enabled`: they stand at the sample map's blocks). The road tile outside the ground atlas (`road-road_tile`) is drawn through the ground layout's `extra` list.
  - **Snapping (T-237):** `scripts/level_creator/snapping.gd` (`LevelSnapping.placement`) decides where a piece goes; the editor's preview and blue outline show that place before the click. **Tile grid:** the piece's top-left corner goes on the corner of the 2 x 2 unit cell under the cursor, so every edge of a building, rooftop and ramp lies on even units (`enemy_navigation.gd`'s `CELL` = 2, which `check_level.py` checks). **Snap distance (a proposal for the board): `SNAP_DISTANCE` = 4 units** (two cells). **Close neighbours:** a building, rooftop or ramp whose edge is within the snap distance of a placed building's, rooftop's or ramp's edge (and, for a building, a road tile's), with the two side by side (their gap the other way within the snap distance too), moves so that edge lies on the neighbour's: flush against it (a building against a road or another building) or lined up with it (a 6 along its 3's edges). Each axis moves to its closest such edge. **Ramps:** a ramp whose top end is within the snap distance of the side it climbs (from its view) of a rooftop at its top height goes onto that side, slid along it to lie within it, and is linked to that rooftop; this wins over the neighbour snap. A move that would take the piece out of the map is not made. **Overlaps:** a rooftop or ramp that would overlap in a way `check_height_data.py` (run by `check_level.py`) refuses (two rooftops overlapping without one standing on the other; a ramp over a rooftop other than the one holding its whole foot) is **not placed**, and the top bar shows `overlap_message` from `creator_texts.json` until the next piece is placed. Buildings, boulders and tiles are never refused (the level check doesn't check their overlaps). Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_snapping.gd`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-237/screenshots/`.
  - **Saving (T-238):** type the level's name in the **Level name** field on the top bar (a new level starts as "New level"; an opened level shows its file's `name`), then press **Save**. The level is written through `LevelStore.save_level` into the levels folder, `game/data/levels/` (`res://data/levels/`, `LevelStore.LEVELS_DIR`; the editor's `levels_dir`, which the creator passes on), as indented JSON in the elevation map's format (`LevelDocument.file_data`: `map`, `legend`, `ground_rows`, `buildings`, `pattern_copies`, `platforms`, `ramps`, plus `name` and the creator's `ground`), and the top bar shows `save_confirmation` from `creator_texts.json`. **Which file:** a level opened from the levels folder is saved over its own file; a new level, or a file opened from elsewhere (the game's own `elevation_sample_map.json`, which is never overwritten), gets a new file named after the level (`LevelDocument.file_stem`: lower case, spaces as `_`, e.g. `my_level.json`; `-2`, `-3`... when the name is taken), and later saves go to that file. An empty name saves under the default name. Back to list then lists the level by its name, and Edit opens it again. The saved file passes `python game/tools/check_level.py`, and the game's own map saved from the editor builds into the same buildings, boulders, rooftops and ramps. Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_editor_save.gd` (writes only into a temporary folder, `RunPaths`). Screenshot (`bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_save_demo.tscn art-archive/T-238/screenshots/saved.png 30`, saving into a temporary folder): `art-archive/T-238/screenshots/`.
  - **End to end (T-240):** `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_end_to_end.gd` goes from a creator with no levels (temporary folder) through New level, one asset of every kind, rotate, mirror, delete, name, Save, Back, the level in the list, `check_level.py` on the file, and Play (8:00 on the clock, minions spawning); it also checks that the main menu's Start still plays `data/level/elevation_sample_map.json`.
  - **Rooftops and ramps of any size (T-251):** (the numbers are proposals: Level design, Content, "Level creator: rooftops of any size and ramps in every direction"). **Sizing:** with a rooftop (Height 3, Height 6) or a ramp (Ramp 0 to 3, 3 to 6, 0 to 6) picked, press the left button on the map, drag, and release: the piece covers the cells dragged across (`editor.gd` `begin_size_drag`, `drag_area`, `end_size_drag`; `LevelDocument.piece_size`: whole 2 x 2 cells, at least `min_size_units` 4 x 4, at most the map). A press and release on one cell places the default size (the catalogue's `footprint_units`, the sample map's 84 x 30 and 30 x 30). A ramp's drag sets only its width along the rooftop's side (`ramp_width_axis`: x for a ramp climbing north or south; at least 4, default 10); its length is `length_units` (12 per 3 of rise: 12, 12, 24). While a resizable piece is picked the top bar shows `size_hint`, during the drag `size_readout` ({w} east-west, {d} north-south, units), for a ramp its direction (`ramp_dir_north` ... `ramp_dir_west`, `asset_panel.gd` `RAMP_DIR_KEYS`); the bottom line is `controls_hint_sizing` (all from `creator_texts.json`). The top bar wraps onto a second line when the labels don't fit. **Rotate** steps a ramp through north, east, south, west (its view: the direction it climbs; its `side` is the rooftop's side opposite: climbing north, it goes up the south side). Snapping and the overlap refusal work as before on the dragged rectangle; a piece that would reach out of the map is not placed. **Preview:** the piece is drawn as it will be (`editor.gd` `preview_block`, `preview_drawing`, see-through at `PREVIEW_ALPHA`) with its blue footprint.
    - **Drawn in the engine:** `elevation_layer.gd` gives every area without an art piece (no `art/elevation_sample/<art or id>.png`) the piece style, and `elevation_platform_drawing.gd` (textured mode: `use_style`, `textured_faces`, `outline_segments`) draws it: the top and the east and south walls as textured polygons in the art's projection (`screen x = (x - y) * 32`, `screen y = (x + y) * 16 - h * 32` art px, drawn at 0.5), mirror-tiled (`TEXTURE_REPEAT_MIRROR`), nearest filtering; walls from the height the piece stands on (`base`: the 3 under a 6, a ramp's bottom) up, with the shades and the 4 px row offset of the sample art; a ramp has no wall and no outline at its top end; a dark 0.5 drawn px (one art px) outline on the top's edges, the visible vertical corners and the walls' feet; a parapet on a rooftop's north and west edges. `elevation_layer.gd` `edge_gaps` breaks the parapet and the outline where a ramp comes within 0.5 units of an edge or a higher rooftop stands across it. Depth order, see-through and the ground height are unchanged (they read the same rectangles). The game's own map, and creator pieces with `art`, keep their art.
    - **The textures are settings in one place:** `art/creator_pieces/piece_style.tres` (script `scripts/level/piece_style.gd`): `roof_top`, `wall`, `ramp_top`, `ramp_side`, `parapet` and the drawing's numbers (parapet height, shades, outline colour and width). T-255 swapped in new textures by changing that file (and added two more style files). The placeholder textures and the panel icons are T-198's PixelLab textures with the sample art's colour fix, made by `python game/tools/build_creator_piece_art.py build` (`check` runs the sprite rules: at most 30 colours, alpha 0 or 255, no Redgull red) into `art/creator_pieces/`. The catalogue entries come from `build_creator_assets.py` (`elevation_entries`).
    - **Enemies** path up and down the new ramps unchanged (`enemy_navigation.gd` reads the same height data). Saved levels pass `check_level.py`.
    - **Texture styles (T-255):** a rooftop or ramp of any size carries a `style` (`office`, `carpark` or `t198`; none means `office`), set by the editor's **Style** button (key T) for the pieces placed next and drawn through `elevation_layer.gd` `piece_style_named` (`PIECE_STYLES`: `art/creator_pieces/piece_style.tres` = office, `piece_style_carpark.tres`, `piece_style_t198.tres` = T-198's). The textures are PixelLab's, made into tiles by `python game/tools/build_creator_textures_t255.py build` (`check`: the sprite rules) into `art/creator_pieces/t255/`, from `art-archive/T-255/` (7 generations; "Creator textures, what T-255 made" in Visual style). `ramp_plain_repeat` in a style tiles a ramp's textures plainly instead of mirrored, so its arrows keep pointing the same way. Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_piece_styles.gd`, `tools/test_build_creator_textures_t255.py`. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-255/screenshots/` (`office`, `carpark`, `t198`, each with `_close`; fixtures `tests/fixtures/level_creator_pieces_<style>[_close].tscn`).
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_pieces_any_size.gd` (sizes, every direction, dragging, the labels, the drawing and its one style, the game's map keeping its art, no red, enemies' paths, `check_level.py`), the T-236 and T-237 tests updated, `tests/unit/test_creator_assets.gd`, `tools/test_build_creator_assets.py`, `tools/test_build_creator_piece_art.py`. **The test level:** `tests/fixtures/level_creator_pieces_level.gd` (built through the editor). Screenshots: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_pieces_demo.tscn art-archive/T-251/screenshots/creator.png 30` (also `_close`, `_drag`, `_ramp`); the drive GIF: `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_pieces_drive.tscn art-archive/T-251/screenshots/drive_end.png 1700` (the level saved to `user://`, played in `run.tscn` with its own ground; the car scripted along five legs, frames saved to `user://t251_drive_frames`), then `python game/tools/frames_to_gif.py "<that folder>" art-archive/T-251/screenshots/drive.gif --fps 12 --skip 3 --scale 0.45 --crop 0.8 --shared --colors 64`. All in `art-archive/T-251/screenshots/`.
  - **Props (T-253):** (the numbers are proposals: Level design, Content, "Level creator: props"). A **Props** category (`prop_barrier`, `prop_cones`, `prop_crates`, `prop_ac_unit`, `prop_water_tank`, `prop_roof_vent`, `prop_street_lamp`, `prop_roof_sign` and `category_props` from `creator_texts.json`) holds eight props, catalogue kind `prop`, ids `prop-barrier` ... `prop-roof-sign`. **Rotate** (R) steps the picked prop through `north`, `east`, `south`, `west` (its views); a click places it with its top-left corner on the clicked 2 x 2 tile (the grid snap only; no overlap refusal; not placed where its footprint would leave the map), on the street or on a rooftop. It writes `{id: "prop-N", asset, rotation, x, y, w, h}` into the level's `props` list (`LevelDocument.place`; x, y, w, h = the footprint turned to the rotation). The preview shows the picked rotation's sprite lifted to the height under it (`editor.gd` `_prop_preview`).
    - **One data file:** `data/level/props.json`: per prop `footprint_units` (facing north; east and west swap them), `height_units`, `collision` (`rect` = the footprint, or `circle` with a `radius`, in the middle of it), `label` (a `creator_texts.json` key), `look` (the parts the placeholder script draws) and `textures` (per rotation the PNG and `anchor_px`, the pixel standing on the footprint's centre on the ground; the art is drawn at `draw_scale` 0.5, 32 art px per unit). The catalogue's `prop` entries (`build_creator_assets.py`, `prop_entries`) are built from it. **T-256 swaps the art** by changing the PNGs in `art/props/` and the `textures` entries (the look and the footprint stay).
    - **Placeholder art:** `python game/tools/build_prop_art.py build` draws each prop's boxes and cylinders in code (no PixelLab; a top and two visible faces, a dark outline, a pale arrow on the top showing the facing, at most 30 colours, alpha 0 or 255) into `art/props/<prop>_<rotation>.png` and writes the `textures` key of `props.json`; `check` runs the rules (also no Redgull red).
    - **In the game (`scripts/level/level_props.gd`, `LevelProps`):** `mvp_map.gd` calls `LevelProps.build(map)` after its boulders: a `StaticBody2D` on collision layer 1 per prop (the car's `hit_obstacle` fires, which cuts the combo as for a boulder; minions and the kaiju are stopped), and a sprite in `map.prop_sprites` under `map.prop_sprites_root` (screen pixels, hidden in the flat view), lifted by the ground height under its centre. The sprites take part in the depth order like the boulders' (`map.depth_sprites()`: the car's, the enemies' and the elevation layer's covering tests and `restack`). `enemy_navigation.gd` closes the cells a prop takes (a rectangle like a building, a circle like a boulder), and `is_open_spot` and both enemies' `is_free_spot` refuse a spot inside one (`map.prop_blocks_spot`). The physics is flat: a car in the air above a rooftop prop crashes into it. The game's own map has no props.
    - **The level check:** `check_level.py` (`check_props`) checks each prop names a known prop and rotation, has that prop's footprint turned to that rotation, and lies inside the map.
    - **Final art (T-256):** the props use PixelLab art, `art/props/<name>_<rotation>_art.png` (8 generations, raws in `art-archive/T-256/raw/`), built by `python game/tools/build_prop_final_art.py build` (crop, at most 30 colours, red to orange, mirror for east and west; sets `props.json` textures and anchors) and checked by `... check`; test `tools/test_build_prop_final_art.py`. The placeholders (`<name>_<rotation>.png`, `build_prop_art.py`) stay on disk, and `build_prop_art.py build` redraws them but leaves the textures on the final art. To go back to them, set the textures to the placeholder names. Screenshots: `art-archive/T-256/screenshots/`.
    - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_creator_props.gd` (all eight in four rotations on the ground and a rooftop, saved, reopened and checked; the car crashes into a rectangular and a round prop and the combo is cut; enemies path round one; the data file, labels, no red), `tools/test_build_prop_art.py`, `tools/test_check_level.py` (`TestCheckProps`), `tools/test_build_creator_assets.py`. **Fixtures and screenshots:** `tests/fixtures/level_creator_props_level.gd` (every prop in four rotations, built through the editor), `level_creator_props_demo.tscn` (street), `_roof`, `_close`, `_panel`, and `level_creator_props_drive.tscn` (the car driven into a barrier, a crate stack and a lamp): `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_props_demo.tscn art-archive/T-253/screenshots/creator_street.png 40`; the GIF as for T-251 (`... level_creator_props_drive.tscn art-archive/T-253/screenshots/drive_end.png 700`, frames in `user://t253_drive_frames`, then `python game/tools/frames_to_gif.py "<that folder>" art-archive/T-253/screenshots/drive.gif --fps 12 --skip 2 --scale 0.5 --crop 0.8 --shared --colors 64`). All in `art-archive/T-253/screenshots/`.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/level_creator/test_editor_place_delete.gd`, `test_editor_rotate_heights.gd` (T-236). Screenshots (`tests/fixtures/level_creator_editor_demo.tscn` and `level_creator_editor_panel_end.tscn`, e.g. `bash game/tools/screenshot.sh res://tests/fixtures/level_creator_editor_demo.tscn art-archive/T-235/screenshots/editor.png 30`): `art-archive/T-235/screenshots/`; rooftops at 3 and 6 with their ramps in both views (`tests/fixtures/level_creator_heights_demo.tscn`, `_nw.tscn`, `_se.tscn`): `art-archive/T-236/screenshots/`.
  - **Road pieces (T-252):** corners, T-junctions, a crossroads and dead ends at today's two-tile-wide roads' size: each piece is 2 x 2 tiles (4 x 4 units), so each open side is a road's full width and joins the existing straight tiles (`y_line_r` / `y_line_l` along y, `x_line_r` / `x_line_l` along x, and the plain ones). `build_ground_tiles.py` appends 52 tiles to the ground atlas (indexes 87 to 138, named `piece_<piece>_<column><row>`, marked `"piece": true` in `ground_atlas.json`), so every older tile keeps its index and the game's own ground (`mvp_ground.json` cells) is unchanged (a test hashes it). Each is the plain asphalt `y_plain_a0` plus the straight tiles' own centre-line pixels on the tile edges the open sides use (`ARM_SEGMENTS`: the line between the two tiles across the road, `edge_lines` takes it from the approved line's mask), so on every open side the two tiles next to the road carry exactly `line_r` and `line_l`'s line pixels (same dash phase) and the line runs on to the piece's centre, where lines meet; a test checks this pixel for pixel. No new colours, no PixelLab. The straights have no edge lines, so none are drawn; a closed side is just the asphalt's edge against the gravel. **Dead ends** end in a kerb: a white end line 0.25 units wide across the full road, 0.75 to 1 unit from the closed side, with gravel (gravel0's pixels) beyond it. The 13 pieces (`corner_ne/es/sw/nw`, `t_nes/esw/swn/wne`, named by their open sides, `cross`, `dead_n/e/s/w` named by the open side) are also drawn as 256 x 128 diamonds on `art/ground/road_pieces.png` (the panel's icons and the placement preview; `ground_atlas.json` `pieces`). Rebuild: `python game/tools/build_ground_tiles.py`, then `python game/tools/build_creator_assets.py`. **Catalogue:** one entry per piece and rotation (`road-piece_<name>`, `footprint_units` [4, 4], `parts`: the four tile entries with their offsets in units), listed first under Roads; the tiles are catalogue entries too (`road-piece_<name>_<column><row>`, `"part": true`) but only the pieces are panel buttons (`LevelDocument.panel_entries`). Picking a piece and clicking places all four tiles at once (`LevelDocument.place`; `LevelSnapping.placement` moves the piece in so all four are on the map, the blue outline is 4 x 4); Delete removes one tile at a time, as for any ground tile. The side panel puts the label of `road_corner`, `road_t_junction`, `road_crossroads` or `road_dead_end` (`creator_texts.json`) over each button (`asset_panel.gd` `LABEL_KEYS`). Rotate does not apply (each rotation is its own button, like the older road tiles' x and y versions, which were already all in the catalogue). An earlier one-tile-wide version of the pieces (which did not join the straights) was dropped. Tests: `tools/test_build_ground_tiles.py` (`RoadPieceTests`), `tools/test_build_creator_assets.py`, `tests/unit/level_creator/test_road_pieces.gd`. Screenshot (`tests/fixtures/level_creator_roads_demo.tscn`: a street grid of today's straights and every piece, and two short dead-end roads): `art-archive/T-252/screenshots/street_grid.png`.

## Meteor effects: the comet tail and the impact (task T-070)

The kaiju's meteors (scripts/enemies/kaiju_meteors.gd) now stream a **comet tail** behind them while they fall (the last `FALL_SECONDS`, 0.5 s, of the warning) and play an **impact animation** where they land (it replaces the plain dust burst; the boulder still appears at landing, as in T-046/T-147, under the first flash frames). The meteor, the warning circle and the boulder are unchanged. Drawing is in `scripts/enemies/meteor_fx.gd` (`MeteorFx`), called by the attack's sky drawing; tail and impact are pure functions of flight time and impact age.

**The setting:** `data/meteor_fx.json`, key `version`, one of:
- `pixel`: sprite sheets drawn by script, `art/effects/meteor_tail_pixel.png` (6 frames, loops at 14 fps) and `meteor_impact_pixel.png` (8 frames over 0.5 s). No PixelLab. `python game/tools/build_meteor_fx.py build` redraws them (byte-identical).
- `pixellab`: sheets animated by PixelLab (`/animate-with-text-v3`, 2 calls) from first frames the script drew, `meteor_tail_pixellab.png` (8 frames) and `meteor_impact_pixellab.png` (8 frames). Built from the raw frames in `art-archive/T-070/pixellab/` by `build_meteor_fx.py from-pixellab` (background and half-transparent pixels cut, at most 30 colours).
- `particles` (default, the board's pick, 2026-10-21): no sprites; square pixel embers, ring, sparks, shards and dust computed each frame in `meteor_fx.gd` (impact 0.6 s).
- `off`: the earlier placeholder (no tail, the plain dust burst).

The environment variable `METEOR_FX` overrides the file (used for recordings). Sheet cells are 128 x 128 art pixels drawn at 0.75; palette is the boulders' violet and magenta, white-hot core, grey-violet dust, never the heart's red. `python game/tools/build_meteor_fx.py check` runs the sprite rules.

**Recording:** `bash game/tools/record_meteor_fx.sh <version> <out.gif> [seconds]` plays `tests/fixtures/meteor_fx_demo.tscn` (a run with meteors falling at once and every 1.5 s, three at a time, the car standing still; needs a window) and writes a GIF with `tools/frames_to_gif.py`. Recordings: `art-archive/T-070/screenshots/meteor_fx_<version>.gif`.

**Tests:** `tests/unit/enemies/test_meteor_fx.gd` (tail only while falling, the impact once and gone, each version draws, the setting, sheet sizes and no red) and `tools/test_build_meteor_fx.py`.

## Airtime counter (HUD)

Task T-243 (HUD and menus, Decisions, "Airtime counter"). `scripts/hud/airtime_counter.gd` (`AirtimeCounter`, a child "AirtimeCounter" of the `Hud`): a wing icon (placeholder drawn in code, white with a black outline, never the heart's red) and the airtime as a two-decimal number in Bangers (`0.00`, seconds, no unit). `Hud.bind_car(car)` (called by `run.gd` for each new car) follows the car's `stepped` signal and feeds `AirtimeCounter.step(car.airborne, car.model.dt)`: it shows from the first airborne step, adds one physics step each step in the air (the same airtime the airtime trick pays out for) and hides and resets on landing. The HUD places it at `Hud.AIRTIME_OFFSET` (70, -10) from the screen's centre: right of the car and a little below where the combo popups pop out. Size, place and icon are proposals for the playtest.

- **Tests:** `tests/unit/hud/test_hud_airtime_counter.gd`.
- **Screenshot and GIF:** `tests/fixtures/airtime_counter_gif.tscn` (a drive off a rooftop; `AIRTIME_PNG` saves a mid-jump screenshot), recorded with `--write-movie` and `tools/frames_to_gif.py`; results in `art-archive/T-243/screenshots/`.

## Drift counter (HUD)

Task T-247 (HUD and menus, Decisions, "Drift counter"). `scripts/hud/drift_counter.gd` (`DriftCounter`, a child "DriftCounter" of the `Hud`) extends `AirtimeCounter` and reuses its look and code (white Bangers number with a black outline, two decimals, seconds, no unit); only the icon differs: two curving skid marks drawn in code (`_make_icon()`, white with a black outline, never the heart's red). `Hud._on_car_stepped` feeds `step(car.model.is_drifting, car.model.dt)` once per physics step: it shows from the first drifting step, adds one step per step drifting and hides and resets when the drift ends (the moment it pays out, T-246). `Hud.bind_car` resets it with the airtime counter. Place: `Hud.DRIFT_OFFSET` (70, 30) from the screen's centre, directly under the airtime counter at the same x, so both fit at once. Place and icon are proposals for the playtest. Note: there is no drifting in the air (T-219), so in play the drift counter ends as the car leaves an edge and the airtime counter starts; the two can overlap only on the step boundary. The screenshot with both is staged.

- **Tests:** `tests/unit/hud/test_hud_drift_counter.gd`.
- **Screenshot and GIF:** `tests/fixtures/drift_counter_gif.tscn` (`DRIFT_PNG` and `DRIFT_BOTH_PNG` save screenshots), recorded with `--write-movie` and `tools/frames_to_gif.py`; results in `art-archive/T-247/screenshots/`.
